# Device details

Every chip, radio and sensor fact a Canary can honestly report should be visible in every app. It should be calm by default and deep on request, and each fact should carry who can see it and where it came from.

**Status:** design, partly built. Of the plan's 22 PRs (§12), four are on main: PR 1 (#1970), PR 2 (#1891), PR 6 (#1971) and PR 7 (#1892). #1961 built one slice of PR 3 (§0). The details record, most of the vocabulary (§2.2), the writer, the routes and every Details screen are plan. Nothing here was checked on a device: each merged PR says it was host-tested only, and every number below comes from the source tree, a host or CI build of it, the published release assets, the installed SDKs, or the `esp-idf` and espefuse sources listed below.

The design was written against securaCV `origin/main` at `351d9f9a`, and its branch then followed main through `377bbc6f`, `e4d07edb` and `3453dd26` ([Review history](#review-history) says what each revision changed and how it was reviewed). It lands from a branch cut at `0a06c14b`, and every section was read against that tree: what the plan proposed and main has since built is written as built, with its PR number and what the code does. **It was then read against main at `62bc0bc7` on 2026-11-16:** every section again, beside the diff from `0a06c14b`, and §1 measured again on that tree. A host-build figure measured at `0a06c14b` is kept, and dated, only where none of the sources it builds changed between the two commits. `62bc0bc7` carries the 2.4.20 train (#1972), with #1969 and #1983. The train moved the WAP's firmware install and update settings (`POST /api/ota/install` and `POST /api/ota/config`, `handle_ota_install` and `handle_ota_config` in `canary_wap.ino`) to the bearer alone, grew `/admin` by 144 B, and stamped the firmware 2.4.20; fw-v2.4.20 and Flasher 0.12.6 were published from `62bc0bc7` that day. Main has since taken #1985, which changes no file under `firmware/` (§13).

Main built these between `351d9f9a` and `0a06c14b`:

- **Owner authority, #1887.** On the WAP it moved these to the bearer alone: setting the vault key, the Bluetooth and mesh pairing confirms, Wi-Fi Connect and AP-only after setup, Forget and Disconnect in every mode, the two reboot routes, and a Passkey Entry's digits on `GET /api/bluetooth`. The administrator page (`/admin` and `/settings`, `web_ui.h`) already signed in with the token, pasted or fetched with a BOOT tap and held in the page's memory only; since #1887 that sign-in checks a pasted token by itself, without the session cookie. The headline page at `/` has no sign-in. #1887 added no owner session, and it left every read this design records for a session where it was. So F346 is decided (§3.7, §7.2 list 6, P8), and P20's move is PR 8's (§3.2, §11, §14).
- **PR 1, #1970.** Both flashers show About this board from the chip and the installed image, and the Lab's native bench shows it from the chip alone (§1.3). All three keep the hardware address off screen until asked (the bench never draws it) and out of saved reports and file names.
- **PR 2, #1891.** Home Assistant reads each Canary's firmware from its health publish and names its product on the device page (§1.3), and its diagnostics dump redacts the identifier keys (§6.12).
- **PR 7, #1892.** Vision's `module_id` is 1 or 0, never the module's own id (§1.4), and both Sentinel health publishes carry the radar link (§7.2 list 4).
- **PR 6, #1971.** The names, board tokens, versions and battery values of §1.4 come from the build and the board, but for three that §1.4 lists as still wrong. The first firmware release that carries it is fw-v2.4.20, published on 2026-11-16 from `62bc0bc7`; fw-v2.4.19 (2026-10-30, `67b36a6a`) does not (GitHub's release list, read on 2026-11-16).
- **One slice of PR 3, #1961.** `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json`, `scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py` and the generated `custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py` (§0).
- **The ledger, #1951.** `docs/audit/repo_todo_sweep_2026-09.md` holds open items only: a finished item is deleted in the PR that fixes it.

One measured fact stops part of the plan as it was written:

- **The `/admin` page's gzip is 67,638 B, 2,102 B over the 65,536 B ceiling this design decided (§14).** It passed the ceiling in #1919 (65,809 B); #1920 and #1951 grew it to 67,528 B, #1971 took 34 B back, and the 2.4.20 train (#1972) added 144 B (the update panel's note that an install or a settings change takes the API token, and the sign-in those two calls raise). No gate holds the ceiling (§1.2), and the `--check` budget the plan gave PR 6 was not built (ledger F479). Every plan step that adds to `/admin` (§5.3) needs room first. Whether a PR brings the page under the ceiling before PR 12's gate lands, or the ceiling moves, is an open question for the maintainer (§14).

How to read the rest:

- Every path, route, function and budget written plainly exists at `62bc0bc7`.
- Anything marked **(new)** does not exist yet.
- No line number is cited. A file path with a function name or a short quoted phrase is the pointer, because line numbers drift.
- Every number in §1 was measured at `62bc0bc7` on 2026-11-16, not copied, but for the self-manifest size, built at `0a06c14b`: the 2.4.20 train changed neither `self_manifest.h` nor either `emit_self_manifest()`. The sources:
  - `firmware/projects/canary-wap/tests_host/check_route_budget.py` and `firmware/projects/canary-wap/tests_host/check_route_security.py` were run on the tree. `firmware/canary/scripts/check_route_security.py` refuses to start without PlatformIO 6.1.19, and this machine has 6.2.0, so its counts were taken by calling its own `tree_sources()`, `parse_routes()`, `check_route_gates()` and `check_route_budgets()`. The flagship's per-variant worst case was counted with that checker's own `parse_routes_at()` and `definition_span()` over `registerHttpHandlers`.
  - The gzip sizes are the `B raw ->` comments in `web_assets_gz.h`, which `gen_web_assets_gz.py --check` reports in sync with its sources. The sizes at earlier PRs are the same comments in `git show <commit>:` of that file.
  - The self-manifest size is from a host build (g++) of `firmware/common/attest/self_manifest.h` with the release-shaped worst-case facts §1.2 lists.
  - The firmware sizes are the `.bin` bytes CI's size guard printed in the Firmware Build run on the 2.4.20 train's push to main (run 43798305143, commit `8daabedc`, 2026-11-16 UTC); `8daabedc` and `62bc0bc7` differ in no file under `firmware/`. CI builds with `-DVALIDATION_BUILD=1`; a release build of this tree was not measured. The slot ceilings are from `firmware/flavors.json` `size_guards`.
  - The MQTT buffers and the console keys were read from the sources §1.2 names.
  - The SDK facts are from the installed Arduino-ESP32 cores, 3.3.8 (IDF 5.5.4) and 2.0.17 (IDF 4.4.7), and from files read at espressif/esp-idf's release tags on 2026-11-16: `components/esp_system/include/esp_system.h` at v5.0 to v5.4, for when each reset reason appeared (§2.3); `components/esp_hw_support/mac_addr.c` at v4.4.7 and v5.5.4, for the address each radio interface takes (§5.6); and the two sources §5.1 and §5.2 name.
  - The eFuse layouts are from espefuse 5.6.0's `efuse_defs/esp32s3.yaml`, `esp32c3.yaml` and `esp32c6.yaml`, parsed field by field.

## 0. The short version

This section is the plan. On main today (the header names the PRs), both flashers show About this board, Home Assistant shows each Canary's firmware and product, most of §1.4's wrong values are fixed at their source, and one vocabulary file exists. No firmware writes the record below, no route, topic or Bluetooth attribute carries it, and no app draws a Details screen from it.

Each Canary describes itself in **one record**, `securacv.canary.details/v1` **(new)**. The record is cut into small **parts**: `about`, `now`, `chip`, `memory`, `image`, `security`, `radio`, a paged `sensors` index, and one `sensor` page per sensor, radio or module.

- **Size.** Every part is at most 768 bytes, and `about` is at most 512 bytes. The bound is generated per part and per kind of sensor, never one figure for every page (§4.2). The carriers are:
  - the USB console, a LAN route and one retained MQTT topic, each of which can hold any part;
  - one bonded Bluetooth attribute, which holds only `about` (one ATT attribute is at most 512 bytes).
- **Wording.** Every fact, label, explanation, privacy class and part sheet is spelled once, in `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json`, a file of its own beside `spec/witness_dictionary.json`. #1961 built the file with its first rows: 11 keys a Canary's answers use, in two classes (`identifier` and `health_counter`), each row naming the firmware sources that emit it. PR 3 grows that file, and this document calls its contents "the block" (§2.1). How its key rows and two classes meet this design's fact rows and ten classes is not decided (§2.2). Ledger HA62 and HA63 are open against the file; HA63 waits on the class question, and F430, the identifier lint, on the `never` rows PR 3 adds (§2.2).
- **Generation.** One generator, `scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py` (#1961; `--check` in `lint.yml`), writes every copy from the block: the firmware key table, the Swift, the JSON the flashers, the Lab and the website read, and the Python Home Assistant projects through. One copy is generated today, `custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py`, whose `REDACTED_DEVICE_KEYS` `diagnostics.py` unions into `TO_REDACT`. Each other copy joins the script in the PR that first reads it (§2.3).
- **Writer.** One pure firmware writer emits only keys the generated table names. It drops every fact the asker has not earned.

Five rules shape everything below:

1. **Details describe the device, never the room.** A details record carries identity of parts, configuration, build, posture and link health. It never carries a reading: no lux, no microphone level, no distance, no breathing or heart rate, no motion score, no coordinates. Readings already have their own surfaces with their own privacy contracts:
   - the radar chokepoint (the "Privacy chokepoint" comment that opens `firmware/projects/canary-sense/src/main.cpp`);
   - the audio barrier;
   - GNSS coarsening.

   A details screen must not become a way around them. Some facts describe the device yet still move with what happens in the room: link counters, the age of the last frame, per-channel votes and qualities, inference timings, and the Wi-Fi signal, even as a word. They are the `activity` class (§3.1), and no LAN tier of the record carries them. At `lan` a part shows only a latched status word that the scene cannot move.
2. **The tier comes from what the asker has proven, never from which route it called.** Every surface declares a ceiling, and the writer enforces it per fact.
3. **Nothing new on an open surface, nothing new outbound, no time of day.**
   - The open contracts gain no new non-build field: `/api/fleet`, mDNS TXT, BLE adverts, unauthenticated GATT, Improv and `node_status`. There is one contract-first addition: an optional `fw` on `/api/fleet` (§6.8), which is build class and already public in mDNS TXT. Fields may be removed or coarsened (§11).
   - Every new path is a read the owner's client starts, or a retained publish on the broker the owner already chose.
   - Uptime is whole hours, counted from a per-boot random offset so the hour steps do not date the boot (§4.1). A build date is a day.
4. **One vocabulary, and the firmware can only spell what it names.** Facts that must never leave a device have no key at all.
5. **A missing value means "cannot say", and "Verified" means a checked signature.** Nothing absent is ever drawn as zero or "fine". The record is "Reported" until a signature checked against a pinned key, over a fresh nonce, earns "Verified" on that app's own screen (§8). Shared text and retained MQTT parts never say "Verified".

```
spec/device_details_vocabulary.json ── "the block" (built in #1961, grown from PR 3) ── the only place a fact is spelled
        │
        ▼  scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py (built in #1961, grown from PR 3; --check in lint.yml)
        ├── custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py     (built in #1961, generated) → Home Assistant (HACS by sync)
        ├── firmware/common/details/device_details_keys.h       (new, generated)  → every firmware tree (keys, caps, held constants; no labels)
        ├── firmware/common/details/device_details_labels.h     (new, generated)  → the display glass only (ASCII labels it shows)
        ├── marked label regions in the on-device pages          (new, generated)  → WAP /admin and companion, flagship UI, display mirror
        ├── canary-local/devices/device_details.json            (new, generated)  → both flashers, Lab, website (carried)
        ├── ios/Shared/DeviceDetailsVocab.swift                 (new, generated)  → iPhone, iPad, tvOS (not rendered by any widget)
        ├── custom_components/securacv/tests/fixtures/details_*.json (new, generated) → HA tests, inside the carried tree
        ├── docs/device_details_reference.md                    (new, generated)  → people: every fact × class × tier × family
        └── spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json                (new, generated)  → the golden bytes every language decodes (its reference writer)
firmware/common/details/device_details.h (new)  pure writer: enum keys only, tier filter, refuse-whole
firmware/common/details/details_esp.h   (new)  header-only reader of the chip, both core axes
firmware/common/time/coarse_uptime.h    (new)  whole hours from one per-boot random offset shared by every surface
firmware/scripts/shipped_configs.py     (new)  per family, the build configurations a release actually ships
canary-local/emulator/src/emu_details.cpp (new)  the emulator's facts provider, every value labeled emulated
scripts/lint_device_details.py          (new)  holds code, apps and copy to the vocabulary
scripts/lint_identifier_reads.py        (new)  holds firmware to the "never read" list
```

Every path the diagram marks new is absent at `62bc0bc7`. The three it marks built are #1961's, and each holds only its first slice today: the file its eleven key rows, the generator its one output, the module the redacted keys (§2.2, §2.3).

## 1. What is true today

### 1.1 Where device facts live now

| Family | What it reads about itself | Where those facts leave | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flagship `firmware/canary` | Heap and PSRAM, reset and wake cause, die temperature, Wi-Fi RSSI/IP/SoftAP auth, running partition and OTA state, first 8 bytes of the ELF SHA-256, flash encryption, secure boot and the key-at-rest label, camera PID and 26 status fields (`CameraManager::getSensorParams`), sensor counters | Its bearer-gated `GET` routes (`/api/status`, `/api/diagnostics`, `/api/thermal`, `/api/wifi/status`, `/api/ota/status` and `/api/peek/sensor` among them), USB console, MQTT, and the BLE GATT status service on `[env:full]` | `auth_gate` (Host check, then bearer; `securacv_network.cpp`) |
| WAP | `sys_monitor::init()` reads model, revision, cores, MHz, flash, heap and PSRAM once. `GET /api/system` also sends the reset cause word, heap `min_free` and `largest_block`, PSRAM `min_free` and uptime to the second (`sys_monitor::get_json()`; `/admin` shows the word as its Reset row, `sysReset` in `web_ui.h`). `GET /api/status` sends `boot_count`, `witness_count`, `chain_seq`, `min_heap`, `last_reset`, `last_reset_crash`, `peek_active` and `uptime_sec` (`handle_status` in `canary_wap.ino`), and `GET /api/diagnostics` the heap low-water and largest block, SD lifetime writes and the egress counters (`handle_diagnostics`; the body is `wap_diagnostics::build_json`). | Those three answers, `GET /api/system`'s from one `snprintf` into a 2,048 B stack buffer | `api_auth_check` (a session or a bearer), so any LAN host that mints a session |
| WAP, public | `/api/device-info` serves identity plus `hw_token`, `uptime_ms`, `chain_length`, `tls_enabled` and the SoftAP's `ap_auth` (`identity_json::write_device_info`). `/api/wifi`, public and CORS-open, sends among other fields the home network's name and the WAP's address on it (`sta_ssid`, `sta_ip`), the seconds since the join (`connected_sec`) and the Wi-Fi signal (`rssi`, in dBm; activity class, §3.1) with `ap_auth`, `ap_auth_reason` and `sta_pmf` (`handle_wifi_status` in `canary_wap.ino`). `/api/wifi/scan`, also public, sends the `ssid` and `rssi` of each network a sweep heard (`scan_cache_json`). `/api/selftest`, also public, allowlisted as a wizard route on the AP and answered on every interface (`handle_selftest_wrap()`), sends among other fields the microphone's live level on the S3 builds (`last_rms`, a reading, §3.1), `uptime_ms`, `safe_mode` and, on a build with Bluetooth when a crash is recorded, `last_crash_stage` and `last_crash_reason` (in its Bluetooth row's metrics), and after the join the home network's `rssi_dbm`, `ssid`, `ip` and `channel` (`selftest_api.h`; P5). `/api/help-qr`, also public, sends a QR of the Help Desk link whose anchor is `#s-safe-mode` while the WAP is in safe mode (`compose_help_url()` in `help_qr_logic.h`). | Any LAN host | none |
| Display | `GET /api/device` serves fw, flavor, chip, cores, MHz, flash, PSRAM, free heap and its low-water mark (`heap_min_kb`), uptime to the second (`up_s`), SSID, IP, `signal` (`WiFi.RSSI()` in dBm, activity class, §3.1), id and a `caps` feature array from a 1,024 B static body (`handle_device()` in `glass_web.cpp`). The mirror page reads `signal` and, since #1971, draws it as a word (`sigWord()` in `mirror_html.h`). | Any LAN host | **no Host guard** (`/api/glass` has one: `handle_glass()` asks `host_is_foreign()`) |
| Sense, Vision, Sentinel | Chip facts only in the USB boot banner; free heap and uptime in seconds in the retained MQTT health (`publish_health_retained()`) | `node_http.cpp` serves `/api/status` (`node_status.h`) and `/api/fleet`, both with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` | none (`node_status.h` calls the body coarse on purpose, but it carries `uptime_s` to the second, P9, and `rssi` in dBm, P23; §3.6) |
| Flashers (no firmware) | Chip family and description, the full MAC, JEDEC flash size and a real-size check, the partition table, otadata, the running image's 256-byte app descriptor, the coredump header, allowlisted NVS counters, eFuse block 0 and, since #1970, the bootloader's image header and an allowlist of block 1 and 2 fields: package, wafer revision, in-package flash and PSRAM, block version (`CHIP_FUSE_FIELDS` in `canary-local/assets/intake.js`) | Shown locally; the MAC only when asked (§1.3) | physical |

Every family's `/api/status` body (`firmware/common/network/node_status.h`) already carries several things:

- `fw`, `uptime_s` and `ip`, and the `hw` board token where the build names a board: the trio, the WAP's S3 target (since #1971) and the displays whose board header defines one. The flagship, the WAP's C3 target and the ten display envs on the 4.3B and 4.3C boards send no `hw` (ledger F398);
- `rssi` in dBm, `name` (the name a person typed at setup on the flagship and the WAP; the configured device id on the display and the trio, `cfg.device_id`), `saved_networks`, and `tz` on every family but the WAP, which sets none;
- one posture word, `creds_at_rest`, which is `"plaintext-nvs"` on every shipped build.

Every family's body also carries `wall_polled_s_ago`, the seconds since anything last polled `/api/fleet` (written by `node_status_build()`). On the display and the trio that body is open to the LAN with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (the trio stamps the poll in `node_http.cpp`'s `handle_fleet()`, the display in `glass_web.cpp`'s). §3.6 records it as a legacy answer that may not grow, and PR 11 shrinks it (P23).

On the WAP the dashboard session already reads posture, counters and activity, and open routes answer any LAN host with no session: `/api/device-info` with posture and a counter (`ap_auth`, `chain_length`) and its uptime in milliseconds; `/api/wifi` with posture (`ap_auth`, `sta_pmf`), the home network's name and the WAP's address on it, the seconds since the join (`connected_sec`) and activity, the Wi-Fi signal in dBm (P4); `/api/wifi/scan` with the name and the signal (activity) of each of the up to 20 strongest networks a sweep heard (`SCAN_CACHE_MAX`), which can include the home network's; `/api/selftest`, on every interface, with posture (safe mode and, on a build with Bluetooth, the last crash's stage and reason), its uptime in milliseconds, on the S3 builds a reading (the microphone's live level, `last_rms`, and its age), the battery's voltage, charge percent and charge state, the card's total and free bytes and, after the join, the home network's name, address, channel and signal in dBm (P5); and `/api/help-qr`, whose QR repeats safe mode (the WAP rows above). The public `/api/fleet` carries the same counter as `chain_height` (§3.6). §3.2 says which of those this design moves and which it records.

### 1.2 The budgets this design spends against (measured)

| Budget | Today | Source |
|---|---|---|
| WAP handler table | FULL/S3 177 of 200, **DEV/S3 142 of 152 (10 free, margin 8)**, FULL/C3 150 of 173; the `+beacon` variants 193/216, 158/168, 166/189 | `check_route_budget.py` |
| WAP route security | 203 registrations, 26 documented-public | `check_route_security.py` |
| Flagship route table | `kRouteTableSlots` 75 (mesh) / 63; worst case **75 / 63, so 0 spare**. `registerHttpHandlers` registers 75 routes in the worst case: 12 in the mesh block and 63 outside it. The comment above the constant says "14 unconditional … = 45 base"; the real unconditional set is 16 (`/`, status, chain, witness, logs, ack-all, `logs/*`, reboot, export, four Wi-Fi routes, `/api/v1/peers`, and `/api/fleet` GET and OPTIONS), so the base is 47. `check_route_budgets()` fails only when the total exceeds the slots; it reads neither the comment nor any margin. | `securacv_network.cpp` (the comment above `kRouteTableSlots`); `check_route_budgets()` in `firmware/canary/scripts/check_route_security.py` (83 registrations, 12 public) |
| WAP page gzip | `/admin` page **67,638 B** against 65,536, so **2,102 B over** (it first passed 65,536 in #1919, at 65,809 B, and the 2.4.20 train added 144 B); CSI dashboard 44,681 B; companion 48,705 B. No ceiling gate exists: `gen_web_assets_gz.py --check` only compares the committed header with what the sources generate (ledger F479) | the `B raw ->` comment above each array in `web_assets_gz.h` |
| Self-manifest | 1,600 B release buffer (flagship BLE scan builds 4,096 B; Vision 1,600 B). A host build of `self_manifest.h` with release-shaped worst-case facts (all 22 `CV_ADD_FEAT` features, the 14 release console commands, every integer at its widest, 64-hex keys, the longest `key_at_rest` label, an empty `fleet`) writes **1,265 B**, so about 335 B of headroom; with a `q` command named `details` added it writes 1,294 B. `test_self_manifest.cpp` pins the worst case from PR 10. | `emit_self_manifest()` in `firmware/canary/src/main.cpp` and in `firmware/projects/canary-vision/src/main.cpp` |

Sense reads whole lines into its tuning console (`help`, `cfg`, `set`, `reset`, `stream`, `raw`), not single keys. The display and Sentinel read no USB input. | each tree's `handle_serial_commands()`; the flagship's `kConsoleCommands`; `firmware/common/console/tuning_console.h` |
| Flash slots (guarded) | Each guard measures the built `.bin` (`check_slot_budget.py` and `firmware.yml`'s size guard both read the file's size). `firmware/flavors.json` guards 18 envs. Measured that way by CI on `62bc0bc7`'s firmware tree (not run on a device): **`canary-display-nightstand-c6`, 0x1F0000 slot, 1,989,488 B, 42,128 B free**, the tightest guarded image; **`canary-sense-wellbeing`**, a release OTA image (`firmware-release.yml` builds and budget-checks it), 0x1E0000, 1,868,096 B, **97,984 B free**; `canary-sense-default`, 0x1E0000, 1,864,256 B, 101,824 B free; `canary-sentinel-lite`, 0x140000, 1,162,304 B, 148,416 B free; the flagship's `release_ha`, the published OTA image, 0x1E0000, 1,766,256 B, 199,824 B free; `canary-wap-default`, 0x330000, 2,398,432 B, 943,904 B free. **`canary-display-nightlight-c3`** is held at the old-table 0x1E0000 ceiling on purpose so fielded min_spiffs boards stay OTA-reachable (the note above its env in `firmware/envs/platformio/canary-display.ini`): 1,611,552 B, 354,528 B under it. The other guards: 0x1E0000 (flagship `release`, `canary-vision-default`, `canary-vision-xiao-c3`, Sentinel door), 0x330000 (`canary-vision-xiao-s3`, `canary-display-watch`) and 0x640000 (`canary-display-dash7`, `-nightstand7`, `-nightstand-s3`, `-touch169` and `-amoled241`, which fill 27.7 to 35.9 % of their 6,553,600 B slots). Both 4 MB displays already build `-DCD_LEAN_BUILD=1`. Thirteen display build envs have **no size guard** (`canary-display-dash`, `-playground`, the ten `-dash-*` feature envs and `-watch-modes`; `check_slot_budget.py`: "unguarded envs pass"). PlatformIO's "Flash used" line, which `docs/PARITY.md`'s build table carries, is not the `.bin`: in the same CI run it read 58,972 to 67,928 B under the `.bin` on the nightstand-c6 and the two Sense images. | `firmware/flavors.json` `size_guards`; the size guard step of the Firmware Build run on the 2.4.20 train's push to main (the header gives the run) |
| Emulator compile set | `src/main.cpp`, `glass_settings.cpp`, `runtime_config.cpp`, `trust.cpp`, `diagnostics.cpp`, `hal/chime.cpp`, `net/mqtt_mgr.cpp`, `net/provision.cpp`, `ui/*`, `care/*`, `fleet/*`, `common/boot/boot_banner.cpp`; plus `common/color/color_engine.cpp`, `look_engine.cpp`, `plumage.cpp` and `src/hal/ambient_led.cpp` on nightstand, touch169, amoled241 and nightlight; plus `io/rtc.cpp` on touch169 and amoled241; plus everything under `canary-local/emulator/src/` and `shim/`. **Not** `glass_web.cpp`, `discovery.cpp` or `hal/display_*.cpp`. | `FIRMWARE_SRCS` and `EMU_SRCS` in `canary-local/emulator/build.sh` |

### 1.3 What the apps show today

- **Apple apps.** The iPhone and the Apple TV each have a per-device view (`DeviceDetailView`, in `ios/Sources/SecuraCV/Views` and in `tvos/WitnessWall`). The iPhone's shows trust, health and a Firmware row, a Birth Certificate card with the key fingerprint in full and a Firmware row of its own (`BirthCertificateCard.swift`; the Keys tab lists each pinned fingerprint too, `KeysView.swift`), and, since #1896, a software-update section for a display or a paired WAP (`FirmwareUpdateSection`); the TV's shows the device's figure, name, product and status. Neither shows a fact read from the chip, and each About section describes the app.
  - The iPhone captures Improv `deviceInfo` (`ImprovClient.swift`) and never shows it.
  - Its BLE console decoder (`BLESnapshot` in `BLEConsole.swift`) decodes `id`, which the WAP sends (`build_snapshot()` in `ble_console.cpp`), and none of `up`, `heap`, `wrssi` or `fw`, which that function sends too.
- **Flashers.** Since #1970 (PR 1):
  - Both show an About this board card from one model (`aboutBoardGroups` in `canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`; the desktop's copy in `desktop/src/app.js` is held to it by `desktop_parity.test.js`): the chip and its revision, the flash, and, folded away, what the chip says and what the installed image declares. A fact that could not be read says so.
  - The hardware address draws no bytes until the person presses Show (`macRevealFact()` in `canary-local/assets/flash.js`, `macRevealRow()` in `desktop/src/app.js`). The desktop's connect line, fleet list and health card, and the browser's hello card and Board report, no longer print it.
  - Saved files are named by the local day and a counter, never the address (`fileStamp`, `reportFileName` and `backupFileName` in `flash-core.js`; `rescue::backup_filename` in `desktop/flash-engine/src/rescue.rs`).
  - Each flasher saves two reports, and neither carries the address. "Save report" writes the share projection (`reportShareProjection`): chip, image and partition facts, and the day. "Save full report for your own records" (`reportFullProjection`) keeps what the health check read: the lifetime boot counter, the witness record count, a chain-head prefix, the coredump header, the OTA pending-verify state, and a millisecond `generatedAt`. Both flashers still show "Lifetime boots" on screen: the browser in its hello card's passport strip and its Board report (`passportRows` in `flash-core.js`, `renderReport()` in `flash.js`), the desktop in its passport and its health card (`renderPassport()` and `renderHealthReport()` in `desktop/src/app.js`).
  - Two things are left, both filed open: a serial line no pattern names still passes into the diagnostic report's log tail (`sanitizeLogTail()`, ledger A132), and the desktop keys its fleet book and each board's stored API key by the address (`bookKey(mac, deviceId)`, ledger A133, PR 18).
  - The Lab app's native USB bench (`canary-local/assets/flash-native.js`) runs the same engine commands as the desktop (`detect_chip`, the `serial:*` events) and keeps the desktop's diagnostics by the parity rule in its header, pinned in `desktop_parity.test.js`. It draws the same About rows from `detect_chip`'s answer; it has no fuse read and no image read, so those rows say "Read with the Flasher". It keeps the address `detect_chip` returns (`st.mac`) and never draws it.
  - The desktop's chip guard still folds espflash's "esp32s3 (revision v0.2)" down to "ESP32-S3" (`canonical_chip` in `desktop/flash-engine/src/catalog.rs`); the revision now reaches the About card on its own (`rescue::parse_revision`).
- **Home Assistant.** Since #1891 (PR 2) it reads `firmware_version` from the **health** topic, where every firmware puts it (for example `publish_health_retained()` in `firmware/projects/canary-sense/src/net/mqtt_mgr.cpp`), and shows it as the device page's firmware (`_async_record_health_firmware` in `custom_components/securacv/__init__.py`). The page's model is the product, named from the status topic's `device_type` through `CANARY_PRODUCT_NAMES` (`const.py`). Until a health reports one it shows what the status topic carries: `firmware_version`, `fw_version`, or the flagship's `firmware`. Before #1891 it read the status topic alone, so the field stayed empty.

### 1.4 Values that are wrong today

PR 6 (#1971) and PR 7 (#1892) fixed most of these at their source, before any Details screen shows them: PR 5 depends on PR 6. A unit keeps sending the old values until it runs a release that carries #1971 (fw-v2.4.20 is the first; see the header), so the planned vocabulary's `legacy` rows list each one as `known_wrong`, and every app's legacy adapter renders a `known_wrong` value as "cannot say", never as the value.

Fixed on main:

- The flagship mDNS `model` was the literal `"XIAO ESP32S3"` on every env. Since #1971 it is `CANARY_BOARD_NAME` (`canary_config.h`; each board port's env sets its own), and a build that names no board sends no `model` (`mdns_txt.h`).
- Its HA discovery `mdl` was `"Canary v1"`. Since #1971 it is `CANARY_PRODUCT_NAME` (`"Canary"`), and the board goes in `hw` (`securacv_mqtt.cpp`).
- The WAP chip fallback was `"ESP32-S3"` when the model is unknown. Since #1971 it is empty (`sys_monitor::init()`): the serial monitor prints "not reported", and `/admin`'s chip row reads `--` before its revision.
- The WAP Improv hardware string was `"XIAO ESP32-S3"` on every target, the C3 included. Since #1971 it is `HARDWARE_BOARD_NAME`, set per target in `build_config.h` (`ble_improv.cpp`).
- Firmware placeholders were hard-coded: `3.1.0` and `'3.0.0'` in `web_ui.h`, and `2.0.1` in `securacv_webui.cpp`. Since #1971 both dashboards' Firmware rows read `--` until the device answers (`test_honest_names.test.js` holds them).
- The display mirror page had two label errors (`mirror_html.h`), both fixed in #1971:
  - it called every non-watch display a "wall panel"; the Kind now comes from the device type `/api/status` names (`KINDS`);
  - it labeled dBm as "dB"; the signal now reads as a word (`sigWord()`), not a number with a unit.
- The Sense, Vision and Sentinel health published `"battery":100` beside `"battery_present":false`. Since #1971 they send no `battery` value, and Home Assistant shows the battery as unknown whenever a health says none is present.
- The flagship's watchdog self-test returned `true` on both branches. Since #1971 the `"watchdog"` row of `TESTS` in `securacv_diagnostics.cpp` has `nullptr` for its probe: it reads "not a probe", and the score counts the nine real probes.
- Vision's `module_id` was a RAM address. Since #1892 it is 1 or 0 (`module_id()` in `vision_mgr.cpp`, §7.3).

Still wrong on main (ledger F398):

- On the Touch 1.69 board the display banner falls to its `#else` glass line and describes a 1.47-inch ST7789 panel with no touch (the `ST7789 1.47` line in `firmware/projects/canary-display/src/main.cpp`). `main.cpp` is emulator-compiled, so the fix is PR 17's.
- The display's health still publishes `"battery":100` beside `"battery_present":false` (`publish_health_retained()` in its `mqtt_mgr.cpp`). That file is emulator-compiled too, so it is fixed in the dist PR (PR 17).
- A WAP on mains power publishes the same pair (`publish_health()` in `csi_mqtt.cpp`). Home Assistant reads it as unknown since #1971, so nothing shows it. Dropping the value changes a wire body `test_wap_mqtt_contract.py` pins, and whether to drop it is an open decision in F398.

---

## 2. The vocabulary

### 2.1 Where it lives

The vocabulary is `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json`, a file of its own beside `spec/witness_dictionary.json`. #1961 (sweep HA56) created it with its first rows, the answer keys a diagnostics dump redacts (§2.2), and `spec/README.md` lists the two files together as the machine-readable vocabularies. This design first placed the vocabulary as a `device_details` block inside the dictionary, on AGENTS.md rule 5; main built the separate file, so the plan grows that file and adds no block beside it. This document still calls the file's contents "the block". The dictionary's `attention` and `household_posture` blocks are the precedent for a vocabulary that is not an event list and has its own normative prose.

- **Normative prose.** This document, committed as `docs/design/device_details.md`.
- **Where they disagree.** From PR 3 the file's `$comment` says this document wins and the file is the bug, the same governance the dictionary's `governance` field keeps with `event_contract.md`. Today's `$comment` (#1961) describes the rows and the generator and names no prose.
- **The witness dictionary's own ids are reused, never redefined.** A part's `modality` must be one of the dictionary's `modalities.items[].id`: `camera`, `wifi-csi`, `radar`, `contact` or `other`.

What stays out of the block, so nothing is said twice:

| Fact | Stays in | The block refers to it by |
|---|---|---|
| MCU, flash, PSRAM, board tier | `firmware/boards/boards.json` | board id |
| Which peripherals a board carries | each board's `pins.h` `HAS_*` flags; `devices/<slug>/device.json` `peripherals` | peripheral id |
| Figures and the confidence ladder | `canary-local/devices/figures.json` | board token (its `hardware` key) |
| Library versions per release | `sbom/sbom-firmware.cdx.json` | (read at runtime from compile-time macros) |
| Feature list and console commands | the self-manifest's `features[]` and `commands[]` | not repeated |

`devices/device.schema.json` is untouched. Device manifests stay a join of ids, with no feature lists, prose or status (`devices/README.md`).

### 2.2 What the block holds

**Built (#1961).** Beside its `$comment` the file holds `vocabulary_version` (1; the generator refuses any other), `classes` and `keys`:

```jsonc
"classes": { "identifier":     { "redacted": true, "means": "A value that names one unit, or one radio peer of it, ..." },
             "health_counter": { "redacted": true, "means": "A count or an age a Canary reports about itself. ..." } },
"keys": [ { "key": "hw_token", "class": "identifier", "means": "the salted hardware token",
            "surfaces": [ "firmware/projects/canary-wap/arduino/canary_wap/identity_json.h",
                          "firmware/canary/lib/securacv_network/src/securacv_network.cpp" ] }, ... ]
```

A `keys` row is a wire key, not a fact: the key, its class, what it means, and the firmware sources (`surfaces`) that emit it as a JSON key. At `62bc0bc7` there are eleven rows, six `identifier` (`hw_token`, `mdns_host`, `module_id`, `local_address`, `address`, `peer_address`) and five `health_counter` (`uptime`, `boot_count`, `sd_writes`, `http_requests`, `last_frame_age_ms`). They are the keys P18 names, except `serial_number` and `chip_id`, which no firmware source emits and which stay literals in `diagnostics.py`'s `INTEGRATION_REDACT_KEYS`. The file is closed, and every row is checked against the firmware: `scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py`'s `validate()` refuses a field it does not know or a missing one (at the top level, in a class and in a row), a row that names no source or names a file that is not a firmware device source, and a row whose named source does not emit its key as the generator's scanner reads it. The scanner reads source text, not the shipped build: it counts a key in dead code (`#if 0`) as sent, and the generator's docstring lists its known false passes.

**Planned (PR 3 onward).** Everything else in this section. The fields below join the same file beside `keys`, and the generator learns each one. The schema stays closed at every level, checked by the generator as today and by `scripts/lint_device_details.py` (new in PR 3). `legacy` (§3.6) records what a `keys` row records, a key an existing answer carries and its class, and adds the answer and the tier it reaches.

**Open, and the maintainer's to decide before PR 3 writes a row:**

- **Row shape and row state (ledger HA62).** A built row is a key checked against firmware sources and has no state for a key no firmware sends yet or sends no longer, while a fact row below carries a state per family (`planned` among them) and `legacy` entries: do the `keys` rows become `legacy` entries or stay a list of their own, and is a retired key a row state or a committed list of every key ever redacted?
- **Classes.** The built file's two classes are named for what a dump redacts and do not nest in §3.1's ten (built `identifier` holds `hw_token`, a `handle` in §3.1; built `health_counter` holds `last_frame_age_ms`, `activity` in §3.1; and both sets use the name `identifier`): does PR 3 move the eleven rows into §3.1's classes, each class keeping a `redacted` flag, or keep the two as a separate redaction axis? Either way the generator refuses a run that would stop redacting a key the committed module redacts (§2.3).

Two more ledger items wait on this section. HA63 (device-uptime keys in no redacted class; the generator's scanner finds six in firmware sources: `uptime_s`, `uptime_ms`, `uptime_sec`, `bucket_uptime_s`, the display `/api/device`'s `up_s` and the WAP Bluetooth console snapshot's `up`) waits on a class for each key, which waits in turn on the class question above. F430 (the identifier lint) takes its deny list from the `never` rows below; §10 item 3 gives the rest of its scope.

```jsonc
{
  "$comment": "...normative prose: docs/design/device_details.md ...",
  "vocabulary_version": 1,                 // built
  "classes":  { /* built: identifier, health_counter. §3.1's ten are planned (build, unit, counter, posture, activity, household, handle, reading, identifier, secret); how the two sets meet is open (above) */ },
  "keys":     [ /* built: the eleven wire-key rows (above) */ ],
  "record_schema": "securacv.canary.details/v1",
  "families": ["canary","canary-wap","canary-display","canary-sense","canary-vision","canary-sentinel"],
  "tiers":    { /* §3.2: the classes each tier may carry */ },
  "gates":    { /* §3.4: per family, the function that earns each tier, and the "held" phrase */ },
  "parts":    [ {"id":"about","cap":512}, {"id":"now","cap":768}, ... ],
  "groups":   [ {"id":"chip","label":"Chip","symbol":"cpu"}, ... ],
  "levels":   [ "glance","about","details","part","technical" ],
  "provenance": [ "read","set","board","build","rom","datasheet","emulated" ],
  "status":   { "ladder": ["driven","driven_bench_only","bench_gated","staged","absent"],
                "runtime": ["working","not_responding","stalled","stopped_answering","turned_off","starting"] },   // §8's six runtime words, in order
  "cannot_say": { /* §6.3: seven phrases, chosen from data */ },
  "words":    { "reset": [ {"id":"power_on","idf":"ESP_RST_POWERON","idf_since":"3.2","label":"Power was connected","fault":false},
                           {"id":"usb","idf":"ESP_RST_USB","idf_since":"5.2", ...},
                           {"id":"efuse","idf":"ESP_RST_EFUSE","idf_since":"5.3", ...}, ... ] },
  "formats":  [ {"id":"bytes_iec","vectors":[[8388608,"8 MB"],[327680,"320 KB"]]}, ... ],
  "facts":    [ /* one row per fact (the row below) */ ],
  "part_kinds": [ /* §7: one sheet per kind of part */ ],
  "disclosures": [ /* §7.2: what leaves a device without anyone asking, held to code */ ],
  "never":    [ /* the identifiers and secrets no SecuraCV surface may read or show, each with its APIs and the exceptions §7.2's list 6 sanctions for it; today's leaks are PR 4's KNOWN_LEAK rows (§10 item 3) */ ],
  "legacy":   [ /* §3.6: existing answers and the keys each may carry, frozen */ ],
  "gaps":     [ /* known gaps: a fact a family cannot give yet, each naming the fact, the families, the decision that holds it (§14) and what ends it */ ]
}
```

A fact row:

```jsonc
{ "id": "chip.rev", "part": "chip", "group": "chip", "level": "about",
  "label": "Revision",
  "explain": "Which printing of the chip this is. Newer printings fix small hardware bugs.",
  "term": "silicon revision (eFuse major.minor)",      // shown only at the technical level
  "type": "str", "max": 6, "format": "chip_rev", "class": "unit",
  "share": true, "adjustable": null,
  "sources": {
    "canary-wap":     { "state": "read", "api": "efuse_hal_get_major_chip_version / _minor_" },
    "canary-display": { "state": "read", "api": "efuse_hal_get_major_chip_version / _minor_" },
    "flasher":        { "state": "rom",  "api": "esptool-js chip description / espflash board-info" } },
  "legacy": [ { "surface": "canary-wap GET /api/system", "path": "device.revision", "tier": "lan", "note": "ESP.getChipRevision()" },
              { "surface": "canary-display GET /api/device", "path": "chip", "tier": "open", "parse": "after ' r'",
                "note": "lan from PR 11, which adds the Host and Origin refusal (P10)" } ] }
```

Two optional row fields carry rules the targets enforce:

- **`duration: true`** marks a fact whose value is a length of time. Only such a fact may end in `_ms`, `_s` or `_h` (§10 item 2).
- **`on_device: true`** marks a fact a Canary's own page or glass shows. Only these labels are generated into firmware (§2.3), so a copy edit to any other fact never moves firmware bytes or the emulator dist.

A `legacy` entry names the tier its surface reaches (the `held` generator and §7.2's list 2 read it, §3.2), and may also list **`known_wrong`** values (§1.4), which the adapters render as "cannot say".

`sources[family].state` is one of the following. The UI never draws a fact whose state is `planned`.

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `read` | The firmware reads it from the part. |
| `set` | The firmware applied it; it is not read back. |
| `board` | Compiled in from the board's pins header. |
| `build` | Compiled in from the image. |
| `rom` | A flasher reads it with no firmware running. |
| `planned` | A PR will add it. |
| `cannot` | A platform limit the UI must state. |
| `absent` | Not on this family. |

Platform limits (`cannot`), checked in the SDKs of the two Arduino cores `firmware/envs/platformio/platforms.ini` pins, arduino-esp32 2.0.17 on IDF 4.4.7 and 3.3.8 on IDF 5.5.4 (each chip's `sdkconfig` and `esp_efuse_table.h`, read again at `0a06c14b`; the pins are unchanged at `62bc0bc7`, and #1986 then moved the core-3 pin to 3.3.11 on IDF 5.5.5, whose SDK this design has not reread):

- No power-management or light-sleep statistics, because `CONFIG_PM_ENABLE` is off in both prebuilt cores.
- No per-task list on the core-2 builds, because the trace facility is compiled out.
- `usb_serial_jtag_is_connected()` exists on IDF 5 only.
- The embedded flash SKU fields (`ESP_EFUSE_FLASH_CAP`, `_FLASH_TEMP`, `_FLASH_VENDOR`) are in the IDF 5 eFuse tables of the S3, the C3 and the C6. The PSRAM SKU fields (`ESP_EFUSE_PSRAM_CAP`, `_PSRAM_TEMP`, `_PSRAM_VENDOR`) are in the IDF 5 S3 table only. None of the six is in the IDF 4.4 tables (2.0.17, S3 and C3). So the `cannot` state is per (core, chip), not per family: a source may carry `cannot_on: [{"core":"2","chip":"esp32c3"}, ...]`, and the flash SKU rows read "Not available on this build" only on core 2, while the PSRAM SKU rows also read it on a core-3 C3 or C6.

Two of the survey's IDF-5-only claims were wrong. `esp_flash_get_physical_size()` and `efuse_hal_get_major_chip_version()` exist on IDF 4.4.7 as well (the 2.0.17 SDK declares them in `spi_flash/include/esp_flash.h` and `hal/include/hal/efuse_hal.h`), so the revision is one `vM.m` rendering on both cores.

A `never` row:

```jsonc
{ "id": "factory_mac", "class": "identifier", "label": "Factory network address",
  "apis": ["esp_efuse_mac_get_default","esp_read_mac","esp_base_mac_addr_get","esp_wifi_get_mac",
           "WiFi.macAddress","WiFi.softAPmacAddress","ESP.getEfuseMac"],
  "flasher": "reveal_only" }
```

`flasher` records the flashers' exception: both flashers draw the address only when the person asks ("Hardware address · Show"), never copy it, and write no byte of it, not even a tail, into a report or a file name (§5.6; built in #1970). The desktop's fleet book in prefs is still keyed by it, until PR 18 re-keys the book. Two other rows carry the exceptions §7.2's list 6 sanctions for them, in an optional `except` field that names who receives the value and by what route (in the table below). An `except` entry is part of the design; a `KNOWN_LEAK` row (§10 item 3) is a leak, and that list must shrink to empty.

The other `never` rows:

| Row | What it covers |
|---|---|
| `ble_address` | `NimBLEDevice::getAddress`, `ble_hs_id_copy_addr` |
| `efuse_unique_id` | `ESP_EFUSE_OPTIONAL_UNIQUE_ID` |
| `efuse_calibration_values` | the per-chip trims and calibration fields: `K_RTC_LDO`, `K_DIG_LDO`, `V_RTC_DBIAS20`, `V_DIG_DBIAS20`, `DIG_DBIAS_HVT`, `THRES_HVT`, the C6's `*_DBIAS` and `DBIAS_VOL_GAP`, `TEMP_CALIB`, `OCODE`, every `ADC*_INIT_CODE*` and `ADC*_CAL_VOL*`, and on the classic ESP32 `ADC_VREF` (`ADC_VREF_AND_SDIO_DREF` in the 2.0.17 table), `ADC1_TP_*` and `ADC2_TP_*`; the lint denies every `ESP_EFUSE_ADC*` descriptor (§10 item 3). The values differ per chip and would fingerprint it. "Calibration present" is derived from the block-version fields only (§5.6). |
| `flash_unique_id` | `esp_flash_read_unique_chip_id` |
| `sd_cid` | the card's serial, held in the same register as the rest of its identity |
| `ieee802154_eui64` | `ESP_MAC_IEEE802154` |
| `espnow_peers` | `esp_now_fetch_peer`, `esp_wifi_ap_get_sta_list`, except the count |
| `third_party_ble_address` | other devices' Bluetooth addresses. `except`: on the WAP, the app paired with this Canary's key (`/api/bluetooth` with the bearer, from PR 8; §7.2 list 6) |
| `router_bssid` | the CSI HAL holds one copy (`s_assoc_bssid` in `csi_hal.cpp`, from `esp_wifi_sta_get_ap_info()`) and compares each frame's sender against it; the copy is never copied further, logged or serialized, and `deinit()` wipes it |
| `sscma_module_id` | a firmware call of the SSCMA library's `ID()` (`AI.ID(`), and a flasher sending the module `ID?`. The pinned library's own I2C `begin()` sends `ID?` once at boot and keeps the answer in a private buffer nothing reads (§7.3); the row denies our calls, and whether that one read is accepted or `begin()` is replaced is open (§12 PR 7) |
| `sscma_module_text` | `AI.info(`, `AI.name(` text: compared, never echoed (§7.8) |
| `sscma_credentials` | the Wi-Fi and MQTT settings stored on the module: the SSCMA library's `WIFI()` and `MQTT()` getters, neither of which any firmware here calls |
| `coredump_image` | `esp_core_dump_image_get`; presence is a fact, the image never is |
| `keys_tokens_passwords` | present or absent only. `except`: the setup receipt's API token and setup-network password, on the flagship and the WAP, to a bearer or a BOOT tap, and on the WAP also to the setup-door claim; and the flagship's API token in the pages `page_token_decide()` grants it to (§7.2 list 6) |

### 2.3 What generates from it

`scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py` (#1961) is the generator. `lint.yml` runs its `--check` (the step "Device-details vocabulary — generated module current, every row emitted"), and `scripts/tests/test_gen_device_vocabulary.py` pins it. Today it writes one file, `custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py`. Every other row of the table below is planned. Each but the last joins this same script; the last is `gen_flash.py` reading the block itself, a separate script with its own link in §2.4's chain. At `62bc0bc7` none of the new files exists, no page has a label region, and `gen_flash.py` reads no vocabulary. Each file's header says it is generated and names the generator.

| Output | Read by |
|---|---|
| `firmware/common/details/device_details_keys.h` (from PR 9) | The flagship, display, Sense, Sentinel and Vision PlatformIO trees through their `-I` build flag for `firmware/common`; the display sketch through the `common_files` list that `firmware/projects/canary-display/setup.sh regen` copies flat, which needs a `details/` rule in its `flatten_includes()` (and an `attest/` one for `self_manifest.h`, which `device_details.h` extends) in the PR that first stages them; the WAP, whose PlatformIO envs compile the sketch directory and add an `-I` only for `firmware/common/boot`, through a byte-identical staged copy in the sketch held by `firmware/scripts/check_details_sync.sh` **(new in PR 8)**; and the host tests. It holds `enum class K`, each key's string, class, part, type and cap; the generated worst-case byte bound **per (part, sensor kind)**; the **`held` constant per (family, shipped configuration, part, sensor kind, tier, core)** (§4.1); `DETAILS_VOCAB_REV`; and the reset-word map. Each reset row is guarded on the IDF release that introduced it, `#if ESP_IDF_VERSION >= ESP_IDF_VERSION_VAL(M,m,0)` after including `esp_idf_version.h`, never on `#ifdef`: `ESP_RST_USB`, `_JTAG`, `_EFUSE`, `_PWR_GLITCH` and `_CPU_LOCKUP` are enumerators of `esp_reset_reason_t`, not macros, so `#ifdef` on any of them is always false. The first two first appear in IDF v5.2.0 and the other three in v5.3.0; patch releases of the older lines gained all five later (v5.1.4 and v5.2.2 have them), and a guard on the 5.2.0 or 5.3.0 floor leaves the word out there (`components/esp_system/include/esp_system.h` at espressif/esp-idf's release tags, read on 2026-11-16). The IDFs of the two Arduino cores the six families build on, 4.4.7 and 5.5.4, have none of the five and all of them. The tree's own guard on three of them, in `improv_ble.cpp`'s `reset_is_power_cycle()` and its mirror in the WAP sketch, names 5.1.0. It builds on both of those, and it would not build on v5.1.0 to v5.1.3, nor on v5.2.0 and v5.2.1, which have `ESP_RST_USB` and `ESP_RST_JTAG` but not `ESP_RST_PWR_GLITCH`. So it is not the floor to copy (ledger F480 holds the guard and the comments beside it). The generator refuses a reason with no `idf_since`. **It holds no label, and no key for `household`, `handle`, `reading`, `identifier` or `secret` facts.** |
| `firmware/common/details/device_details_labels.h` (from PR 17) | The display glass's `settings_ui.cpp` only: ASCII C strings for the `on_device` facts the glass shows. It is emulator-compiled from PR 17, so an edit to one of these labels moves the dist (§2.4). |
| Marked label regions in `web_ui.h` and `companion_pwa.h` (WAP, from PR 12), `securacv_webui.cpp` (flagship, from PR 14), and both copies of `mirror_html.h` (display, from PR 11) | Each page's JavaScript label table for the `on_device` facts, written between generated begin/end markers in the page source, the way `gen_cad_params.py` writes a manifest-owned literal into a hand-written `.scad`. The generator holds a table of the regions it owns; `--check` refuses a missing, duplicated or edited region **for each page in that table**. A page joins the table in the PR that first fills it, and that PR runs §2.4's whole chain for it: the WAP pages are gzipped afterwards (`gen_web_assets_gz.py`; its raw literals are compiled out, and it gzips them verbatim, so a marker alone moves `web_assets_gz.h` and grows `/admin`'s gzip, which no gate caps today: at `62bc0bc7` it is 67,638 B by the generator's own settings, 2,102 B past the 65,536 B ceiling this plan set (§5.3, §14); it was 67,494 B at `0a06c14b`, and the 2.4.20 train (#1972) added 144 B), and the mirror page is copied into the display sketch (`setup.sh regen`, held by `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`). |
| `canary-local/devices/device_details.json` (from PR 10) | The browser flasher and the Lab (same-origin fetch); the desktop Flasher (`desktop/src-tauri/build.rs` bakes it beside `flash.json`; bundles copy with `cp -RL` per `.github/RELEASE_LESSONS.md`); the website (fetched by path, listed in `upstream-contract.json`, carried back as `canary-local/devices/site_contract.json`, held by `scripts/tests/test_site_contract.py`) |
| `ios/Shared/DeviceDetailsVocab.swift` (from PR 3) | The iPhone and iPad screens and the TV. It carries the `SecuraCV-Parity` marker, so `lint_apple_parity.py` requires `tvos/WitnessWall/project.yml` to list it. `ios/project.yml` also compiles `- path: Shared` wholesale into `SecuraCVWidgets`, `SecuraCVWatch` and `SecuraCVWatchWidgets`, so the file is present there but unused. The "no details in widgets" rule is therefore scoped by source path, not by target: the three widget and notification source directories, and the Shared files those targets render (`FleetGlanceViews.swift`, `FleetActivityAttributes.swift`, `WakePayload.swift`). This file is on none of those paths, so it needs no exemption (§6.5, §10 item 2). |
| `custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py` (built, #1961) | Home Assistant. Today it holds `DEVICE_KEY_CLASSES`, `REDACTED_CLASSES` and `REDACTED_DEVICE_KEYS`, and `diagnostics.py` unions the last into `TO_REDACT`, so a key the block gains in a redacted class is redacted with no edit there. A run that would stop redacting a key the committed module redacts is refused, unless `INTEGRATION_REDACT_KEYS` in `diagnostics.py` names the key or `--allow-drop` does. The planned HA projections (§6.12, PR 16) read their facts here too. The module reaches the HACS mirror only through `homeassistant-mirror.yml`. |
| `spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json` (from PR 9) | The golden bytes: every part at every tier for a fixed set of facts, written by the generator's **reference writer** (Python, from the vocabulary). `test_device_details.cpp` compiles the firmware writer and compares its bytes to this file; it writes nothing. This is the `spec/fixtures/gen_witness_page_v1.py` pattern, where the script writes and the C++ test compares (`firmware.yml`'s step "Check the witness-page fixture is fresh (generator --check)"), and two writers in two languages that agree are the point of a golden file. |
| `custom_components/securacv/tests/fixtures/details_v1.json` (from PR 9) and `details_vectors.json` (from PR 3) | The HA tests. The first is a byte copy of `spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json`; the second holds the format, share-projection and signature vectors. Both live **inside** the carried tree because the HACS mirror's CI runs `pytest custom_components/securacv/tests` with no `spec/` (its `tests.yml` deselects the three tests that check against monorepo files and have no skip of their own, and the others that read monorepo files skip themselves there as "monorepo-only"; #1961 kept the generator's own test in `scripts/tests/` so it would not be one more deselect). |
| `docs/device_details_reference.md` (from PR 3) | People: every fact, its class, the tiers that may carry it per family, each family's "What it can share" lists, and the block's known gaps (`gaps`). Indexed from `docs/README.md`. |
| `canary-local/tools/gen_flash.py` (reads the block) | The flashers' `settings_help` topics (`chip`, `mac`, `flash_size`, `temperature`, `boots`, `self_check`, …) take their `label` and `explain` text from the block (`flash.json` spells the second `what`), so help-dot copy has one source |

**Each output joins the generator in the PR that first has a reader for it** (the "from PR n" notes above). PR 3 adds only outputs outside `firmware/`: the Swift vocabulary, what the Python module #1961 began still lacks, the reference doc and the HA vectors; in the same PR `gen_flash.py` starts taking the flashers' help text from the block. The JSON vocabulary joins in PR 10, where the flashers, the Lab and the website first read it. So PR 3 changes no firmware byte, and no firmware tree carries a generated file nothing compiles. Firmware sources are already inputs, though: `--check` re-reads every source a `keys` row names (§2.4).

**A generator that cannot spell a value must refuse.** Any row a target cannot express fails the run and names the row. The built generator already does this for a key or class name outside `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`. Planned examples:

- a non-ASCII byte in a C label;
- a format with no test vectors;
- a `max` that would push a part past its cap, for any sensor kind that part serves.

### 2.4 Regeneration order

The generated-files order lives in two files: `docs/GENERATED_FILES.md` is the account, and `.claude/rules/generated.md` is the path-scoped rule that points at it. Both already carry today's chain, which has one link: edit `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json`, run `python3 scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py`, commit the file and the module together. A firmware edit is an input too. `--check` re-reads every source a `keys` row names, so renaming or dropping such a key turns `lint.yml` red, and the old key goes into `diagnostics.py`'s `INTEGRATION_REDACT_KEYS`, with the release that last sent it, before the row moves.

The list below is the chain as the planned outputs join it. Each PR that adds a link adds it to both files.

```
edit spec/device_details_vocabulary.json
  → scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py                         (every output above: the golden fixture from its reference writer,
                                                              the HA copy of it, the headers, the page regions;
                                                              it prints "dist: moves" when an emulator-compiled output changed)
  → make -C firmware/tests_host                              (test_device_details compares the firmware writer to the fixture;
                                                              it writes nothing, so nothing here can leave a stale copy)
  → re-stage the WAP sketch's byte-identical copies          (firmware/scripts/check_details_sync.sh names any that differ)
  → firmware/projects/canary-wap/arduino/canary_wap/gen_web_assets_gz.py   (the WAP pages' label regions are compiled out raw)
  → canary-local/tools/gen_wap.py, then git diff wap.json     (it checks that companion_pwa.h still holds the wizard's titles; it has no --check, canary-local.yml's "WAP data drift" step runs it and diffs)
  → canary-local/tools/gen_homeassistant.py, if wap.json moved (it reads wap.json; that step's error names both generators)
  → canary-local/tools/gen_csp.py                            (it hashes what wap.json embeds)
  → firmware/projects/canary-display/setup.sh regen
  → if the generator printed "dist: moves": commit the edit and every regenerated file and push the branch
                                                             (the rebuild checks out the dispatched ref, so it builds the branch as GitHub has it)
      → dispatch "Rebuild emulator dist (pinned emsdk)" on that branch → pull the bot's dist commit
  → canary-local/tools/gen_flash.py                          (fw_version from the pulled dist; help text from the block)
  → commit
```

What moves the dist once PR 17 has landed:

- any change to `device_details_keys.h` (the display's `mqtt_mgr.cpp` and `settings_ui.cpp` include it) or to a glass label in `device_details_labels.h`;
- any change to `firmware/common/details/device_details.h`, because `mqtt_mgr.cpp` calls `details::build()`;
- any change to `firmware/projects/canary-display/include/canary/details_facts.h`, the facts provider that `mqtt_mgr.cpp` and `settings_ui.cpp` read through and `emu_details.cpp` implements (§5.4);
- **any change to `firmware/common/attest/self_manifest.h`**, because the details writer extends its `manifest::detail::Writer`. From PR 17 a manifest edit made for another product (the flagship and the Vision compile `self_manifest.h` today, and the WAP a staged copy from PR 9) therefore moves the display dist too, and the PR that makes it owes the rebuild;
- `canary-local/emulator/src/emu_details.cpp`, like everything under `emulator/src/`.

A change to an `explain`, a `term`, a part sheet or a label no Canary shows moves neither firmware nor the dist, because `rev` (§4.3) hashes only the wire-relevant fields and only `on_device` labels are generated into firmware. `docs/GENERATED_FILES.md`'s list of what moves the dist gains the `device_details.h`, `self_manifest.h` and `include/canary/details_facts.h` lines in PR 17.

`gen_device_vocabulary.py` already appears in the derivation grep both files give, because `lint.yml` calls it as `python3 scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py --check`.

---

## 3. Privacy classes, tiers and gates

### 3.1 Classes: what a fact is

| Class | What it is | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| `build` | The same for every unit running this image | Firmware version, git, project, build day, IDF, Arduino core and library versions (compile-time macros), product, board token, partition layout, the app descriptor's `secure_version`, the radio address modes this image uses (public or random) |
| `unit` | This unit's hardware and state, not an identifier, not attack surface, and not moved by the scene | Chip model, revision, package, wafer and block version, cores, actual CPU MHz, crystal, features; flash size, mode, speed, JEDEC vendor; embedded flash and PSRAM SKU; calibration **present**; part ids; a part's latched status word; uptime in whole hours (§4.1); free heap and PSRAM in KiB (the current value only); chip temperature in whole °C; running slot and OTA state |
| `counter` | Slowly growing per-unit counters. Over months they link one unit across networks. | Boot count, SD lifetime writes, bond count |
| `posture` | Tells an attacker what to try, or whether an attempt worked | Secure boot, flash encryption mode, NVS encryption, key at rest, JTAG and download-mode fuses, the eFuse rollback floor, SoftAP auth and PMF, "Wi-Fi password saved: yes or no"; the reset cause word, "a crash record is saved" and the crash summary (task and cause), because a panic or watchdog word answers whether a remote crash attempt landed; the heap and PSRAM low-water marks, the largest free block, fragmentation and the lowest stack headroom, because they show how close memory came to failing |
| `activity` | Describes the device but moves with what is happening in the room | A sensor's link counters (CRC errors, unknown and dropped frames: the MR60 parser decodes presence, the target count, distance, breathing rate and heart rate, counts a frame of any other type as unknown when both checksums pass and its payload is at most 32 bytes, and refuses a longer frame of any type and counts it with the CRC errors, `firmware/common/sensors/mmwave_mr60/mr60_uart.h`'s `MR60_TYPE_*` constants, `MR60_MAX_PAYLOAD` and `unknown_count()`, and `classify_()` and `push()` in `mr60_uart.cpp`; which of the module's frames those are, and whether they follow presence, is a bench question, and the dropped count rises with burst traffic while targets are present, so all three are treated as moving with the room), the last-frame age, Sentinel's per-channel votes and qualities (its light channel votes Weak when the light changes by 50 lux or more against its slow baseline, and Denied when a sensor that answered at boot fails a read, `canary-sentinel/include/canary/sentinel_links.h`'s `light_sample()`, since #1892), the Vision module's `perf()` timings (the SSCMA library's `AI.perf()`, which no firmware here calls yet), CSI frames received and drops, microphone frame and silent-line counts, and the Wi-Fi signal, in dBm or even as a word ("Strong", "Good", "Weak"): RSSI moves with bodies between the device and its router, and a word is the same reading cut at thresholds, so a body crossing the path while the link sits near one flips it. A word latched with hysteresis and a hold would still move for a body that stays in the path, so the signal is activity, not a latched `unit` word. The record spells it only as the word; dBm has no key (§3.3) |
| `household` | Facts about the home or its network | SSID, IP, channel, Wi-Fi country, broker host, saved-network count, the person-typed name, time zone, GNSS satellites and DOPs |
| `handle` | The stable handles a Canary already answers with: the WAP's provisioning receipt and its `/api/device-info` carry all three (`identity_json.h`) | Key fingerprint, `hw_token`, `device_id` |
| `reading` | Live measurements of the room | Lux, microphone level, radar range, breathing or heart rate, coordinates, motion scores, raw touch counts |
| `identifier` | Factory and per-unit identifiers | Everything in `never` with class `identifier` |
| `secret` | Credentials and memory | Keys, tokens, passwords, core dump images, module-stored credentials |

These ten classes are this design's, and none is in the tree yet. The vocabulary file main built (#1961) has two classes of its own, `identifier` and `health_counter`, named for what a diagnostics dump redacts; how the two sets meet is open (§2.2).

**A details record carries only `build`, `unit`, `counter`, `posture` and `activity` facts.** The other classes have no generated key:

- `household` facts stay where they already are. The glass shows its own network on its own screen, and no Details screen shows a device-reported SSID (the setup pickers go on listing the networks a Canary reports).
- `handle` facts come to the apps from the pairing record and the provisioning receipt, never from details.
- `reading` facts have their own channels.
- `identifier` and `secret` facts are never read.

### 3.2 Tiers: who is asking

| Tier | Who has proven what | Classes it may carry |
|---|---|---|
| `open` | Anyone in radio range, or on the LAN without naming the device | **Nothing new.** Existing contracts gain no new non-build field, and may shrink (rule 3, §5.8). |
| `lan` | A LAN request that names this device. On the WAP that is a dashboard session, which the landing page mints for any LAN host (`check_route_security.py`'s docstring: "mints a pair token for any caller that reaches the WAP"), so it is LAN trust and not owner proof; `/api/details` also refuses a foreign Host first (§3.5). On a display or the trio it is a GET whose Host names the device (`firmware/common/network/host_guard.h`) and whose Origin is absent or same-site. | `build`, `unit` in the record. What a WAP session already reads elsewhere is below. |
| `broker` | Holds the owner's broker credentials. On the WAP a dashboard session can also point the MQTT bridge at a broker of its own, and a display that holds no broker credentials follows a LAN referral, so there the broker bodies reach the lan tier too (§3.6). | `build`, `unit` (the `about` part only) |
| `serial` | Holds the USB cable | `build`, `unit`, `counter`, `posture`, `activity`. The diagnostic console stays "read-only, public-only" (`docs/design/self_star_roadmap.md`, the line that ends "on the diagnostic console"): none of these is a secret. Posture is here because the cable holder already holds the `rom` tier, where any flasher reads the eFuse posture, and because the console already prints it: the flagship's `f` card (Provenance / fingerprint) prints SecureBoot, FlashEnc and KeyAtRest, and Boot/Seq beside them (`firmware/canary/src/main.cpp`, `case 'f'`), and the self-manifest carries `key_at_rest` and `boots` (`firmware/common/attest/self_manifest.h`). A held count there would claim to withhold what the same cable already shows. Serial-tier lines must never reach a shared report: from PR 10 both flashers' `sanitizeLogTail()` replace each details line with its share projection (§6.4). Since PR 1 (#1970) they replace the lines `TAIL_IDENTITY` names, the self-manifest line among them, and a line no pattern names still passes (ledger A132); no firmware prints a details line yet. |
| `glass` | Touching the display's own screen | `build`, `unit`, `counter`, `posture`, `activity` (household facts show on the glass from its own state, not from the record) |
| `owner` | Holds this Canary's bearer token. On the WAP that is one accounted bearer grant (§3.5), which the administrator page (`/admin` and `/settings`, `web_ui.h`) sends once its owner signs in with the token (pasted, or fetched by a BOOT tap, and held in the page's memory only; since #1887 the sign-in checks a pasted token by itself); the headline page at `/` (`csi_dashboard_html.h`) has no sign-in and sends no bearer, and a session is not owner proof. On the flagship it is `auth_gate`. | `build`, `unit`, `counter`, `posture`, `activity` |
| `rom` | A flasher reading the chip in download mode | Chip and allowlisted eFuse fields, the installed image's descriptor and bootloader header, the partitions, the eFuse posture, the MAC on screen behind a reveal only (§5.6; both flashers since PR 1, #1970) |

**The record never carries posture or activity at a LAN tier, not even as one summary word.** Any LAN host can mint a WAP session, and a summary word ("not encrypted") is exactly the difference an attacker wants between two units. An activity count that rises while people are home is a presence sensor by another name.

**Older answers already break that rule on the WAP, and the design says so rather than pretending.** Two different things are true there, and they get two different fixes. Every host build this section reports was run again with g++ on the sources at `0a06c14b`, on 2026-11-16, and gave the figures written here, and none of the code they build changed between `0a06c14b` and `62bc0bc7`; none ran on a device.

- **Posture moves, and `/api/device-info` loses its counter; the chain sequence stays on other open answers (PR 8, P21).** Today a session reads the reset cause word (`/api/system` `reset_reason`, `/api/status` `last_reset` and `last_reset_crash`) and the heap low-water and largest block (`/api/system`, `/api/status` `min_heap`, `/api/diagnostics`). Any LAN host, with no session, reads the SoftAP's `ap_auth` and the `chain_length` counter on the public `/api/device-info`; `ap_auth`, `ap_auth_reason` and `sta_pmf` on the public, CORS-open `/api/wifi`; and, on a build with Bluetooth, the last crash's cause and stage and whether the WAP is in safe mode, in the Bluetooth row's metrics on the public `/api/selftest` (`last_crash_reason`, `last_crash_stage` and `safe_mode`, `selftest_api.h`'s `probe_bluetooth()`), the first two written to NVS at each crash reboot and never removed, so they describe the last crash however long ago (`hardware_state.h`'s `safe_mode_check()`). PR 8 serves every posture value `/api/system`, `/api/status` and `/api/diagnostics` give a session, but `/api/status`'s `safe_mode` (below) and `node_status`'s `creds_at_rest` (§3.6), only on the bearer branch of the audience seam (§3.5); serves `/api/wifi`'s three only on the SoftAP interface or to the bearer; gives the self-test's metrics, these three among them, only on the SoftAP interface (P5); and drops `ap_auth` and `chain_length` from `/api/device-info`, which no page reads. That takes the chain sequence off one open answer, not off the LAN or the air: any LAN host still reads it as `chain_height` on the public, CORS-open `/api/fleet` (`handle_fleet()`, through `fleet_selfreport.h`), which no PR in this plan changes (§5.8), and on a build with Bluetooth any radio in range reads it in the BLE status service and, its low 16 bits, in the fleet beacon (§3.6). The only page that reads the reset cause from a session's three answers is `/admin`'s Reset row (`#sysReset`), which moves into the owner-tier Protection panel. The companion's setup checks read the last crash's cause and stage from `/api/selftest` instead, and show them while the WAP is in safe mode ("Why it went into recovery", `companion_pwa.h`); from PR 8 they get them on the SoftAP interface only. On a standalone (AP-only) WAP that interface is every caller's, so there any session still reads them, and `/api/wifi`'s three, after PR 8 (§3.6). One posture summary was recorded rather than moved: the strip's `status_reason` codes `restarted` and `low_memory` (`status_tier_logic.h`), because the strip, which the headline page at `/` draws (`csi_dashboard_html.h`), is how an owner on the everyday session learns of a crash. They move to the bearer in PR 8 with P20's reads (next item), and a session's strip is then evaluated without the crash flag and the heap floor, so it shows neither reason, nor a tier only they would set. No page shows the two reasons after that: the headline page has no sign-in and sends no bearer, and the page that signs in (`/admin` and `/settings`, `web_ui.h`) does not read `status_reason`. An owner then learns of a crash from the health log's reset line and the tamper word, which a session still reads (below), and from Protection (§3.7) in the apps that hold the bearer, the iPhone app once PR 5 has landed and the desktop Flasher's Details drawer once PR 18 has, and in `/admin` once it has room for the panel (§5.3); whether a signed-in page should draw the two reasons before the panel reaches `/admin` is open, and the maintainer's to decide. Six more things still give a session posture after PR 8. Two of them reach any LAN host too: safe mode, and, while a self-test row's `detail` stays on every interface (open, §12 PR 8), the largest free block a refused Bluetooth start reports. §3.6 records them all:
  - **The health log's reset line.** The health log, which `/api/logs` serves to a session and the Bluetooth log export to every authenticated bond (`READ_AUTHEN` on both readable characteristics of `ble_log_export.cpp`), holds a line from each boot after a panic, a watchdog or a brownout reset ("Last reset: panic (firmware crash)", "Last reset: watchdog timeout", "Last reset: brownout (supply voltage sag)", in `canary_wap.ino`'s `setup()`), written at WARNING or ERROR, which the store setting can never drop (`LOG_LEVEL_STORE_MAX`; the filter is the first line of `log_health()`), and served while it is among the ring's last 100 entries. A host build of the reset lines, verbatim, with a `log_health` that keeps what the sketch's filter passes, stored the panic, all three watchdog causes and the brownout at each of eight store settings from 0 to 4,294,967,295, and nothing for a power-on or a software reset.
  - **The largest free block after a refused Bluetooth start.** When Bluetooth was refused for low memory, `/api/bluetooth`'s `init_fail_reason` and the `detail` of the health log's line about it ("largest block … total free …", which `/api/logs` serves beside the line's `message`, "BLE not started: insufficient heap") give the largest free block at the refusal (`bluetooth_channel.cpp`'s `init()`, where `ble_heap_guard::can_init()` refuses; `bluetooth_api.h`'s `handle_bluetooth_status()`). Today the public `/api/selftest` gives the same reason to any LAN host, in its Bluetooth row's `detail` on a failed start and in that row's `init_fail_reason` metric (`selftest_api.h`'s `probe_bluetooth()`). P5 keeps the metrics for the SoftAP interface (PR 8); whether a row's `detail` goes with them is open (§12 PR 8).
  - **The tamper word.** On every WAP the loop feeds the tamper watcher the boot's reset class (`tamper_events_watch()`, called in `canary_wap.ino`'s `loop()`; the module is registered whatever the build, `csi_integration.cpp`: "Unconditional, like"). After a panic, a watchdog or a brownout it commits one P0 anomaly row, `system.integrity` / `tamper`, which quiet hours do not hold (`csi_event.cpp`: `v.category != CSI_CATEGORY_ANOMALY`), whose state is `unexpected_reboot`, `watchdog` or `power_loss`, and keeps that kind as the boot's standing kind (`tamper_events_module.cpp`'s `tamper_events_watch()` and `tamper_events_active_kind()`). `/api/events/today` serves the row while it is among its 64 newest, and for the whole boot, whenever no SD card or enclosure story stands in front of it, ends its answer with `"tamper":{"kind":…}` (`csi_integration.cpp`'s `handle_events_today()`); `/api/csi/stream` gives the row as its snapshot until another event commits (`handle_stream()`, from the snapshot `csi_event_on_committed()` writes). Both take `CSI_AUTH_OR_RETURN`, which takes the session, and the CSI dashboard shows the row as "Rebooted unexpectedly", "Recovered from a system hang" or "Power was cut" (`csi_dashboard_html.h`). A host build of `tamper_events_module.cpp` against the real chokepoint, bundler and module registry at the default ceiling, P0, fed each reset class as the loop feeds it, gave no row and no standing kind for a power-on or a software reset, and for a panic, the three watchdog causes and a brownout one P0 anomaly row each and the standing kinds `unexpected_reboot`, `watchdog`, `watchdog`, `watchdog` and `power_loss`.
  - **Safe mode.** Three crash resets (a panic, a watchdog or a brownout) with no minute of steady running between them put the WAP in safe mode, where it stays at least the minute before its recovery reboot, and, once its three recovery reboots are spent, until the owner retries (`hardware_state.h`: the `SAFE_MODE_*` constants, `reset_is_crash()`, `safe_mode_check()`, `safe_mode_update()` and `safe_mode_force_retry()`). `/api/status` answers `safe_mode` to a session (`handle_status()`), which the administrator page's safe-mode bar reads (`web_ui.h`'s `refreshStatus()`), and a session's strip, evaluated without the crash flag and the heap floor, still says `action_required` for the reason `safe_mode` (`tin.safe_mode` in `handle_status()`; `status_tier_logic.h`'s `evaluate()`). With no session at all, any LAN host reads it in `/api/selftest`'s top-level `safe_mode` (`selftest_api.h`'s `run_to_json()`), which is not a probe's metric, so PR 8, which keeps full metrics for the SoftAP interface, leaves it on every interface (P5); and in `/api/help-qr`'s QR, a public route that encodes the Help Desk link with `#s-safe-mode` while the WAP is in safe mode (`help_qr_logic.h`'s `compose_help_url()`, which `handle_help_qr()` calls), which the CSI dashboard shows. A host build of `status_tier_logic.h` with a session's inputs as PR 8 sets them gave `good` / `ok` without safe mode and `action_required` / `safe_mode` with it, and `help_qr_logic.h` composed `https://securacv.com/help#s-safe-mode`.
