  - **The key-at-rest word.** `/api/status`'s `node_status` answers a session `creds_at_rest`, the key-at-rest wire label (`node_status.h`), which the WAP sets to `plaintext-nvs` (`handle_status()`), so it also says that NVS is not encrypted. PR 8 leaves it with the session while every shipped build reports that word (§3.6, §14, P16).
  - **The flash-encryption lines.** On a FULL build (`build_config.h`'s default profile, the one that compiles the mesh) with flash encryption off, the health log, which `/api/logs` serves to a session and the log export to every authenticated bond, carries "opera: refused to load — flash encryption disabled (audit O2)", which `mesh_network.cpp`'s `load_opera_config()` writes at ALERT, a level no store setting drops, at each boot that starts the mesh (`init()`, which `setup()` calls outside safe mode), and "opera: refused to persist secret — flash encryption disabled (audit O2)", which `persist_opera_config()` writes at ALERT each time the mesh is to be stored: when a session turns it on or off, renames it, leaves it or founds one by starting a pairing (`POST /api/mesh/enable`, `/api/mesh/name`, `/api/mesh/leave` and `/api/mesh/pair/start`, each behind `api_auth_check`). So a session can bring the second line back on demand after the first has left the ring's last 100 entries. Both fit the entry's 80-byte `message` (63 and 73 bytes). Neither is written while flash encryption is on, so a session that stores the mesh and then reads the log learns either answer.

  So after PR 8 a session still learns whether the last restart was a crash, a watchdog or a brownout, and any LAN host whether three came in a row: the move takes the other reset causes, the strip's two words and the heap's and PSRAM's low-water marks off a session's answers, not the crash, nor the largest block a refused Bluetooth start reports, and `held` counts neither the reset cause nor the largest free block for a session (below). Whether PR 8 also takes the crash off these answers is open (§14). `tls_enabled` on `/api/device-info` stays too: anyone can learn it by connecting to the port, and the sign-in footer shows it.
- **The session's counters and activity were recorded, not moved, until the owner-authority PR; PR 8 moves P20's reads, and what a session reads elsewhere stays (P20).** A WAP session is the WAP's dashboard. It reads the health log, the witness records, the chain and the live view themselves, all behind `api_auth_check` (the live view's stream and snapshot behind `api_auth_check_or_query`, which takes the session too): `/api/logs` serves the health log, whose entries carry `timestamp_ms`, the milliseconds since boot (`log_health()` stamps it, `handle_logs()` serves it); `/api/witness` and `/api/v1/witness` serve the witness records, which carry the chain's uptime bucket (`time_bucket`) and, on `/api/v1/witness` and only under a believable clock, a ten-minute `timestamp` bucket; no witness record carries `timestamp_ms` (`handle_witness()`, `handle_witness_v1()`), and the v1 page's header carries `uptime_s` (`witness_page.h`'s `header_build()`); and `/api/chain` and the peek routes serve the chain and the live view. So `witness_count`, `chain_seq`, `peek_active` and uptime in seconds summarize what that session already reads in full, and hiding them would protect nothing; `boot_count` and SD lifetime writes it reads nowhere else, and the egress counters only on a broker it points the MQTT bridge at (§3.6). The decision (§14) kept all seven on the session until owner sign-in, to move behind the owner predicate in the owner-authority PR. That PR landed as #1887 and moved none of them: at `62bc0bc7` `/api/status`, `/api/system` and `/api/diagnostics` still take `api_auth_check()`, the session or the bearer. The administrator page's sign-in gives that page the bearer, the WAP's owner predicate (the owner row above). So PR 8 serves them on the audience seam's bearer branch (§3.5): `/api/status`'s `boot_count`, `witness_count`, `chain_seq`, `peek_active` and top-level `uptime_sec`, `/api/system`'s uptime, and `/api/diagnostics`'s SD writes and egress counters. The health log, the witness records, the chain and the live view stay on the session, as #1887 left them, and no PR in this plan moves them, so a session still reads several of these facts elsewhere, behind the same `api_auth_check`: the witness count as `/api/witness`'s `total` and `/api/export`'s `records_total`; the chain's sequence in `/api/witness`'s record, as `/api/v1/witness`'s `total`, `/api/chain`'s `sequence` and `/api/export`'s `chain_seq`; the live view's state on `/api/peek/status`; and the uptime to the second. Each health-log entry carries its `timestamp_ms`; the v1 witness page's header carries `uptime_s`, by which its readers age the records' `time_bucket` when the device has met no clock, so it cannot simply go; `/api/export` answers `export_time_ms`, the milliseconds since boot, and names its card copy by them; `/api/diagnostics`, `/api/battery/history` and `/api/rf/status` answer `uptime_sec`; and `/api/status`'s `node_status` answers `uptime_s`, in whole hours from PR 11 (P9). A host build of `witness_page.h`, `wap_diagnostics.h` and `node_status.h`, at an uptime of 93,784 s, gave `"uptime_s":93784` in the witness page's header and in `node_status` and `"uptime_sec":93784` in the diagnostics body. So any LAN host that mints a session dates the WAP's boot to the second, as P9 says of the open answers (§11), and no PR in this plan changes that. The move takes the boot count and the SD writes off a session's answers, the egress counters off its HTTP answers but not off a broker it points the MQTT bridge at (§3.6), and the other summaries off `/api/status`'s top level and `/api/system` only; it hides little more, and `held` counts none of what a session still reads as withheld (below). The WAP's Wi-Fi signal moves with them, from the four answers that give it a session whatever the settings: `/api/wifi`'s `rssi` in dBm, which the administrator page's Wi-Fi bars read (`web_ui.h`'s `loadWifiStatus()`); the `rssi` of each network in `/api/wifi/scan`'s answer, which lists the first 20 networks a sweep reports, strongest first (`SCAN_CACHE_MAX`, `wifi_scan_cache_fill()`, `scan_cache_json()`), the home network when it is among them; `node_status`'s `rssi` in `/api/status` (the same host build, given -61 dBm, wrote `"rssi":-61`), which PR 11 drops from `node_status.h` for every family (P23); and `/api/selftest`'s Wi-Fi row, which carries it in its `rssi_dbm` metric, as its `code` and in its `detail` ("Joined … · …dBm"), on every interface (`selftest_api.h`'s `probe_wifi()`, written out by `run_to_json()`; P5). PR 8 gives the signal on the two Wi-Fi routes only on the SoftAP interface or to the bearer (§11, P4), gives the self-test's metrics only on the SoftAP interface (P5), and builds the WAP's `node_status` for a session with `rssi` 0, which `node_status.h` omits, because the signal moves with the room (§3.1). That leaves the signal on three lan-tier answers no PR in this plan moves, and, unless PR 8 also takes the self-test's Wi-Fi row's `code` and `detail` off the other interfaces, which is open (§12 PR 8), on that row for any LAN host; §3.6 records both, so `held` does not count it at the WAP's lan tier. `/api/csi/window` answers a session, once the Wi-Fi sensing's privacy ceiling is P2, the last window's raw feature vector (`handle_window()` in `csi_integration.cpp`), whose `v[20..23]` are the window's RSSI mean, spread, highest and lowest in dBm (`csi_features.cpp`, under "Lay out the 32-dim vector"), over the frames the sensing keeps, which by default, once the WAP knows its router's address, are the router's and a registered peer Canary's alone (`csi_hal.cpp`'s `transmitter_accepted()`); a host build of `csi_features.cpp` fed six frames at -58 to -64 dBm gave -61, 1, -58 and -64 there. Its `v[8..19]` are the window's motion and breathing bins, readings of the room (§3.1). And a phone bonded to the WAP, which the record holds at the lan tier (§5.3), reads the bonded console snapshot's `wrssi`, the signal in dBm (`ble_console.cpp`'s `build_snapshot()`), which the companion page shows as "(… dBm)" (`companion_pwa.h`, its `s-wifi` line), and BLE provisioning's scan list, with the `rssi` of each network it lists (the strongest of a sweep, 20 at most and fewer when they do not fit the payload, the home network's when it is among them), which a bond can ask for at any time (`ble_provision.cpp`'s `scan_list_json()` and `publish_scan_results()`). Today any session raises the ceiling: `POST /api/settings` takes `{"privacy_ceiling":"p2"}` from the same session (`handle_settings_post()` opens with `CSI_AUTH_OR_RETURN`, which takes `cv_session_validate`) and applies it at once (`store_privacy_ceiling_from_settings()`, then `apply_privacy_ceiling_from_nvs()`), and a raise to P1 or above also lets the breathing module's `breathing_confirmed` event, with its breathing rate, through the chokepoint (`core_breathing.cpp` declares it `CSI_PRIVACY_P1`; `csi_event.cpp`: `decl->privacy > g_privacy_ceiling`), which a session then reads as `bpm` in `/api/csi/stream`'s snapshot and in `/api/events/today`'s rows (`handle_stream()`, `handle_events_today()`). A raise is stored (`cp.pc`, `csi_settings_nvs.cpp`) and applied again at every boot (`csi_integration.cpp`'s `init()` calls `apply_privacy_ceiling_from_nvs()`), so an update keeps it, and nothing tells an owner's raise from any LAN host's. PR 8 takes a raise to the bearer and leaves a session free to lower the ceiling, and its first boot lowers a stored ceiling above P0 to P0, once, so from PR 8 a session reads the window only while the owner holds the ceiling at P2 (§12 PR 8). The breathing rate is not held as tightly. `/api/events/today` drops a row above the ceiling in force when it is read, but `/api/csi/stream` checks the ceiling only when an event commits (`csi_event_on_committed()`), and lowering it clears nothing (`csi_event_set_privacy_ceiling()` only stores the new value). So after the ceiling comes down from P1 the last `breathing_confirmed` snapshot, its `bpm` in it, stays on the stream until another event commits: from PR 8 a session reads a breathing rate in the events only while the owner holds the ceiling at P1 or above, and on the stream until the first event after it is lowered. Whether PR 8 also clears the snapshot when the ceiling is lowered is open, and the maintainer's to decide. The same `POST /api/settings` lets a session turn off the foreign-frame filter (`filter_foreign`, in the same `handle_settings_post()`), which PR 8 leaves with the session: with it off every transmitter's frames pass (`transmitter_accepted()`), so the window's RSSI and bins cover every device in range, not the router's and its peers' alone. The mesh's answers on FULL builds move with them too. They take the same `api_auth_check` (`handle_mesh_status_auth()` and `handle_mesh_peers_auth()`): `/api/mesh` carries the mesh's id (`opera_id`, the 16-byte id every member of the household mesh shares, in hex, which changes only when the mesh is rekeyed or formed anew) and its name, its uptime in milliseconds and its message, alert and auth-failure counters (`handle_mesh_status()`), and `/api/mesh/peers` each peer's name, key fingerprint, state, signal, alert count and seconds since it was last heard (`handle_mesh_peers()`). `/api/mesh/alerts` stays on the session, which the administrator page's alerts panel reads (`web_ui.h`'s `loadOperaAlerts()`): it lists the alerts the WAP holds, up to 32, each with the name of the member that sent it, the same name `/api/mesh/peers` gives, the WAP's milliseconds since boot when it arrived (`timestamp_ms`) and, for a tamper alert or an offline notice, that member's chain sequence (`witness_seq`) (`handle_mesh_alerts()`, behind `api_auth_check` in `handle_mesh_alerts_auth()`; `mesh_network.cpp` fills each stored `MeshAlert`), and each alert's `detail`: for a power alert the member's supply voltage in mV and estimated runtime in seconds, for an offline notice its final sequence and the first 8 bytes of its chain head in hex, and for a tamper alert the text the member sent (`mesh_network.cpp`: "Voltage: %umV, Runtime: %us", "Final seq: %u, hash: %s"). The WAP also writes each alert to the health log as it arrives, the member's name and the `detail` in one line (`"From %s: %s"`, of which the log's ring keeps the first 47 bytes), where a session reads it too. So a session still reads the name of each member whose alert the WAP holds, and what the alert says. The health log carries more of the mesh than its alerts, to a session on `/api/logs` and to a bond through the log export: each member's name at every change of its state ("Peer state changed", detail `<name>: <old> -> <new>`, written at INFO, which the default store setting keeps), the mesh's new name after a rename ("Opera name changed", INFO), and a removed member's key fingerprint, 16 hex digits ("Peer removed", written at WARNING, which no store setting drops), each with its `timestamp_ms`. So after PR 8 a session still reads every member's name and when its state changed, the mesh's name once it has been renamed, and a removed member's fingerprint; no PR in this plan moves the health log, and §3.6 records these lines. The id is no secret from the radio, though: on FULL builds every opera frame the WAP sends carries it in the clear, beside the whole key fingerprint and the seconds since boot, for any radio in range to read (§7.2 list 1), so moving `/api/mesh` behind owner proof takes it off the LAN answers, not off the air (§14). The name is on the air too until PR 8, in the OFFER the WAP sends, while it is the initiator of a pairing, to every radio that asks for one (§7.2 list 1). The confirmation code `/api/mesh` also answers during a mesh pairing is not one of these reads, and it stays on the session: F346 (decided, #1887) found that both ends compute it, as they do a Bluetooth Numeric Comparison's digits, and that the step that grants is the confirm, which takes the bearer (§7.2 list 2).

**So `held` never claims a fact is withheld from a tier that can read it elsewhere on that family.** It counts only facts no recorded legacy answer gives that tier, judged per fact through each fact row's `legacy` mappings (§2.2, §3.6, §4.1). On the WAP after PR 8 the lan tier's held count, which a session and a bond share (§5.3), is its posture facts other than the reset cause, the largest free block, SoftAP auth and PMF, key at rest and NVS encryption, flash encryption on a FULL build and, on a build with Bluetooth, the last crash's cause and stage; the boot count and the SD card's lifetime writes; and any other counter or activity fact no legacy answer gives that tier. It counts nothing a session or a bond still reads in another answer (§3.6 records those producers): not the witness count, the chain's sequence, the live view's state or the uptime (above), not the reset cause or the largest free block, which the health log, the tamper word and `/api/bluetooth` give a session (above), not key at rest or NVS encryption, which `node_status`'s `creds_at_rest` gives a session on `/api/status` (above), not, on a FULL build, flash encryption, which the health log's flash-encryption lines give a session and a bond (above), not SoftAP auth and PMF or, on a build with Bluetooth, the last crash's cause and stage, which `/api/wifi` and the self-test's metrics still give every caller on the SoftAP interface after PR 8 (P4, P5), and so every session on a standalone (AP-only) WAP, whose SoftAP is its only network (`g_wifi_ap_only`), not the event-egress counters, which a session reads on a broker it points the MQTT bridge at (§3.6), not the Wi-Fi signal, which the raw sensing window gives a session at P2, the console snapshot and BLE provisioning's scan list give a bond, and the self-test's Wi-Fi row gives every caller while its `code` and `detail` stay on every interface (above), and not the counters no PR in this plan moves, the camera's on `/api/peek/status` (`init_attempts`, `last_init_err` and its name, `fps`, `frame_count`), the microphone's on `/api/audio/status` (`frames_processed`, `i2s_read_errors`, `mic_silent`), the Wi-Fi sensing's in `/api/status`'s `csi` object (`frames_received`, the drops by cause, the window counts) and its frame rate in `/api/csi/stream`'s `supply.fps` at any ceiling (`handle_stream()`), and the Bluetooth presence scan's on `/api/presence` (`sensor.adverts_seen`, `adverts_resolved_household` and `pause_count`, `household_api.h`'s `handle_presence_status()`, behind `api_auth_check` through its `auth_gated` wrapper, registered by `household_api::register_routes()` in every profile that serves HTTP). The record's own uptime is whole hours, a `unit` fact the lan tier carries anyway (§3.1).

### 3.3 The policy, in one place

The table below is generated into the writer. A cell marked *held* means the writer omits the fact. The part's `held` field is not counted from what the writer omitted; it is a generated constant (§4.1), so it cannot vary with posture state. A held fact counts toward it only where no recorded legacy answer gives that fact to that tier on that family (§3.2).

| Class \ tier | lan | broker | serial | glass | owner | Copy Details (§6.4) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| build | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| unit | yes | about part only | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| counter | held | held | yes | yes | yes | no |
| posture | held | held | yes | yes | yes | no |
| activity | held | held | yes | yes | yes | no |
| household, handle, reading | no key | no key | no key | no key | no key | no |
| identifier, secret | never read | — | — | — | — | — |

The forms are coarse at every tier, so the writer never needs a per-tier rounding rule:

- Uptime is whole hours from a per-boot random offset (§4.1), temperature is whole °C, and memory is KiB.
- Signal is a word ("Strong", "Good", "Weak") at the tiers that carry it, serial, glass and owner (it is activity, §3.1); dBm stays out of the record.
- The build date is a day.

### 3.4 Gates per family: the real functions

| Family | lan | owner | serial | glass | broker | Phrase for a held fact |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| canary (flagship) | none (no session on this tree) | `auth_gate()` (`firmware/canary/lib/securacv_network/src/securacv_network.cpp`: Host first, then bearer) + `rate_limit_check()` | console `q` (new in PR 10), `Tier::Diag` | — | MQTT | "Shown to the app paired with this Canary's key" |
| canary-wap | `api_auth_audience()` **(new in PR 8)**: session; `/api/details` asks the Host first (§3.5) | `api_auth_audience()`: one accounted bearer grant, which the administrator page sends once signed in with the token (§3.2) | console `j` and `q` (new keys) | — | MQTT | "Shown to the app paired with this Canary's key, or to this page signed in with it", for the facts a session cannot read anywhere; a fact a session still reads in another answer (a count, the uptime, the reset cause, the key-at-rest word, the Wi-Fi signal, an activity counter) is not counted as held (§3.2) |
| canary-display | `GET /api/details` (new in PR 11): refuses a foreign Host (`host_guard.h`'s `host_names_this_device()`, as `/api/fleet`'s `host_is_foreign()` asks it) and a cross-site Origin first, sends no CORS header | none exists today | console `q` (new), read in `glass_web_tick()`, so answered whenever no setup wizard is up: not while `setup()` has the wizard open (a boot with no network saved: the first one, or the one after Settings › Forget Wi-Fi), nor while `loop()` has it open; from PR 17 during both (§5.4) | Settings › About on the glass (new) | MQTT | "Shown on this Canary's own screen" |
| canary-sense, -vision, -sentinel | `GET /api/details` (new in PR 11) in `node_http.cpp`, with the same Host and Origin refusal and no CORS header; `node_http.cpp` reads neither header today | none exists today | console `j` and `q` (new; Vision adds `q` to its `j`/`h`) | — | MQTT | "Read with the Flasher" |

### 3.5 The WAP audience seam

`api_auth_audience(req, token)` **(new in PR 8, `api_auth.h`)** is one function. Its decision is a pure function, `audience_decide()` **(new)** in `auth_logic.h`, host-tested in `firmware/projects/canary-wap/tests_host/test_auth_logic.cpp`, which exists today and gains its cases. It runs in this order:

1. **One accounted grant.** `api_auth_bearer_result()` (`api_auth.h`) makes one compare and one lockout read per request (sweep F339). It reads the header through `api_auth_bearer_valid()`, which asks for no seal's reason, so as it stands a `Sealed` header the WAP cannot open (it rebooted, the seal's session aged out, or its counter was replayed) comes back `WRONG`. The seam keeps the answer `api_auth_check_bearer()` gives such a header today, which the desktop Flasher's sealed `GET /api/status` depends on (`fleet.rs`'s `action_route()`; `seal.rs`'s `on_refused()` opens a new session only on a 401 whose `seal` word is `no_session`, `expired` or `replay`): only a seal that opens is compared. `audience_decide()` takes the seal's word as an input, and `test_auth_logic.cpp` gains a case for each word.

   | Result | Answer |
   |---|---|
   | `OK` | owner |
   | `HELD` | 429 with `Retry-After` |
   | a `Sealed` header that does not open | 401 with its `seal` word, as today; a strike for every word but `no_session` and `expired` |
   | `WRONG` | 403 |
   | `NONE` | go on to step 2 |
2. **A valid session cookie (`cv_session_validate`) means lan.** A cookie never resets the backoff.
3. **Anything else is the open caller.** `/api/wifi` and `/api/wifi/scan` answer it today, the setup wizard included (`companion_pwa.h` fetches `/api/wifi` with no credential, and `handle_ui()` mints no session during setup), so on those two it gets their open answer, the full one on the SoftAP interface and words elsewhere (§12 PR 8). Every other route the seam gates answers it 401.

**The Host question, and why the seam does not ask it.** #1848 (F344, F337) merged at `7ed3a0f8`. Since then the sketch stages `host_guard.h` (`#include "host_guard.h"` in `canary_wap.ino`), one of the thirteen `firmware/common/network` headers it stages; `check_improv_sync.sh` holds five of them, `host_guard.h` by its own `PAIRS` line, and names the scripts that hold the other eight (`HELD_ELSEWHERE`). The WAP asks `host_names_this_device()` through three functions:

- `request_host_names_device()` (`canary_wap.ino`): after setup the pages (`/`, `/admin`, `/settings`, `/companion`) answer a foreign Host with a fixed 403 page, and `/` asks it ahead of the cookie and the `?cv_pair=` exchange (`handle_ui()`); the provisioning receipt asks it first in every mode, before it reads a bearer, takes the claim or judges the tap (`handle_provisioning_receipt()`).
- `session_validate_cookie()` (`csi_integration.cpp`): every cookie gate treats a missing or foreign Host as no session, `api_auth_check()`'s cookie branch included, which reaches it through `cv_session_validate`.
- `pair_link_authority()` (`csi_integration.cpp`, since the 2.4.20 train, #1972) grants nothing: it decides whether the pair landing's paste-this link (`send_pair_landing()`) and `GET /api/pair/token`'s `pair_url` (`handle_pair_token()`) name the request's Host or leave the address out. Before #1972 both named `192.168.4.1`.

Nothing else asks it. `api_auth.h` reads no Host, so a bearer outside the receipt is answered under any Host (F57's open question), and the public routes, `/api/wifi`, `/api/wifi/scan` and `/api/selftest` among them, ask nothing (#1848's F357). Two more places read a Host for another purpose: `request_host_is_setup_name()`, which gates `handle_ui()`'s first-boot branch, the one that mints a pair token and redirects to the wizard, and the wizard's pair-token re-issue, `handle_wifi_pair_token()`; and `handle_https_redirect()`. So:

- **PR 8** puts the seam on the legacy routes it tiers (`/api/status`, `/api/system`, `/api/diagnostics` and `/api/wifi` among them; §12 PR 8 has the list) and leaves their Host behavior as #1848 left it: the seam's lan branch is the cookie, which already refuses a foreign Host, its owner branch is the bearer, which does not, and the open answer on the two Wi-Fi routes asks nothing, as today.
- **PR 12** reuses the staged copy and its `PAIRS` line and adds no second copy (decided, §14). `/api/details` refuses (403) a request whose Host fails `host_names_this_device()` before the seam reads any grant, so on that route the bearer is behind the Host question too, and a refused request is never an asker for the lockout. `test_host_guard.cpp` already covers the rule; the WAP test adds the order (Host, then grant).

This avoids a trap the shortcut falls into. `api_auth_bearer_grant(...) ? Owner : api_auth_check(...) ? Lan : refused` asks about one bearer **twice**. When a wrong bearer arrives without a cookie, the grant makes the one compare and strikes the address, and `api_auth_check()` then falls through to `api_auth_check_bearer()`, whose lockout read finds the backoff that strike just armed: the request is answered 429 where the seam answers 403, on a second lockout read. When a wrong bearer arrives with a valid cookie, the shortcut strikes the address and then serves the request at the lan tier, where the seam refuses it. (A host run of `auth_backoff.h`'s `grant()` and then `locked()` on one address, the second in the same millisecond: one compare, then held for 2,000 ms.)

`check_route_security.py` learns three things:

- the seam as a gate (PR 8);
- that `Tier::Owner` may appear only in the `OK` branch of `api_auth_bearer_result()`, with planted mutants that must fail (PR 8);
- that the details handler asks the Host first and is in no `SECRET_ANSWERS` entry, because it answers no secret (PR 12).

### 3.6 Legacy answers: recorded, frozen, and allowed to shrink

The block's `legacy` list (planned, PR 3) names every existing answer that carries device facts, with the keys each may carry, **the tier each key reaches**, and the class of each. The eleven `keys` rows #1961 built already record some of the same keys, and how the two lists meet is open (§2.2). `lint_device_details.py` **(new)** parses each producer. Where `check_wap_json_answers.py` already reads a construct, the parser is lifted from it: a `snprintf` format's keys (`printf_calls()`, `formats_of()`), ArduinoJson `doc["…"]` assignments inside one function's span (`bound_document()`) and a struct's fields (`parse_fields()`). That script has no parser for `wap_json::` writer calls (its rule J6 holds the `identity_json.h` answers to their measured builder instead), for NimBLE `createCharacteristic` UUIDs, for a binary frame's layout or for an `MDNS.addServiceTxt()` call's key, so those four are written new. The producers are:

- WAP: `sys_monitor.h get_json`; `handle_status`'s ArduinoJson body (`canary_wap.ino`); `wap_diagnostics.h build_json`; `handle_wifi_status` and `handle_wifi_scan`; `handle_mesh_status` and `handle_mesh_peers` (FULL profiles); `selftest_api.h` (its probes' metrics, `uptime_ms`, `total_bytes`, `free_bytes`, `muted`, `soc_pct`, `charge_state` and `init_fail_reason` among them, §7.2 list 2; `ap_clients` is written only before the join, when no home-network host reaches the WAP, which reads the count in `handle_wifi_status`'s `ap_clients`); `bluetooth_api.h`; `household_api.h`'s `handle_list` (each enrolled phone's `label`, `role` and `last_seen_ago_ms`) and `handle_presence_status`'s context, override and owner keys (`auto_context`, `effective_context`, `override_active`, `override_ms_remaining`, `owner_seen_recently`, `ms_since_any_owner`), household facts a session reads today and after every PR in this plan; `identity_json.h write_device_info`; the BLE status service's characteristics (`ble_status_api.h`); the bonded console snapshot (`ble_console.cpp`'s `build_snapshot()`) and BLE provisioning's scan list (`ble_provision.cpp`'s `scan_list_json()`), which a bond reads at the lan tier (§5.3); and, from PR 8, the answers that still give a session what PR 8 moves elsewhere, or an activity count (§3.2): `handle_witness`'s `total` and records, `witness_page.h`'s header (`total`, `uptime_s`), `handle_chain`'s `sequence`, `handle_export`'s `chain_seq`, `records_total` and `export_time_ms`, `handle_logs`'s `timestamp_ms`, `message` (the reset line and, on FULL builds, the flash-encryption lines) and `detail` (a refused Bluetooth start's largest block, a mesh alert's text, a member's name and state change, the mesh's new name, a removed member's key fingerprint) and the Bluetooth log export's records, which carry the same entries to an authenticated bond (`ble_log_export.cpp`), the witness export's head and record, which carry the chain sequence, and the head the witness count, to one (`ble_witness_export.cpp`), `handle_mesh_alerts`'s `timestamp_ms`, `sender_name`, `detail` and `witness_seq` (FULL profiles), `handle_window`'s `v` at P2, `handle_events_today`'s rows (`state`, the tamper word among them, and `bpm` at P1 or above) and its `tamper` envelope's `kind`, `handle_stream`'s snapshot (the same keys) and `supply.fps`, `household_api.h`'s `handle_presence_status` `sensor` counters, `bluetooth_api.h`'s `init_fail_reason`, `handle_battery_history`'s and `rf_presence_api.h`'s `handle_rf_status`'s `uptime_sec`, `handle_peek_status`'s `peek_active` and camera counters, and `handle_audio_status`'s microphone counters, beside `handle_status`'s `csi` counters, `safe_mode` and strip reason `safe_mode`, `node_status`'s `uptime_s` and `wap_diagnostics.h`'s `uptime_sec` in the producers above; and, at the open tier, `selftest_api.h`'s `safe_mode`, its Wi-Fi row's `code` and `detail` and its Bluetooth row's `detail` while they stay on every interface (§12 PR 8), and the anchors `help_qr_logic.h` puts in `/api/help-qr`'s QR;
- display: `glass_web.cpp handle_device`, `handle_log` (the lines `log_line()` mirrors to `/api/log`), `handle_glass` (the mirror's `/api/glass`) and `handle_settings_get` (`/api/settings`);
- Sense, Vision and Sentinel: the peer heartbeat they multicast to the network (`peer_protocol.h`'s `Heartbeat` fields, which `put_heartbeat()` writes, and the frame header that `peer_link.cpp`'s `stamp()` fills);
- every family, at the open tier: the `/api/fleet` body that `fleet_selfreport.h` builds (its `chain_height` is the chain sequence on the WAP and the flagship and the chain length on the trio; the display sends none) and the `_securacv._tcp` TXT keys, the trio's and the flagship's from `mdns_txt.h`'s table, the WAP's from `canary_wap.ino`'s `MDNS.addServiceTxt()` calls and the display's from `discovery.cpp`'s;
- every family, the `node_status.h` keys: at the open tier on the displays and the trio (`handle_status()` in `glass_web.cpp` and `node_http.cpp`), to a session on the WAP (in `handle_status`'s body, above) and to the bearer on the flagship (`auth_gate()`);
- every family, at the broker tier, and on the WAP and the display at their lan tiers too (the MQTT exception below says why): the MQTT bodies that carry the facts the exceptions below name. Read at `62bc0bc7`, their producers are `mqtt_mgr.cpp`'s `publish_status_retained()` and `publish_health_retained()` on the display and the trio, and the trio's `publish_heartbeat()`, `publish_state_retained()` and `publish_chain_retained()`; `csi_mqtt.cpp`'s `publish_status()`, `publish_health()`, `publish_chain()`, `publish_counts()`, `publish_egress()`, `publish_mesh()` and `publish_sensing()` (whose body `canary_wap.ino`'s `loop()` builds) on the WAP; and `mqtt_publish_status_update()`, `mqtt_publish_health_update()` and `mqtt_publish_sensing_update()` in the flagship's `main.cpp`;
- the flagship diagnostics answer.

It fails on any key not on its list, and on a key that reaches a tier its row does not say. The `held` generator reads the same rows (§4.1). **A new fact goes through the details record, never into an old answer.** A parser that cannot find its construct fails loudly, as `lint_dictionary_sync.py`'s do.

The display's `/api/device` list is recorded as it is today, `signal` and `caps` included (`glass_web.cpp`'s `handle_device()`), and at the open tier, because `handle_device()` asks neither the Host nor the Origin (P10); so is `/api/log` (`handle_log()`, P22). PR 11 adds the refusal to both, which moves their entries to the lan tier, and removes `ssid`, `signal`, `heap_min_kb` and `up_s` from `/api/device` (P10, P9); the list shrinks with it.

The recorded exceptions, each with its finding:

- the WAP session's counters and activity that P20 names, the mesh's included, the WAP's Wi-Fi signal and the strip's `restarted` and `low_memory` reasons, recorded only until PR 8, which moves them to the bearer; and, after it too, what a session still reads in the answers no PR in this plan moves: the witness count, the chain's sequence, the live view's state, the uptime to the second, the camera's, the microphone's, the Wi-Fi sensing's and the Bluetooth presence scan's counters and the sensing's frame rate, the reset cause in the health log's reset line and in the tamper word on `/api/events/today` and `/api/csi/stream`, safe mode in `/api/status`'s `safe_mode` and the strip's `safe_mode` reason, the largest free block in a refused Bluetooth start's reason and its log line's detail, and, on FULL builds, whether flash encryption is off in the health log's flash-encryption lines (P21), the Wi-Fi signal in the raw sensing window while the privacy ceiling is P2, the breathing rate in the events while it is P1 or above and on `/api/csi/stream` until the first event after it is lowered, and, on FULL builds, the name of each member whose mesh alert the WAP holds, what the alert says, that member's chain sequence and when the alert arrived, and, in the health log, each member's name at every change of its state, the mesh's name after a rename and a removed member's key fingerprint (§3.2, P20);
- safe mode at the open tier, in `/api/selftest`'s `safe_mode` and `/api/help-qr`'s QR, which any LAN host reads after PR 8 too, until the maintainer decides (§3.2, §14, P21);
- the self-test's Wi-Fi row's `code` and `detail`, which give the Wi-Fi signal in dBm, and its Bluetooth row's `detail`, which gives the largest free block after a start refused for low memory, at the open tier, until the maintainer decides whether PR 8 takes them off the interfaces other than the SoftAP's (§3.2, §12 PR 8, P5);
- an authenticated bond's reads at the lan tier (§5.3; every one of these characteristics takes `READ_AUTHEN`): the console snapshot's uptime in seconds and Wi-Fi signal in dBm (`up`, `wrssi`) and, beside them, a reading and household facts the record never carries (the Wi-Fi sensing's motion score, `motion`; the minutes since an owner's phone was last seen, `owner_min`; the home, away, quiet or traveling context, `ctx`; the household roster's size, `hh`) and the Bluetooth advert counters (`ble`, `ble_hh`), which rise with phones nearby and have no fact in the record (`ble_console.cpp`'s `build_snapshot()`); BLE provisioning's scan list, with the signal of each network it lists, the home network's when it is among them; the log export, the whole health log with its reset line, its mesh lines, its flash-encryption lines and each entry's milliseconds since boot (`ble_log_export.cpp`'s `emit_record_at_index()`); and the witness export's head, with the chain sequence and the witness count (`s`, `t`), and its latest record, with the chain sequence (`ble_witness_export.cpp`, built by `canary_wap.ino`'s `ble_witness_get_head_json()` and `ble_witness_get_record_json()`);
- the MQTT bodies at the broker tier: the Wi-Fi signal in dBm (`rssi`, in the status of the WAP, the display and the trio), the uptime in seconds (`uptime`, every family) and status's `ts_ms`, the milliseconds since boot (the display and the trio), the heap's low-water mark (`heap_min`, the display and the trio; `min_heap` in the flagship's health), Sense's and Sentinel's radar link (`link_ok`, `last_frame_age_ms`, `frame_errors`), the display's and the flagship's `unexpected_reboot` and `power_loss_detected` flags, the trio's state body (`uptime_s`, `ts_ms`, Sense's `frame_errors`, and the chain length and the count of signed state publishes since boot that Sense's signed envelope carries, `chain_length` and `seq`) and chain length (`length`), the flagship's counters (status's `seq` and `records_created`; health's `boot_count`, `records_created`, `records_verified`, `verify_failures`, `sd_writes`, `sd_errors`, `chain_persists`, `chain_persist_failures`, `http_requests`, `charge_cycles` and `thermal_pause_events`, and the counts in its `sd`, `offline_queue` and `csi_event_egress` objects, among them), the microphone's counters (the WAP's sensing body's `i2s_read_errors`, on a build with acoustic events; the flagship's sensing body's `acoustic_stats`, `frames_processed` and `i2s_read_errors` among them, on a build with acoustic transients), and the WAP's chain sequence (`chain`'s `length`), witness count (`counts`'s `total`), event-egress counters (`egress`) and mesh airtime counters (`mesh`). They stay: the broker is the household's, and Home Assistant shows the signal, the radar link and the unexpected reboot (the `rssi` entity in `canary-sense/src/ha/ha_discovery.cpp`; `custom_components/securacv/sensor.py`'s `SecuraCVCanaryRadarLinkSensor`; `binary_sensor.py`'s read of `unexpected_reboot`), as the display's Roll Call shows the signal (`fleet.on_rssi()` in `canary-display/src/net/mqtt_mgr.cpp`). The record adds none of them at the broker tier, and `held` does not count them there. Who reaches that broker is not the same on every family. On the flagship and the trio no LAN request moves it: the flagship's `POST /api/mqtt/config` takes `auth_gate()`, and the trio serves no route that sets one. A display that holds no broker credentials adopts the broker the LAN refers it to, from a `_securacv._tcp` advert's `broker` key or an `_mqtt._tcp` advert, while it has none and each time its own has been down past its rediscovery deadline (`discovery.cpp`'s `discovery_find_broker()`, which refuses every referral once the display holds credentials). On the WAP any dashboard session can point the MQTT bridge at a broker of its own: `POST /api/mqtt/config` takes the session, and `move_needs_password()` lets any move through whose body carries a password (`csi_mqtt.cpp`'s `handle_config_post()`; `docs/security/SECURITY_MODEL.md` speaks of a broker a session "re-pointed the MQTT bridge at"). So on the WAP these bodies reach any session, and on a display without broker credentials any LAN host that advertises a broker, and `held` counts none of their facts at those lan tiers, the WAP's event-egress counters included (§3.2); whether PR 8 takes a broker move on the WAP to the bearer is open, and the maintainer's to decide;
- the trio's peer heartbeat: the key fingerprint, which keys every receiver's peer table (a handle, §3.1), and its role, whether it is on its hub, the fingerprint of the member it takes as coordinator, the onboarding, hub and update words, the capability bits and the peer and saved-network counts, which `node_status` and the TXT keys already give the network. Its uptime in seconds, its frame counter (`seq`, which restarts at each boot and counts every frame the unit sends, heartbeats and events, so it dates the boot as the uptime does) and its signal in dBm are recorded only until PR 11, which coarsens the first, starts the second at a random value each boot and zeroes the third (P9, P23, §5.5); how the random start and the coarse uptime sit beside #1932's reboot window is open (§5.5, §14). Since #1932 the header also carries `boot_id`, a random non-zero byte drawn once per boot, by which a receiver tells a new boot from a replay: it changes at each restart and carries no time, and it stays;
- the WAP's opera frames on FULL builds, which any radio in range reads (§7.2 list 1): the opera id and the whole key fingerprint, by which every receiver checks membership and finds the sender, the heartbeat's member count, BLE Scout's arrived and departed events with the label the owner gave the tag, and, while the WAP is pairing (any dashboard session can start a pairing), the keys the unsigned pairing frames carry for the pairing to read (the joiner's public key and role, the initiator's device key in the OFFER it sends any radio that asks, and the two ephemeral keys) and with them the device id, which follows from the key fingerprint (§7.2 list 1), until the maintainer decides whether the frames are encrypted (§14); and the header's seconds since boot, its send counter, whose place in its 1,024-frame reservation step counts the frames since the boot, the heartbeat's seconds since the mesh started, the five padding bytes its struct leaves unset, what the pairing frames carry that no canary-wap receiver reads (the name fields, which spell out the device id in each DISCOVER and ACCEPT and the mesh's name in the OFFER, the last byte of each, which the code never writes, the OFFER's member count, the ACCEPT's device key and the initiator's DISCOVER), and, from a WAP whose owner turned the mesh off, the Scout's events and the hub-election and channel-lock frames at any time, and the heartbeats too once a pairing a session starts on it ends with a member held, until the next reboot or until the setting is written off again, recorded only until PR 8, which sends 0 for both clocks and the padding, starts each boot's counters past a random skip, clears the pairing payloads and sends only what their receivers read, and sends nothing, a pairing included, from a mesh turned off (P9, P20);
- the SoftAP interface's answers after PR 8: `/api/wifi`'s `ap_auth`, `ap_auth_reason` and `sta_pmf` and the self-test's full metrics, the last crash's cause and stage among them on a build with Bluetooth, which every caller on that interface reads while the SoftAP is up, and so every session on a standalone (AP-only) WAP (§3.2, P4, P5);
- `tls_enabled` on the public `/api/device-info` (observable by connecting);
- the chain sequence at the open tier: the BLE status service's chain sequence and `/api/fleet`'s `chain_height`, the same fact the fleet beacon broadcasts as chain height (§7.2 list 1; §5.8 keeps `/api/fleet` and the beacon as they are); the status service's uptime goes to whole hours in PR 8 (P9);
- the display's `/api/log` (P22).

`node_status.h`'s `creds_at_rest` is a recorded exception too: a posture word on the CORS-open `/api/status` of the displays and the trio, which the WAP's `/api/status` answers a session and the flagship's the bearer (`auth_gate()`). It is the same on every shipped build today (PARITY.md and TRUST_MODEL.md say so), so it tells a LAN observer nothing about one unit over another. Once a shipped build can report another word, some units differ, and the word becomes a differential and must move behind owner proof (§11, finding P16). Owner proof is not the same on every family (§14): the flagship's copy already takes the bearer, and the WAP's session copy would move to the bearer; the displays and the trio have no owner credential (§3.7), so on them the word leaves the open answers, and only the glass and USB may carry it, unless the family has gained one by then. That takes NVS encryption, which needs the arduino-as-IDF-component migration (`firmware/ESP32S3_OPTIMIZATION_ROADMAP.md` item 9); the opt-in secure kit does not change the word (`docs/PARITY.md`, footnote 1, "Plaintext NVS, reported honestly"), and `key_at_rest.h`'s `classify()` gives `plaintext-nvs` whenever NVS encryption is off, with flash encryption and Secure Boot or without.

### 3.7 Owner authority: where it plugs in

Nothing here waited on owner authority, and the owner-authority PR has since landed, as #1887. It made the sign-in of the WAP's administrator page (`/admin` and `/settings`) check a pasted token by itself (the page already took the token, pasted or fetched with a BOOT tap, and held it in the page's memory only), and it moved the routes that grant something to the bearer alone (the `api_auth.h` header lists them; the 2.4.20 train, #1972, moved the firmware install and the update settings, `POST /api/ota/install` and `POST /api/ota/config`, to the bearer too). It added no owner session: the cookie the landing page mints for any LAN host is still LAN trust, and every read this design records for a session still answers it. So on the WAP the owner predicate is the bearer, which the seam's owner branch reads (§3.5) and the administrator page, signed in with the token, sends. These places change, each in the PR named:

1. **PR 8** serves the session's recorded reads on the bearer branch (P20): `/api/status`'s `boot_count`, `witness_count`, `chain_seq`, `peek_active` and top-level `uptime_sec`, `/api/system`'s uptime, `/api/diagnostics`'s SD writes and egress counters, the WAP's Wi-Fi signal, the mesh's id, name, counters and peer rows, and the strip's `restarted` and `low_memory` reasons. The mesh's id stays readable to any radio in range all the same, in every opera frame, beside the whole key fingerprint (§7.2 list 1): this move takes it off the LAN answers, not off the air, and whether the frames stop carrying it is open (§14). Each member's name at every change of its state, the mesh's name after a rename and a removed member's key fingerprint stay in the health log, which a session and a bond read (§3.2). A session still reads the witness count, the chain's sequence, the live view's state, the uptime, the reset cause (in the health log and the tamper word), safe mode, the key-at-rest word and, on FULL builds, whether flash encryption is off (in the health log), the camera's, the microphone's and the Wi-Fi sensing's counters and the signal in other answers (the self-test's Wi-Fi row while its `code` and `detail` stay on every interface, the sensing window at P2), and a bond reads the signal too (§3.2), so the companion's held line at the session tier (PR 12) counts none of them. PR 8 also takes a raise of the Wi-Fi sensing's privacy ceiling to the bearer, and its first boot lowers a ceiling stored above P0 before it (§3.2).
2. The WAP's `/admin` Protection panel shows posture (including the reset cause and crash summary) and activity (the camera, microphone and CSI link counters) to a page signed in with the token: **PR 12** adds the panel, once `/admin` has room for it, which it has not today (§5.3), and **PR 22** its activity rows. Neither app shows Protection today. The iPhone app gains it in PR 5 (§6.5), and the desktop Flasher in PR 18's Details drawer (§6.9), which follows PR 12; until the panel reaches `/admin`, an owner reads it in the iPhone app once PR 5 has landed. A session reads the counters `/api/peek/status`, `/api/audio/status` and `/api/status` carry, and the reset cause in the health log and the tamper word (§3.2), so no held line counts those; the companion's held rows, which count only what a session cannot read anywhere, unlock only for a page that holds the token.
3. The bonded-BLE details characteristic (§5.3, **PR 13**) stays at the lan tier. F346 is decided: since #1887 a session confirms no bond and reads no Passkey Entry's digits, and since #1836 a bond no owner asked for (Just Works) is deleted. But a bond a session confirmed before #1887 stays until the owner removes it, and a Just Works bond that escapes #1836's delete stays in NimBLE's store (left out of the rebuilt paired list since #1920; open and bench-gated as ledger F446), so a bond is not yet owner proof on every WAP. When the characteristic moves to the owner tier is open (§14).
4. **PR 22**: every setting in §9 that is marked owner becomes writable from Details, behind the bearer. §9 records two things that PR inherits: a session already writes the WAP's BLE transmit power, and the iPhone app's Nightlight section keeps a glass-knob value the display refused (ledger A134; since #1986 the app's writes carry the display's CSRF token).
5. **PR 22**: "Copy details (owner)" becomes available. It still carries no counter, posture, activity or handle (§6.4); it adds only the owner-tier build and unit facts.
6. The WAP's `/api/bluetooth` names, addresses, signal, distances and per-device times and counts, which PR 8 gives the bearer alone (P7), are then already at the owner tier; nothing moves.

If a later PR gives the WAP an owner session, `api_auth_audience()` returns owner for it: one branch, plus a checker plant (§13).

The displays and the trio have no owner credential and gain none here. Their posture and activity stay on the glass and USB until a family gains one.

---

## 4. The details record

### 4.1 Wire rules

- Each part is one JSON object on one line:

  ```json
  {"schema":"securacv.canary.details/v1","part":"<id>","tier":"<tier>","held":<n>, ...}
  ```

  A `sensor` part's header also carries `"id"` (and `"view":"link"` for its activity view), and a `sensors` index page carries `"page"`, so a body names the request it answers and a signed body binds it (§8).

  The schema string does not contain `securacv.canary.manifest/`, the prefix the browser flasher's and the website's manifest finders scan for (`MANIFEST_SCHEMA_PREFIX` in `canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`, `SCHEMA_PREFIX` in the website's `js/serial.js`), and it is not the whole string `securacv.canary.manifest/v1` that the desktop's `inspect_line()` matches (`desktop/flash-engine/src/monitor.rs`).
- **A missing key means "cannot say".** The writer never fakes 0, "unknown" or a fallback chip name.
- Integers carry their unit in the key name (`_mb`, `_kb`, `_mhz`, `_c`, `_h`, `_dbm` for transmit power only). A length of time may end in `_ms`, `_s` or `_h` only when its fact row says `duration: true`; no key names a point in time (§10 item 2).
- **Durations only, never time of day.** `built_day` is days since the epoch, the `born_day` convention. `up_h` comes from `firmware/common/time/coarse_uptime.h` **(new in PR 8, pure, host-tested)**, the one helper every surface uses (the WAP's BLE status service from PR 8, the details writer from PR 9, `node_status.h` and the trio's peer heartbeat from PR 11; `/api/device-info` drops its uptime in PR 8, since nothing reads it):

  ```
  up_h = uptime_s < offset_s ? 0 : (uptime_s - offset_s) / 3600     // offset_s drawn once per boot
  ```

  The glue draws `offset_s` in [0, 3600) with `esp_random()` at boot and never stores it. **There is one offset per boot, shared by every surface on that device.** Two surfaces with independent offsets would step at two different instants each hour, and a poller watching both would learn the boot to within the gap between them. Whole hours alone do not hide the boot: anyone polling sees the value step to `N` at exactly boot + N hours, which dates the power-on to within the polling interval. With the offset, the steps fall at boot + offset + N hours, and the boot is known only to within the hour the coarse form promises. Subtracting the offset, rather than adding it, means the value never overstates: the app says "Up for at least 3 hours", or "Up for less than two hours" for 0, since 0 covers the boot's first hour plus the offset.
- **Identity is never in details:** no fingerprint, no `hw_token`, no MAC and no serial. The apps already hold identity from pairing, and the manifest carries it on USB.
- **Strings.** Trusted constants are ASCII (`static_assert`) and cost one byte per character. Text a module reports (a touch controller's product string) is clamped to its cap and escaped, budgeted at 6 bytes per character.
- **Overflow.** The writer refuses the whole part rather than cut it. It returns 0, and the handler answers `503 {"error":"details unavailable"}`.
- `held` is a **generated constant per (family, shipped configuration, part, sensor kind, tier, core)**: the number of facts in the vocabulary for that part that
  - have a class the tier withholds;
  - have a source for that configuration and core that is `read`, `set`, `board` or `build` (a WAP profile that compiles the camera out has no camera facts to hold);
  - belong to that sensor kind's sheet, for a `sensor` part (a BH1750 or PIR page holds no link counters, so it never claims to);
  - and are given to that tier by no recorded legacy answer on that family (§3.2, §3.6).

  It is never counted at run time. A run-time count would change with posture state: crash task and cause exist only when a crash record does, and SoftAP auth and PMF only while an AP is up, so `{"part":"security","tier":"lan","held":N}` would itself leak posture. An app turns `held` into the per-family phrase from §3.4, for example "4 more details are shown only to the app paired with this Canary's key". It never implies the facts are absent, and by the rule above it never claims something is withheld that the same tier reads elsewhere.

### 4.2 Parts

The worst cases below are estimates from each field's cap: strings at their cap, integers at their widest, module text at 6 bytes per character. Each counts 91 bytes for the header. The plain header §4.1 spells is at most 83 B (part `security`, a six-letter tier and a three-digit `held`, counted for this landing); a `sensor` part's `id` and `view` come on top of it. Revision 3 reran the estimates with every field the table lists (Review history). They are estimates, not measurements: the block has no `facts` rows and no caps yet (§2.2), so nothing at `62bc0bc7` can compute them, and this landing did not derive them again. The generator computes the real bound **per (part, sensor kind)** from the generated caps, and the host test checks each; either fails if any bound passes its cap.

| Part | Holds | Cap | Worst (est.) | Changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `about` | `rev` (vocabulary), product, `hw` board token, fw, chip, chip_rev, cores, flash_mb, psram_mb, idf, core, the list of parts this firmware answers (no reset word: it is posture) | **512** (one ATT attribute) | about 470 | at boot |
| `now` | `up_h`, `temp_c`, heap free (KiB), PSRAM free, health word; activity: signal word; posture: reset cause word, `crash_saved`, heap low-water; counter: `boots` | 768 | 317 | live |
| `chip` | model, rev, package, wafer, block version, cores, `cpu_mhz` (actual), `xtal_mhz`, features, `calib` (present, from the block version); flash {size, physical size, mode, speed, JEDEC vendor, SKU capacity/vendor/temperature grade on IDF 5}; PSRAM {size, mode, speed, SKU} | 768 | 703 | at boot |
| `memory` | heap {total, free} and PSRAM the same (KiB), degrade word, settings-storage entries used/free/total; posture: the low-water marks (the IDF low-water mark, one meaning), largest free block, fragmentation %, lowest stack headroom where read | 768 | 410 | live |
| `image` | fw, git, project, `built_day`, idf, core, libraries {ArduinoJson, NimBLE, LVGL where compiled; PubSubClient 2.8 defines no version macro, so it has no key}, profile, running slot, slot count and size, image size, OTA state, first 8 bytes of the ELF SHA-256, `secure_version` (the image's) | 768 | 567 | at boot |
| `security` | secure boot, flash encryption mode, NVS encryption, key at rest, JTAG, download mode, eFuse rollback floor, SoftAP auth, PMF, crash task and cause (all posture) | 768 | 390 | at boot |
| `radio` | Wi-Fi {standard, width, max TX dBm, power save, PHY on IDF 5}; BLE {stack, version, TX dBm, MTU, address mode}; SoftAP address mode; USB serial descriptor mode; ESP-NOW {version, peer count}; 802.15.4 status | 768 | 419 | at boot |
| `sensors&page=n` | up to 6 items {id, kind, part, latched status word}, `next` | 768 | 658 | at boot and on status change |
| `sensor&id=` | kind, part, maker, provenance, bus, address, pins (as built), module firmware where read, the latched status word, current settings (up to 6 knobs). **No activity.** | 768 | 646, every kind | on status change |
| `sensor&id=&view=link` | **activity only**, for a kind whose sheet has any: the MR60's link {ok, age word, errors, unknown, dropped}; Vision's link and `perf()` timings {pre, infer, post ms}; Sentinel fusion's channels {id, vote word, quality word, has reported}, one per channel the preset compiles: six on the door, window and hallway presets (PIR, radar, light, CSI, Wi-Fi RF, BLE), four on mailbox-lite (PIR, Wi-Fi RF, BLE, light) and all nine on perimeter-demo. A kind with none answers no link view. | 768 | MR60 223; Vision 293; Sentinel's six channels about 560; nine pass the cap (below) | live |
| `end` (USB only) | the number of lines sent | — | — | — |

A **latched status word** (Working, Not responding, Stalled) changes only on a link transition held for a fixed debounce, never on a frame-by-frame count, so the scene cannot move it. It is the only per-sensor health a `lan` or `broker` reader gets. `test_device_details.cpp` **(new)** replays a scripted MR60 stream (present, then empty, then present) and requires byte-identical `lan` sensor pages across it.

**Why the activity view is its own part.** In revision 2 the `sensor` part carried the activity fields too, and its 745 B estimate left them out: Vision's timings take it to 815 B and four Sentinel channels to 1,036 B, both past the cap. Splitting them out also makes the tier rule plain: at `lan` and `broker` the link view is wholly held, and the identity page carries nothing the scene moves. `firmware/common/fusion/sentinel_fusion.h` defines nine channels (`enum class Channel`), and by the same estimate all nine are 770 B, past the cap. One preset compiles all nine: `perimeter-demo`, which env `canary-sentinel-demo-head` builds (`firmware/configs/canary-sentinel/perimeter-demo/config.h` sets `FEATURE_CONTACT`, `FEATURE_VISION` and `FEATURE_TAMPER` over the door preset's six). So the bound is generated from the channels each preset compiles, and the generator refuses a kind whose compiled set passes the cap; that kind's list then pages with `&page=`, which the perimeter-demo list must. No release ships a Sentinel image today: `firmware/flavors.json` gives the project `build_envs` only (`canary-sentinel-door` and `canary-sentinel-lite`) and an `unreleased` note.

Six items per index page: the estimate is 658 B for six, 748 B for seven and 838 B for eight. Seven would leave 20 B, which one longer kind name spends.

### 4.3 `rev`: when the app and the firmware disagree

`rev` is the first 8 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the canonical **wire-relevant** projection of the block (§2.1: keys, types, caps, classes, parts, tiers, `since`, and the reset and format word ids), generated into both the firmware and every app. Labels, explanations, terms and part sheets are outside the hash, so a copy edit does not change `rev` or any byte of the details record. It still changes the firmware wherever an `on_device` label is compiled in: the label regions in the on-device pages, and from PR 17 the glass's `device_details_labels.h`, whose edit also moves the emulator dist (§2.3, §2.4). An app whose `rev` differs still renders every key it knows. For keys it does not know, it shows "This Canary reports N details this app doesn't know yet". It never renders an unknown key's raw spelling, and an unknown key never enters Copy Details.

### 4.4 An example (lan tier, a Sense)

```json
{"schema":"securacv.canary.details/v1","part":"about","tier":"lan","held":0,"rev":"3f9a12c0","product":"canary-sense","hw":"xiao-esp32c6-mr60","fw":"2.4.20","chip":"ESP32-C6","chip_rev":"v0.1","cores":1,"flash_mb":4,"psram_mb":0,"idf":"v5.5.4","core":"3.3.8","parts":["now","chip","memory","image","security","radio","sensors"]}
{"schema":"securacv.canary.details/v1","part":"security","tier":"lan","held":10}
```

The `10` is illustrative; the generator computes the constant for (canary-sense, its shipped configuration, security, lan, core 3). Whatever it is, it is the same with or without a crash record or a setup AP.

---

## 5. Producers and transports, family by family

### 5.1 Shared code

- **`firmware/common/details/device_details.h` (new)**: pure, with no Arduino, no heap and no network header.
  - It provides `size_t details::build(Part, const Facts&, Tier, char* out, size_t cap)`. Called with `out == nullptr` it returns the exact length; otherwise it writes.
  - It reuses the escaping and latching of `manifest::detail::Writer` (`firmware/common/attest/self_manifest.h`) and **extends** it with a measure mode. Today that writer always dereferences its pointer, so this is an extension of the keystone, not a fork.
  - Every `put()` takes a generated `K::` constant. **There is no string-key overload.** Each put checks the key's class against the tier and refuses strings that are not ASCII or are over their cap: the key is omitted, never cut. The part's `held` comes from the generated constant table, never from these omissions (§4.1).
- **`firmware/common/details/details_esp.h` (new)**: header-only on purpose. `scripts/lint_common_lib_manifests.py` documents the path-prefixed `.cpp` link trap, so each tree includes it from exactly one glue file. Static facts are read once at boot into a struct of about 300 B; live facts are read on request.

  | Facts | APIs |
  |---|---|
  | Chip | `esp_chip_info()`; `efuse_hal_get_major/minor_chip_version()` (both cores); `esp_efuse_read_field_blob()` on the named field descriptors `PKG_VERSION`, `WAFER_VERSION_*`, `BLK_VERSION_*` only; the `FLASH_CAP`, `FLASH_TEMP` and `FLASH_VENDOR` SKU fields under `#if ESP_IDF_VERSION_MAJOR >= 5` on the S3, C3 and C6, and the `PSRAM_*` SKU fields only when `CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3` is also set (§2.2); "calibration present" from the block version, as IDF's own calibration-version check does, never from a trim field (the classic ESP32 of the flagship's `esp32cam` and `esp32-wroom` reach ports has no block-version field in either core's `esp_efuse_table.h`, so the fact is in its `not_on` list there and reads "Not on this chip", §6.3); `getCpuFrequencyMhz()`; `getXtalFrequencyMhz()`. `lint_identifier_reads.py` **(new in PR 4)** denies every trim and calibration descriptor (§2.2 `efuse_calibration_values`). |
  | Flash | `ESP.getFlashChipSize/Mode/Speed`; `esp_flash_read_id()` (vendor and capacity only, once at boot, before the dash's RGB scanout starts); `esp_flash_get_physical_size()` |
  | Memory | `heap_caps_get_*` for internal memory and SPIRAM, including the true low-water mark; `nvs_get_stats()` |
  | Image | `esp_app_get_description()` (IDF 5) or `esp_ota_get_app_description()` (IDF 4); the running partition and its state; `esp_core_dump_image_check()` **for presence only**, wrapped by the one allowlisted helper; `ESP_ARDUINO_VERSION_*`, `ARDUINOJSON_VERSION`, `NIMBLE_CPP_VERSION_*` (NimBLE 2.x only; 1.4.3 reads "Not reported") and `LVGL_VERSION_*` where compiled |
  | Posture | `esp_secure_boot_enabled()`, `esp_flash_encryption_enabled()` and its mode, `key_at_rest::classify()`, `esp_efuse_read_secure_version()`, the JTAG and download-mode fuse bits. **Reads only; `lint_identifier_reads.py` bans every eFuse write and burn API.** |
  | Radio | `esp_wifi_get_protocol/bandwidth/max_tx_power/ps` after Wi-Fi init; the NimBLE TX power and MTU; `esp_now_get_version()` and the peer **count** |
  | Temperature | Only through a provider the family passes in (the flagship's `thermal_read_die_c()`, the WAP's sys_monitor reading). IDF 5 allows one temperature-sensor instance (a second `temperature_sensor_install()` is refused as "Already installed", `components/esp_driver_tsens/src/temperature_sensor.c` at the release tag v5.5.4). |

  The reset word comes from one generated table. It replaces the WAP's three spellings (`reset_reason_name()` in `hardware_state.h`, and in `sys_monitor.h` both `get_json()`'s `reset_reason` and `print_status()`'s "Reset Reason" line) and adds the IDF 5 reasons that fall to "unknown" today: USB and JTAG, which first appear in the IDF v5.2 release tag, and eFuse, power glitch and CPU lockup, which first appear in v5.3 (later patch releases of the older lines carry them too: v5.1.4 has all five). Each row is guarded by `ESP_IDF_VERSION >= ESP_IDF_VERSION_VAL(...)` (§2.3), and `test_device_details.cpp` compiles the map twice, under an IDF 4.4 shim and an IDF 5.5 shim, and requires `ESP_RST_USB` to map to its word on the second.

  It never includes `esp_mac.h`, and it names no `never` API. The lint proves both.
- **`firmware/common/time/coarse_uptime.h` (new, PR 8)**: the uptime helper of §4.1, pure and header-only, with `test_coarse_uptime.cpp` **(new)** proving that the value never exceeds the true uptime, that its step instants are not aligned to the boot, and that two readers on one device step at the same instant (one offset per boot). It lands with its first user, the WAP's BLE status service (PR 8), and its WAP staged copy starts `check_details_sync.sh` **(new)**.
- **Cost.** About 6 KB of code and 2 KB of strings per image (an estimate), one 768 B buffer per carrier context, and about 300 B of cached facts. The first PR that links it reports the measured delta against every `firmware/flavors.json` size guard and every release env's build size. The lean form is sized against the tightest guarded images, in the `.bin` bytes their guards read (CI's size guard on #1972's merge, whose firmware tree is this one's, 2026-11-16; §1.2): `canary-display-nightstand-c6` (0x1F0000 slot, 42,128 B free), `canary-sense-wellbeing` (0x1E0000, 97,984 B free; the default Sense image has 101,824 B), Sentinel Lite (0x140000, 148,416 B free) and the flagship's `release_ha`, the published OTA image (0x1E0000, 199,824 B free). `canary-display-nightlight-c3`, the other 4 MB display, is guarded at the old 0x1E0000 ceiling, 354,528 B under it. Both 4 MB displays already build `CD_LEAN_BUILD`; the lean form joins it there, and Sentinel Lite gets the board's equivalent. The lean form drops the SKU decode and the per-sensor `set` objects, and the measurement decides anything further.

### 5.2 Flagship (`firmware/canary`, esp_http_server, bearer)

- **USB.**
  - The key `q` ("details") joins `kConsoleCommands` as `Tier::Diag`. `table_is_safe()` stays host-tested.
  - The manifest's `commands[]` gains `{"key":"q","name":"details"}`: 29 B with its comma. A host build of `self_manifest.h` with §1.2's release-shaped worst-case facts writes 1,265 B, and 1,294 B with `q`, of the 1,600 B release buffer (measured at `0a06c14b`, 2026-11-16). `test_self_manifest.cpp` gains that release-shaped worst case as a pinned assertion.
  - The key prints one line per part, at the serial tier, then an `end` line.
- **HTTP.**
  - **`GET /api/details?part=&id=&page=` (new)** sits behind `auth_gate` and `rate_limit_check`; owner tier only.
  - Today the table has **no spare slot**: the worst case is 75/63 against `kRouteTableSlots` 75/63. One unconditional registration makes it 76/64. PR 14 raises the constant to **78/66**, so 2 spare slots per variant exist for the first time. It corrects the comment's arithmetic: 17 unconditional with `/api/details` (16 today, not 14), 48 base (47 today, not 45). It also teaches `check_route_budgets()` a `MIN_SPARE = 2` assertion per variant, with a planted case that must fail. Today the checker fails only when the total exceeds the slots, and it does not read the comment. `check_route_security.py` then counts 84 registrations, 12 public. Each extra slot costs one pointer in esp_http_server's handler array (`hd_calls`, `components/esp_http_server/src/httpd_main.c` at the release tags v4.4.7 and v5.5.4).
  - The body uses a 768 B buffer on the handler stack (8,192 B plain, 10,240 B under TLS: `config.stack_size` and `ssl.httpd.stack_size` in `startHttpServer()`, `securacv_network.cpp`).
  - Camera facts are read under the camera lifecycle lock (`securacv_camera.h`); SD facts come from the diagnostics module's SD snapshot (`diag_get_sd()`), whose card-space read `update_sd()` already limits to once every 10 minutes.
- **MQTT.** A retained `securacv/<id>/details` carries the `about` part (§5.7).
- **Toolchains.** Most envs are core 2.0.17 (IDF 4.4.7); `[env:full]` is IDF 5.5. IDF-5-only reads are `#if`'d and omitted on core 2.
- **Hardware token.** The flagship stays without one until `figures.json` gives it a mapping (decided, §14: `hw` stays omitted until a flagship figure exists, and PR 6, #1971, did not invent one). `board:seeed_xiao_esp32s3` is the WAP's figure with `shared: false`, and `CANARY_FIGURE_HARDWARE` also feeds the OTA board binding (`ota_cfg.board` in `firmware/canary/src/main.cpp`). Reusing it would draw a WAP and bind the flagship to the WAP's images.

### 5.3 WAP (Arduino sketch, esp_http_server, LAN-trust session plus bearer)

- **USB.** The new keys are `j` (the self-manifest, a first for the WAP) and `q`.
  - `self_manifest.h`, `device_details.h` and the generated keys are staged into the sketch as byte-identical copies, held by `firmware/scripts/check_details_sync.sh` **(new)** on the pattern of `check_csi_sync.sh`.
- **HTTP.**
  - **`GET /api/details` (new)** refuses a foreign Host first, through the staged `host_guard.h`, then sits behind `api_auth_audience()` (§3.5).
  - The budget gains an itemized line, `const int details_handlers = 1;`, added to `total_handlers` (the itemized block in `start_http_server()`, `canary_wap.ino`, under "RAISE a number here").
  - The route also goes in `wap_server.h`'s table, which `test_dashboard_route_match.test.js` holds.
  - It is sent with `httpd_resp_sendstr()` and `Cache-Control: no-store`, not `http_send_json()`, which adds `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`.
  - The handler reads only boot-cached facts and the snapshots the loop task already publishes (`loop_snapshot.h`). It calls nothing the loop task owns (`check_wap_loop_commands.py`).
  - Budget after the change:

  | Config | Today | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | DEV/S3 | 142 of 152 | 143 of 153, **still 10 free** |
  | FULL/S3 | 177 of 200 | 178 of 201 |
  | FULL/C3 | 150 of 173 | 151 of 174 |
  | +beacon variants | — | the same deltas |
- **Answer rule.** `check_wap_json_answers.py` gains **J13**, the next free rule (this design first called it J10; J10 to J12 have since been taken, for Bluetooth names, `String` appends and the health-log scrub):
  - The details answer is what `details::build()` wrote into a buffer of `DETAILS_PART_CAP` bytes. A 768 B stack buffer is fine here: `/api/system` already answers from 2,048 B on this task.
  - It is refused whole on 0.
  - No `snprintf` spells it and no `serializeJson` serializes it.
  - J13 gets a mutation self-test.
- **Signed variant.** The same route with `&nonce=` (§8), at no extra slot.
- **Pages.** The `/admin` page has no gzip headroom: on this tree it is 67,638 B, 2,102 B past the 65,536 B ceiling this plan decided (§14). It first passed the ceiling in #1919, at 65,809 B, and #1972 added 144 B after #1971. So it gets no new panel at the lan tier, and every step below that adds to `/admin` needs room made first.
  - System Health's six-row DEVICE INFO block becomes one "About this Canary ›" link to `/companion#about` (PR 12). The firmware placeholders went in PR 6 (#1971): the page reads "--" until it has a value, and the footer's version is gone.
  - The companion page (48,705 B gzip at `62bc0bc7`, as at `0a06c14b`, 16,831 B under 65,536) gets the About panel. It is data-driven from `/api/details`, takes its labels from the generated label region in `companion_pwa.h` (§2.3), and uses the session (lan tier). Its held line counts only the facts the lan tier cannot read anywhere after PR 8, as §3.2 lists them (its posture facts other than the reset cause, the largest free block, SoftAP auth and PMF, key at rest and NVS encryption, flash encryption on a FULL build and, on a build with Bluetooth, the last crash's cause and stage; the boot count and the SD writes; and any other counter or activity fact no legacy answer gives it), with the WAP's phrase. It never says a fact a session still reads in another answer, such as the witness count, the uptime or the reset cause, is shown only to the app.
  - A **Protection** panel appears in `/admin` Settings › Device only for a page signed in with the token (owner tier). `/admin` already sends the bearer when signed in (`secureFetch()` in `web_ui.h`). The panel waits for a PR that makes room in `/admin`: the owner-authority PR, #1887, left the page at 65,326 B, 210 B under the ceiling, and the page has grown 2,312 B since. Neither app shows Protection today: the iPhone app gains it in PR 5 (§6.5) and the desktop Flasher in PR 18's Details drawer (§6.9), which follows PR 12, so until the panel reaches `/admin` an owner reads it in the iPhone app once PR 5 has landed.
  - PR 6 was to make `gen_web_assets_gz.py --check` print each asset's gzip size and headroom, and fail when `/admin`'s gzip (`CANARY_UI_HTML`) grows past a budget recorded in the script. #1971 did not build that gate: `--check` still only byte-compares the generated header and prints no size. So nothing holds §14's rule that every `web_ui.h` edit before PR 12 shrinks or holds the page, and four PRs grew the page after #1887 (#1919, #1920 and #1951, then #1972 after #1971). The gate is still owed (ledger F479), with its budget set to what its PR measures, never more than the page that PR starts from (67,638 B on this tree), and lowered by each PR that shrinks the page. PR 8, the next PR in this plan to edit `web_ui.h`, depends on it; which PR adds it is open (§14).
  - PR 12 replaces that budget with a per-asset ceiling. The ceiling was decided hard (§14): CI fails a WAP gzip asset at 65,536 B or more. `/admin` is past it today, so that gate cannot land until the page has lost at least 2,103 B (to 65,535 B) or the ceiling is decided again. Which of the two happens is open (§14).
  - All new page copy passes `firmware/scripts/microcopy_lint.sh`: "settings storage", never the banned term.
- **BLE.** A second characteristic in the bonded console service carries `about`, at most 512 B, one ATT attribute. It uses `READ | READ_ENC | READ_AUTHEN`, the same read gate as the snapshot (the `g_snapshot` characteristic in `ble_console.cpp`). It carries the **lan tier**: F346 is decided, but a bond is not yet owner proof on every WAP (§3.7). Nothing is added to DIS, the status service, Opera, the chirp or the fleet beacon.
- **MQTT.** `csi_mqtt.cpp` publishes the retained details topic. The status topic gains `hardware`.
- **Hardware token.** Built in PR 6 (#1971). The S3 target defines `CANARY_FIGURE_HARDWARE "board:seeed_xiao_esp32s3"` (`build_config.h`), which `figures.json` maps to the WAP, and `handle_status()` and `handle_fleet()` read it, so `node_status`'s `hw` and the fleet self row carry it. The C3 target defines none, and both omit it.
- **Flash.** The 0x330000 slot has room: `canary-wap-default`'s `.bin` is 2,398,432 B, 943,904 B under it (CI's size guard on #1972's merge; §1.2). The PR reports the delta.

### 5.4 Displays (nine manifests, Arduino `WebServer`, no owner credential)

- **HTTP.**
  - **`GET /api/details` (new)**, lan tier. It refuses a foreign Host and a cross-site Origin first (403) and sends no CORS header. That is the same "who is asking" question `/api/fleet` already asks before it adds wellbeing words to peer rows (`handle_fleet()` in `glass_web.cpp`: `coarse_only = origin_is_cross_site() || host_is_foreign()`).
  - `WebServer` has no fixed handler table. The body is a 768 B static buffer on the loop task, the only task `WebServer` runs on.
- **The existing `/api/device`.** Its only reader is the same image's mirror page (`device()` in `mirror_html.h`).
  - In the same PR it gains the Host and Origin refusal, as `/api/log` does too, and loses `ssid`, `signal` (the dBm reading), `heap_min_kb` (the heap low-water mark, posture by §3.1, for the reason the WAP's low-water marks move in PR 8) and `up_s` (uptime to the second, P9). `caps` stays: it is build class.
  - `/api/log` mirrors the cable's lines to the LAN, each stamped with uptime to a tenth of a second (`log_capture()` in `glass_web.cpp`), and those lines include household and update events ("Household acknowledge received" in `mqtt_mgr.cpp`; "Firmware update rolled back" in `ota_mgr.cpp`; "Timezone set by hand from the web page" in `glass_web.cpp`). PR 11 drops the stamps (the lines keep their order) and records the route as a legacy answer. P22 is decided (§14): the route stays on the lan tier, without stamps and behind the Host and Origin refusal. The glass switch the design proposed ("Show the log on my network for 10 minutes", since the display has no owner credential and the glass is physical) is not taken now, and is revisited if a bench run shows the log carrying household events a LAN host should not read.
  - The mirror page moves to `/api/details` and labels its rows from the generated region in `mirror_html.h` (both copies, held by `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`). The lan tier carries no signal, not even as a word (activity, §3.1). Since #1971 the mirror turns `/api/device`'s `signal` into a word of its own (`sigWord()`: "strong signal", "ok signal", "weak signal"); that word goes with the key in PR 11, and the mirror's signal row then reads "Shown on this Canary's own screen" (§3.4), where the glass shows it. The display's lan-tier `held` does not count the signal all the same: a display with no broker credentials publishes its status, `rssi` included, to any broker the LAN refers it to (§3.6).
  - `/api/device` stays, frozen by the legacy rule, until the app release that prefers `/api/details` has shipped. Its removal is then a one-line PR.
- **USB.** `q` only; the display has no key, so it has no manifest. It is read inside `glass_web_tick()`. The glue is in `src/net/details_glue.cpp` **(new)**.
  - **When it answers.** `glass_web_tick()` runs only while no setup wizard is up. `setup()` calls `provision_run()`, a blocking modal phase that "returns with the STA associated and credentials persisted" (the comment above the call in `firmware/projects/canary-display/src/main.cpp`), on any boot whose saved network is the placeholder (`provision_needed()` is `wifi_is_placeholder()`): a first boot with no network saved, and the boot after Settings › Forget Wi-Fi, which forgets the network and restarts (`Page::NetForget` in `settings_ui.cpp`). A display flashed with a network skips the wizard: both flashers can write one for every display product (`wifi_nvs` in `canary-local/devices/flash.json`). `glass_web_init()` comes after the wizard ("Started AFTER provisioning") and after the boot's own join, which `wifi_init_or_reboot()` waits on for at most `WIFI_BOOT_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s) before it leaves retries to `wifi_loop()`; until then `glass_web_tick()` returns at once without a server (`if (!s_server) return;`). `loop()` opens the same blocking wizard in two cases (`wifi_wants_setup()`, "reopening setup"): when the saved credentials fail with a cause a person can fix and neither this power cycle nor any saved network has ever joined (the ring's persisted joined bit, since #1972), and, whatever the display's history, after ten minutes offline with credentials saved (`recovery_reraise_due()` in `firmware/common/network/onboarding_fsm.h`). `provision.cpp` reads no serial input. So from PR 10 a display answers `q` whenever no wizard is up: from the end of the boot's join when a network is saved, joined or not, and from the wizard's join when none is, except while `loop()` has the wizard open, which an outage of ten minutes or more does on any display. First-boot setup is when a flasher's "From the Board Itself" most wants it (Vision's own `j` comment, the one that says "first-boot diagnosis", makes the same point, `canary-vision/src/main.cpp`).
