Until PR 17 the flashers say so when no details line comes: "This display answers when its setup screen is not showing. Finish setup, or wait for it to rejoin Wi-Fi, then read again." **PR 17 closes the gap**: `provision_run()`'s loop polls the same reader through a hook, which covers both wizards, and because `provision.cpp` is emulator-compiled, that change rides the dist PR.
  - **Never to the LAN.** On the displays `log_line()` prints to the cable and also to `g_log_sink` (`include/canary/log.h`), which `glass_web_init()` points at the ring that `/api/log` serves to the LAN (`log_capture()` and `handle_log()` in `glass_web.cpp`). The `q` output therefore goes through `dbg_serial()` only, never `log_line()` or `g_log_sink`, and `lint_device_details.py` **(new in PR 3)** fails if `details_glue.cpp` names either.
  - None of these files is in the emulator's compile set, because `emu_net.cpp` stubs `glass_web_tick()`. So PR 10 does not move the dist, and the Dist drift check confirms it.
- **Glass.** A new Settings › About page, `Page::About`, is appended after `WxLoc` as the enum comment requires (`enum class Page` in `settings_ui.cpp`, the comment that begins "APPENDED"), at the glass tier. Its labels come from the generated `device_details_labels.h` **(new in PR 17)**. Household facts show here from the glass's own state.
  - `settings_ui.cpp` and `main.cpp` **are** emulator-compiled. So this ships in the dist PR (PR 17), following `docs/GENERATED_FILES.md`'s order: `setup.sh regen` → "Rebuild emulator dist (pinned emsdk)" → pull → `gen_flash.py` and the catalogs, with the commit and push the rebuild needs before its dispatch, which that file does not state (§12 PR 17's order, step 4).
- **Part identities.** Touch, RTC, IMU and SD probes are cached at boot. `io/rtc.cpp` is compiled on touch169 and amoled241, and `fleet/*.cpp` is compiled for every flavor, so these ride the dist PR too.
- **The emulator's facts (PR 17).** `mqtt_mgr.cpp` (the details topic and honest health) and `settings_ui.cpp` (`Page::About`) are emulator-compiled, and the shim has no `esp_chip_info`, `efuse_hal`, `esp_flash`, app-descriptor, secure-boot or flash-encryption headers (`canary-local/emulator/shim/` holds `Arduino.h`, `esp_heap_caps.h`, `esp_system.h`, `esp_random.h` and a few others; `EmuESP` offers `getChipModel()` and the like). So both read facts through a provider interface, `include/canary/details_facts.h` **(new)**. On hardware `src/net/details_glue.cpp` implements it with `details_esp.h`; in the emulator `canary-local/emulator/src/emu_details.cpp` **(new)** implements it with each flavor's own chip, every value labeled `emulated` (`EMU_SRCS` in `build.sh` already compiles `emulator/src/*.cpp`). No emulator-compiled file includes `details_esp.h`, or an IDF header the shim does not carry, and the dist link proves it.

### 5.5 Sense, Vision, Sentinel (`node_http`, no owner credential)

- **HTTP.** `node_http.h`'s `Hooks` gains `void (*fill_details)(...)` **(new)**; a null hook means the route is not registered.
  - It serves **`GET /api/details`**, lan tier, refusing a foreign Host and a cross-site Origin first, with no CORS header.
  - `/api/status` and `/api/fleet` stay CORS-open. PR 11 makes `/api/status` coarse in fact, not only in name (P9, P23): `node_status.h` drops `rssi` in dBm with no word in its place (the signal moves with bodies in the room, and a word is the same reading cut at thresholds, §3.1), drops `wall_polled_s_ago` (when something last polled `/api/fleet`, stamped in `node_http.cpp`'s `handle_fleet()`; the `wall` word already says direct, hub or none), and takes `uptime_s` from `coarse_uptime.h`. No app reads either removed key. The WAP and the flagship embed the same body in their own `/api/status` (the WAP's behind a session, which from PR 8 gets it without `rssi`, §3.2; the flagship's behind the bearer), and no app reads `node_status` there either. A paired app that shows dBm reads it from the Wi-Fi status answers: the flagship's `/api/wifi/status`, behind `auth_gate` (`handle_wifi_status()` in `securacv_network.cpp`), and the WAP's `/api/wifi`. The WAP's is not owner-gated today: `handle_wifi_status()` in `canary_wap.ino` makes no auth call and answers `rssi` to any LAN host, CORS-open, and the WAP's `check_route_security.py` lists the route as documented-public. From PR 8 it gives `rssi` only on the SoftAP interface or to the bearer (§3.2).
- **Peer heartbeat (PR 11, P9, P23).** Whenever it holds a fleet key (derived from the Wi-Fi name and password, or handed by the hub), each of the three multicasts a heartbeat every 10 s to 239.83.67.86:5867 with TTL 1 (`HEARTBEAT_MS` in `peer_protocol.h`; the group, port and TTL from `fleet_beacon_udp.h`, set in `peer_link.cpp`'s `open_sock()`; the send in `send_heartbeat()`), from `peer_link_setup()` and `peer_link_tick()`, which every Sense, Vision and Sentinel build calls from its `main.cpp`. Any host on the subnet that joins the group reads it: the tag proves the sender holds the fleet key and hides nothing (it is an HMAC over the bytes before it, `peer_protocol.h`'s FRAME comment). Beside the sender's key fingerprint, its role, a flag for whether it is on its hub and the fingerprint of the member it takes as coordinator (the FRAME comment), today it carries `uptime_s`, the seconds since boot (`f.uptime_s = now_ms / 1000u` in `stamp()`); `seq`, the "per-boot counter", which starts at 0 each boot (`s_seq`) and rises by one per frame (`f.seq = ++s_seq`); since #1932, `boot_id`, a random non-zero byte drawn once per boot; and the signal in dBm (`hb.rssi = (int8_t)canary::net::wifi_rssi()` in each of the three `main.cpp` files). Nothing calls `send_event()`, and the first heartbeat goes out the moment the socket opens (`s_next_hb_ms = now_ms` in `tick()`), so `seq` counts heartbeats: one frame puts the boot about 10 s × (`seq` − 1) back, off by the time before the first heartbeat (the Wi-Fi join) and any time offline. So, whatever `/api/status` says, any host on the network can date the boot to the second from `uptime_s`, to within the join from `seq` alone, and read the signal six times a minute. Vision multicasts its fleet beacon to the same group and port, with no key (`fleet_udp.cpp`, §7.2 list 2); no PR in this plan changes that. PR 11 changes all three:
  - the `rssi` byte is sent as 0. A receiver only stores it (`get_heartbeat()` into the peer row, called by `detail::take_fields()` in `peer_protocol.h`), and nothing reads it there;
  - `uptime_s` carries `coarse_uptime.h`'s whole hours, in seconds, from the same per-boot offset as `node_status`, in both `stamp()` and `election()` (`peer_link.cpp`);
  - `seq` starts each boot at a value drawn once in `begin()`, `peer::seq_start(esp_random())` **(new, pure)**, which shifts the draw into [0, 2^31), so it cannot wrap within a boot (2^31 frames at one every 10 s is about 680 years), and it still rises by one per frame. Without this, coarse hours would hide nothing while `seq` counts heartbeats from 1;
  - `elect()` compares those values with no age term, the tie still going to the lowest fingerprint. Aging an hour-granular value by the seconds since it was heard can let two nodes in the same hour each elect the other;
  - the replay rule is #1932's now, and both changes above run into it. A row tracks a boot by `boot_id`. A frame is accepted when it advances the counter of the boot the row tracks, or of the boot before it. A frame that claims a new boot (another `boot_id`; where either side has none, a lower uptime) is taken only from that boot's first frames and seconds (`seq` at most `REBOOT_SEQ_WINDOW`, 8, and `uptime_s` under `REBOOT_UPTIME_WINDOW_S`, 120), at most once per peer in `REBOOT_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (30 s), and provisionally, until a second in-order frame confirms it. Anything else is a replay, and a reboot heard past the window is refused until its row expires, `STALE_MS` (35 s) after the row last took a frame, and is then a new peer (`accept()` and the REPLAY comment in `peer_protocol.h`). A counter that starts anywhere in [0, 2^31) is almost never 8 or less, so every reboot would be refused until the old row expires and then taken in as new. A whole-hour `uptime_s` reads 0 for a boot's first hour or more, so the 120 s limit would not close in that time. A host build of `peer_protocol.h` at `0a06c14b` (2026-11-16) showed both: a reboot whose counter starts at 1,234,567 was a replay twice, its row was gone after 35 s, and the next frame was taken as a new peer; and a frame with counter 7 and uptime 0 was taken as a reboot, where the same frame with its true uptime of 3,000 s was a replay. How PR 11 keeps #1932's window beside a counter and an uptime that do not date the boot is open (§14).

  Until every node runs PR 11's image, an older receiver still ages the coarse values, so a mixed fleet can disagree about the coordinator. The coordinator only answers the fleet's roll call and runs a staged OTA (`peer_protocol.h`'s header).
- **USB.**
  - Vision adds `q` to its `j`/`h` table (+29 B of its 1,600 B manifest; `test_self_manifest.cpp` gains Vision's worst case beside the flagship's).
  - Sense gets `j` and `q` through a new command hook in `firmware/common/console/tuning_console.h` (that console reads whole lines, so each is a one-letter line).
  - Sentinel gets a minimal reader for `j` and `q`.
  - All three hold signing keys and chains, so their manifests are real.
  - The flash catalog reads these handlers: `supports_serial_receipt()` in `canary-local/tools/gen_flash.py` decides whether a flasher asks a product for `j`, and it finds a `j` only in the shapes the flagship and Vision use today. PR 10 makes Sense's one-letter line and the WAP's staged copy shapes it reads, or the flashers go on not asking those two (§12 PR 10).
- **MQTT.** The retained details topic fits the 1,536 B buffers (§5.7).
- **Hardware tokens.** Built in PR 6 (#1971). `esp32c3-super-mini`, `xiao-esp32c6-sentinel` and `xiao-esp32c3-sentinel-lite` define `CANARY_FIGURE_HARDWARE` in their `pins.h`; `figures.json` still lists the three as unmapped hardware, so none has a figure. Each trio project's `mdns_mgr.cpp` and `ota_mgr.cpp` now include `pins.h` (before, they tested the token and never saw it), so `hw` is in mDNS and `node_status` on every Sense, Vision and Sentinel env, and the OTA engine is told the board.
  - The binding is inert today: the release workflow writes version-1 OTA manifests (`.github/workflows/firmware-release.yml` calls `ota_release.py manifest` with no `--board`). A version-1 manifest carries no board, so `securacv_ota_board_ok` has nothing to compare.
  - The token is the same string a version-2 manifest would carry, so the binding stays right when releases move to version 2.

### 5.6 The flashers, with no firmware running

Built as PR 1 (#1970; host-tested, not run on a device). Both flashers and the Lab's native bench show an **About this board** card. The row model is `aboutBoardGroups()` in `canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`, which the browser flasher and the Lab's bench (`flash-native.js`) call. `desktop/src/app.js` carries a copy, because its bundle cannot import `canary-local`, and `desktop_parity.test.js` runs both over the same fact sets. Every row sits in a group that names its source:

- **"Read from the chip".** The Chip row gives the model and its silicon revision, and the Flash row the size from the flash chip's JEDEC id, the chip's own reading. Under "More about this board" come the package, the flash and PSRAM inside the package, and whether factory calibration was written, all from the eFuse fields below. The desktop's `ChipInfo` (`desktop/flash-engine/src/flash.rs`) gained `chip_desc`, `revision`, `crystal_mhz` and `features`; `canonical_chip()` keeps its guard role.
- **"From the installed image".** From the app descriptor, already read (256 B): the firmware's project and version, the IDF version ("Built with"), `secure_version` ("Anti-rollback number") and the first 8 bytes of the ELF SHA-256 ("Image fingerprint"). From the bootloader's image header, at 0x0 on the S3, C3 and C6 and at 0x1000 on the classic ESP32: flash mode and speed, and the flash size the image was built for. That size is the image's claim, and the chip's JEDEC size can contradict it, so one row shows both ("4 MB · the chip has 8 MB"). The parsers are `parseAppDescriptor()` and `parseBootloaderHeader()` in `flash-core.js`, and `parse_app_descriptor()` and `parse_bootloader_header()` in `desktop/flash-engine/src/health.rs`.
- **"From the maker’s datasheet".** Radios, cores and crystal, listed by chip model. This design put the crystal and features on the chip line, as read. #1970 found that for the S3, C3 and C6 both ROM tools print fixed strings there and read nothing, so the rows say datasheet, and the desktop shows what espflash printed only under "Show technical names".
- **What cannot be given.** A row a flasher cannot fill says "Not checked yet", "Not checked", "Not on this chip", "Not reported" or, on the Lab's bench, which reads no fuse and no image, "Read with the Flasher" (`ABOUT_CANNOT`). It is never a zero or a guess. The classic ESP32 has no field table, so after a read its fuse rows say "Not checked". No flasher code sends the flash unique-ID command.
- **eFuse identity and SKU**, from a **per-chip field allowlist**, never a word allowlist. On these chips a single 32-bit word holds SKU fields next to per-chip calibration trims, so a word-granular rule would read and decode values that fingerprint the chip. Each allowed field is named with its block, word, bit and width from espefuse 5.6.0's `efuse_defs/<chip>.yaml`. The tables are `CHIP_FUSE_FIELDS` in `canary-local/assets/intake.js` and `ALLOWED_S3`, `ALLOWED_C3` and `ALLOWED_C6` in `desktop/src-tauri/src/efuse.rs`, and `desktop_parity.test.js` pins every (chip, key, block, word, bit, width) between them, beside the block-0 pins it already had.

  | Chip | Allowed fields (block · word · bit · width) |
  |---|---|
  | ESP32-S3 | BLOCK1 w3: `WAFER_VERSION_MINOR_LO` 18·3, `PKG_VERSION` 21·3, `BLK_VERSION_MINOR` 24·3, `FLASH_CAP` 27·3, `FLASH_TEMP` 30·2. BLOCK1 w4: `FLASH_VENDOR` 0·3, `PSRAM_CAP` 3·2, `PSRAM_TEMP` 5·2, `PSRAM_VENDOR` 7·2. BLOCK1 w5: `PSRAM_CAP_3` 19·1, `WAFER_VERSION_MINOR_HI` 23·1, `WAFER_VERSION_MAJOR` 24·2. BLOCK2 w4: `BLK_VERSION_MAJOR` 0·2. |
  | ESP32-C3 | BLOCK1 w3: `WAFER_VERSION_MINOR_LO` 18·3, `PKG_VERSION` 21·3, `BLK_VERSION_MINOR` 24·3, `FLASH_CAP` 27·3, `FLASH_TEMP` 30·2. BLOCK1 w4: `FLASH_VENDOR` 0·3. BLOCK1 w5: `WAFER_VERSION_MINOR_HI` 23·1, `WAFER_VERSION_MAJOR` 24·2. BLOCK2 w4: `BLK_VERSION_MAJOR` 0·2. |
  | ESP32-C6 | BLOCK1 w3: `WAFER_VERSION_MINOR` 18·4, `WAFER_VERSION_MAJOR` 22·2, `PKG_VERSION` 24·3, `BLK_VERSION_MINOR` 27·3, `BLK_VERSION_MAJOR` 30·2. BLOCK1 w4: `FLASH_CAP` 0·3, `FLASH_TEMP` 3·2, `FLASH_VENDOR` 5·3. (No PSRAM fields; no BLOCK2 read at all.) |

  What the allowlist excludes, so nobody reads the table as permission:

  | Chip | Read, then masked before anything is kept | Never read |
  |---|---|---|
  | ESP32-S3 | BLOCK1 w3 bits 0-17 (`SPI_PAD_CONFIG_D5-D7`); w4 `RESERVED_1_137`, `K_RTC_LDO`, `K_DIG_LDO`, `V_RTC_DBIAS20` (which runs into w5); w5 `V_DIG_DBIAS20`, `DIG_DBIAS_HVT`, `RESERVED_1_176`, `RESERVED_1_180`, `ADC2_CAL_VOL_ATTEN3`; BLOCK2 w4 `RESERVED_2_130`, `TEMP_CALIB`, `OCODE`, `ADC1_INIT_CODE_ATTEN0` and the low bits of `ADC1_INIT_CODE_ATTEN1` (41 bits of per-chip trim in BLOCK1 alone) | BLOCK1 w0-2 (the MAC and pad config); BLOCK2 w0-3 (`OPTIONAL_UNIQUE_ID`); BLOCK2 w5-7 (the rest of the ADC calibration); BLOCK3 (the custom MAC) |
  | ESP32-C3 | BLOCK1 w3 bits 0-17 (`SPI_PAD_CONFIG_D5-D7`); w4 `RESERVED_1_131`, `K_RTC_LDO`, `K_DIG_LDO`, `V_RTC_DBIAS20`, `V_DIG_DBIAS20` (which runs into w5); w5 `DIG_DBIAS_HVT`, `THRES_HVT`, `RESERVED_1_180`, `RESERVED_1_186`; BLOCK2 w4 `RESERVED_2_130`, `TEMP_CALIB`, `OCODE`, `ADC1_INIT_CODE_ATTEN0` and the low bits of `ADC1_INIT_CODE_ATTEN1` | BLOCK1 w0-2; BLOCK2 w0-3 and w5-7; BLOCK3 |
  | ESP32-C6 | BLOCK1 w3 bits 0-17 (`SPI_PAD_CONF_2`); w4 bits 8-31 (`RESERVED_1_136`) | BLOCK1 w0-2 (the MAC, `MAC_EXT` and the `*_DBIAS` trims); BLOCK1 w5; all of BLOCK2 (calibration and the unique id); BLOCK3 |

  - **Masking happens in the reader.** ROM `READ_REG` returns whole words, so the JS reader (`readChipFuses()` and `fieldsOfWord()` in `intake.js`) and the Rust one (`read_chip_fields()` and `fields_of_word()` in `efuse.rs`) reduce each word to its allowed fields as it is read and keep only those. No BLOCK1 or BLOCK2 word is stored in a struct, serialized (`EfuseScan.words` carries block 0 only, which holds no per-chip field), shown at L4, put in a Raw Record, or written to a saved report. "Show technical names" shows the allowlisted field values, never a word.
  - **"Calibration present"** comes from the block-version fields alone, as IDF's own calibration-version check does. No trim value is read into it. The row is labeled "Factory calibration".
  - **The tests flip the trims.** On each chip, a word with every masked bit set decodes exactly like one with every masked bit clear: `intake.test.mjs` holds the JS reader to that, and a test in `efuse.rs` ("a masked bit reached a field") the Rust one.
- **MAC.** **No MAC bytes on screen by default**: the row reads "Hardware address" with a "Show" button, and the reveal shows the whole address on that page only. It is never copied. The note reads "This stays on this computer."
  - **Why not even the tail.** Until #1970 both nursery strips drew the last two bytes of the base MAC, and the diagnostic report carried the same two as `macTail`. The base MAC is the station address in every Wi-Fi frame the board sends, so the tail matches the board in radio range on every family, and no address policy changes that. The other radios derive from it too. The S3 cores set two universal addresses, a Bluetooth universe and no SoftAP universe (`CONFIG_ESP_MAC_UNIVERSAL_MAC_ADDRESSES=2` in the 3.3.8 esp32s3 `sdkconfig`; 2.0.17 spells it `CONFIG_ESP32S3_UNIVERSAL_MAC_ADDRESSES=2`): Bluetooth is base + 1, and the SoftAP's BSSID is `esp_derive_local_mac()` of the base, which sets the locally administered bit in the first octet and keeps the other five bytes (its comment in `esp_mac.h`), so **on the S3 the setup BSSID's tail equals the base MAC's tail byte for byte**. The C3 and C6 set four, and so does the classic ESP32 of the flagship's `esp32cam` and `esp32-wroom` reach ports (2.0.17's `CONFIG_ESP32_UNIVERSAL_MAC_ADDRESSES=4`): the SoftAP is base + 1 and Bluetooth base + 2. The counts are each installed core's `sdkconfig`; the offsets are in IDF's `components/esp_hw_support/mac_addr.c` (`esp_read_mac()` at the release tag v4.4.7, `generate_mac()` at v5.5.4), read on 2026-11-16. Neither strip draws any of the address now. The browser's roster and the desktop's fleet book still keep it, to know a board that comes back, and the fleet book is keyed by it until PR 18 (ledger A133).
  - **No MAC bytes are written into any saved report or shared text.** That covers both report forms below and the diagnostic text: its error line is masked (`maskHardwareAddress()`), and its log-tail scrubber replaces any line that still holds a MAC-shaped token with a visible marker. A backup is different by nature: it is a byte copy of the flash, so it holds whatever the board stores.
  - **A saved report is a share projection by default.** "Save report (.json)" writes `reportShareProjection()`: chip, image and partition facts only, with no counter (lifetime boots, witness records, updates seen), no posture (a crash record, OTA pending-verify, the eFuse security scan), no chain-head prefix, no MAC bytes, and every date as a day, never a time. A second button, **"Save full report for your own records"**, writes the rest (`reportFullProjection()`, still no MAC bytes), and its note says the file describes this one board and is not for posting. Both flashers; the Lab's native bench saves no report.
  - **File names carry no MAC bytes.** Reports and backups are named by the local date and a counter, as in `canary-2026-10-22-1-backup.bin` (`fileStamp()`, `backupFileName()` and `reportFileName()`; `rescue::backup_filename(day, n)` on the desktop). `macStamp()` is gone. Both restore panels' `canary-…-backup.bin` pattern still matches.
  - Both flashers follow one rule, pinned in `desktop_parity.test.js`.
- **Grove Vision module.** The browser's bench line reads "It answered: SSCMA firmware …", from a `VER?` probe. The model is named only when the flasher itself wrote it and its SHA-256 matched `manifest-vision-model.json`: "SecuraCV person detection, installed and checked". A file the person picked reads "Your model file, installed". Neither flasher sends `ID?`, and neither shows the module's own model text (§7.8).

What #1970 left, which stays plan:

- The fleet book and the stored device keys are re-keyed by device id (PR 18; ledger A133).
- The diagnostic report's log tail still passes any line no pattern names, so it is not public-only by construction, and once firmware answers `q` each `securacv.canary.details/v1` line in it is replaced by its share projection (PR 10; ledger A132).
- By its CHANGELOG entry, #1970 built the card's groups in one column, with one "Copy these details" button and no copy per row, and a desktop connect line that names the chip, its revision and its flash. This design first drew a sidebar, a copy per row, and the PSRAM size and bus mode on that line. §6.9 now describes the card as built; the copy per row has no PR, and whether one takes it is open (§6.2, §12 PR 1).

### 5.7 Budgets in one table

| Budget | Before | After | Margin rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| WAP DEV/S3 handlers | 142 / 152 | 143 / 153 | ≥ 8 free (10) |
| WAP FULL/S3, FULL/C3 | 177 / 200, 150 / 173 | 178 / 201, 151 / 174 | ≥ 8 (23) |
| WAP route security | 203 regs, 26 public | 204, 26 | — |
| Flagship route table | worst 75 / 63 of 75 / 63 (0 spare) | 76 / 64 of 78 / 66 | 2 spare, asserted by `check_route_budgets()` from PR 14 |
| WAP `/admin` gzip | 67,638 B on this tree (67,494 B at `0a06c14b`; #1972 added 144 B), 2,102 B past 65,536 | must shrink before anything is added (the six-row DEVICE INFO block, Hardware ID row included, goes; the label region holds only the link's text) | no gate today: PR 6's budget was not built (§5.3, ledger F479). Whether PR 12's 65,536 ceiling stands is open (§14) |
| WAP companion gzip | 48,705 B at `62bc0bc7` | + About panel and its label region (est. 3-5 KB) | the 65,536 ceiling, gated from PR 12 if it stands (§14) |
| Manifest (flagship) | 1,265 B measured worst case in 1,600 (§1.2) | 1,294 B (`q` in `commands[]`, +29 B) | host test pins the worst case |
| Manifest (Vision) | 1,600 B buffer | + 29 B | host test pins Vision's worst case |
| MQTT details topic | — | PubSubClient 2.8 refuses a publish when `bufferSize < MQTT_MAX_HEADER_SIZE (5) + 2 + strnlen(topic, bufferSize) + plength` (`PubSubClient::publish()`): `about` ≤ 512 + topic ≤ 65 + 2-byte topic length + header ≤ 5 = **≤ 584 B** | under 1,536 / 1,792 / 2,048. The WAP publishes through esp_mqtt, whose buffer the sketch leaves at its default. The host test computes the bound with the +2. |
| Flash (tightest guarded, `.bin` bytes) | nightstand-c6 42,128 B free, Sense wellbeing 97,984 B (default 101,824 B), Sentinel Lite 148,416 B, the flagship's `release_ha` 199,824 B, and nightlight-c3, the other 4 MB display, 354,528 B under its old-table guard: CI's size guard on #1972's merge, whose firmware tree is this one's, 2026-11-16 (§1.2) | the measured delta of PR 9's first link | each guard green; the delta reported for every release env, guarded or not |
| BLE attribute | — | `about` ≤ 512 (the only part this carrier holds) | ATT maximum 512 |
| USB dump (flagship worst: 7 device parts, 3 index pages, about 14 sensor pages and their link views) | — | about 18.5 KB at worst-case escaping (14.4 KB without the link views), typically 4-7 KB: estimates from §4.2's caps | about 1.6 s at 115,200 baud worst; native USB CDC is faster. Readers collect line by line, so the browser flasher's 8,000-character windows (`.slice(-8000)` in `flash.js`) never lose a part. |
| RAM | — | 768 B per carrier context, about 300 B cached | stack or static, never a shared buffer across tasks |

### 5.8 What does not change

The following gain no new field, apart from the one contract-first addition named in rule 3:

- `/api/fleet`: DISCOVERY.md's coarse contract, and the kernel's `deny_unknown_fields` `FleetRow` (`src/fleet_peers.rs`). §6.8's optional `fw` is the one addition, and it goes contract-first.
- The mDNS TXT keys.
- The fleet beacon bytes.
- The chirp layout. Its hour field becomes 0 in PR 8.
- The WAP's DIS, the status service, Opera GATT, Improv `GET_DEVICE_INFO`, and the `node_status.h` keys.

No surface gains a compiled-in host, a DNS lookup or a client socket. `regression_check.sh`'s "Zero phone-home" check keeps passing unchanged.

---

## 6. Information architecture

### 6.1 Five levels, the same in every app

| Level | Name | Where | What it holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| L0 | Glance | fleet row, hive cell, Wall card, watch row, widget | Name, figure, status word. **No device details.** An "Update available" dot only where the app already knows it. |
| L1 | About | a section near the end of the device page, where Home puts accessory info | Model · Firmware (with "Up to date" or "Update available") · Chip, then **About This Canary ›** |
| L2 | About This Canary | its own page | Header (figure, name, provenance line), then This Canary, Right Now, Sensors & Radios, Details groups, Identity, What It Can Share, and a footer |
| L3 | Part page / group page | one per sensor, radio or the chip; one per group | About This Part, Health, Settings (read-only with "Change in …"), What It Can Share; or every row of a group, each with its plain explanation |
| L4 | Technical | a per-viewer **Show Technical Names** switch; a Raw Record disclosure in the desktop Flasher and the Lab only | Technical terms, raw units, bus addresses, a part's own register-level ids (a touch controller's product id, a camera PID) and cites. Never an eFuse word: the flashers keep only the allowlisted fields (§5.6). |

### 6.2 Layout grammar

- **A row is a label, a value and a copy action.**
  - iPhone and iPad: long press, Copy; ⌘C on a focused row.
  - Flashers and Lab: a copy button on hover or focus. This is not built: the About-this-board card of #1970 has one **Copy these details** button for the whole card and no per-row copy. No PR in this plan holds it; whether one takes it or this rule drops it for the flashers is open (§12 PR 1, §14).
  - tvOS and the watch have no copy.
  - A row with a held or forbidden value has no copy.
- **Monospaced type is for identifiers only.** The key fingerprint, once revealed, is grouped 4 × 4 (`7FA3 19C0 …`), in the monospaced type the views give identifiers one by one today (`UnsealView.swift`, `Text(keyID).font(.body.monospaced())`). `ios/Shared/Theme.swift` sets no identifier style and nothing in the app groups a fingerprint, so the grouping is new. Everything else is plain text, with units spelled out for VoiceOver ("8 megabytes").
- **Explanations sit one tap away.** On Apple platforms an ⓘ in each section header opens "What These Mean", built from the vocabulary's `explain` text. In the flashers the existing help dots take the same text once `gen_flash.py` reads it from the vocabulary (§2.3); today they read the `settings_help` topics of `canary-local/devices/flash.json`.
- **Provenance goes in the section footer, not on every row.** For example: "Reported by this Canary over your network, just now." A row carries its own label only where it differs from its section.
- **Live rows poll every 5 s only while visible,** and a stale value never wears present tense ("Last heard 2 minutes ago").

### 6.3 "Cannot say": seven phrases, chosen from data

| Situation | Phrase | How the app knows |
|---|---|---|
| The device sent no key | **Not reported** | key absent; device `rev` ≥ the fact's `since` |
| The firmware predates the fact | **Update to see this** | device `rev` older than the fact's `since` |
| This silicon has no such thing | **Not on this chip** | the fact's `not_on` list (for example die temperature on the classic ESP32) |
| This toolchain cannot read it | **Not available on this build** | `sources[family].state == "cannot"` |
| The part cannot be asked | **This part can't tell us** | the part kind's `identify: none` (PIR, BH1750, PDM mic, panels on write-only buses) |
| Only a USB read can see it | **Read with the Flasher** | the fact's source is `rom` |
| Above this viewer's tier | the family's phrase from §3.4 | the record's `held` count |

The flashers' About-this-board card (#1970) has no details record to choose from, so it keeps its own table, `ABOUT_CANNOT` (`canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`, copied into `desktop/src/app.js`). It uses three of these phrases as written (**Not reported**, **Not on this chip**, **Read with the Flasher**) and two of its own for a USB read: **Not checked yet** (the read has not run) and **Not checked** (the read failed, or the chip has no field table).

### 6.4 Copy Details and Share

**Copy Details** (on iPhone and iPad, in the About page's footer and its toolbar menu; a button in the flashers, the Lab and the website; none on tvOS or the watch) produces a plain-text report.

- **Built so far (#1970).** Both flashers' About-this-board card has **Copy these details**. `aboutBoardText()` writes `About this board · <app>`, then each group's source line and its rows: chip, installed-image and datasheet facts. It leaves the hardware address out. It carries no vocabulary `rev`, because no details record exists yet to copy from. The Lab's native bench has no copy button.
- **Contents.** It carries `build` and `unit` facts only, the vocabulary `rev` and the app version.
- **Never included.**
  - counter, posture, activity or household facts;
  - any handle: the key fingerprint, `hw_token` or `device_id`. The fingerprint is not a private value. Until PR 4 empties it (P17), the WAP's Device Information Service serves it in full as its serial before pairing (`canary_wap.ino` hands `g_device.fingerprint_hex` to `bluetooth_channel::set_device_metadata()`, and `bluetooth_channel.cpp` registers it as `serial_for_dis`), a FULL WAP that holds an opera sends it in full in every opera frame (§7.2 list 1), and its last two bytes are on air for good in the BLE name and the beacons. So a posted report that carries any of it lets a stranger in radio range match the device. It is copied only by its own explicit **Copy Fingerprint** button in Identity (§6.5), never inside Copy Details;
  - a MAC, a host name or an unknown key.
- **First line.** It names its provenance in words the shared text can earn. The recipient cannot check a signature over a re-rendered subset, and anyone can type "Verified", so shared text never says it. It says either "Reported by this Canary" or "Reported by this Canary · signature checked by SecuraCV <app> <version> on <day>". The second form appears only when every part the text draws from verified (§8), and it names the parts that were checked.
- **Same bytes everywhere.** The projection's rules go in the vocabulary, and golden vectors make every app's report byte-identical for the same input. The vectors pin the provenance wording too.
- **Diagnostic reports.** The flashers' shareable diagnostic report is a share projection too.
  - **Built.** Since before #1970, `sanitizeLogTail()` (`canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`, mirrored in `desktop/src/app.js`) has scrubbed the last 12 lines before they go into the report: a credential line becomes `[redacted: credential line]` (`TAIL_REDACT`). #1970 (PR 1) added `TAIL_IDENTITY`: a line that names this one board, or a household peer, becomes a visible marker, `[identity line: shown in the flasher, not in reports]`. The `TAIL_IDENTITY` list names those lines: the `securacv.canary.manifest/` line (it carries the fingerprint, `device_id` and `boots`), the "Hardware ID" boot line (P14), and device-id, key-fingerprint, `.local`-name, hardware-address and peer-name lines among others. The manifest marker landed first because the desktop's report reads the serial monitor's text when its flash console is empty (`copyDiagnosticReport()`, `$("serial-console")`), and its monitor's manifest button sends `j` (`$("monitor-manifest")`).
  - **Still open (ledger A132).** A line no pattern names passes, so the tail is not public-only by construction.
  - **PR 10.** Every `securacv.canary.details/v1` line is replaced with its share projection, because serial-tier lines carry counters, posture and activity.

### 6.5 iPhone

- **L0.**
  - The fleet row in `FleetView.swift` (`WitnessRow`) gains a context menu, the app's first, with one item, **About This Canary**.
  - Widgets, the Lock Screen, the Live Activity, notifications and complications carry no device details by design. Their code is not all in their own directories: both fleet-glance providers (`FleetGlanceProvider`, `PhoneFleetGlanceProvider`) draw `FleetGlanceWidgetView` from `ios/Shared/FleetGlanceViews.swift` ("compiled into BOTH widget targets"), the Live Activity's state is `ios/Shared/FleetActivityAttributes.swift`, and the notification extension compiles one Shared file, `ios/Shared/WakePayload.swift`. The rule is scoped by **source path**, not by target: `ios/project.yml` compiles `- path: Shared` wholesale into `SecuraCVWidgets` and `SecuraCVWatchWidgets`, so a target-scoped rule would fail on the generated vocabulary the first time it ran. The lint refuses any details key literal, and any reference to `DeviceDetailsVocab`, under `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWidgets/`, `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWatchWidgets/` and `ios/Sources/SecuraCVNotificationService/`, and in those three Shared files. The generated `ios/Shared/DeviceDetailsVocab.swift` is on none of those paths, so it needs no exemption. A planted widget file and a planted Shared glance view that use a key must each fail it. One input is not free of them: three widget providers read a `WristSnapshot`, the two fleet-glance providers (`WristCache.load()`, `PhoneGlanceCache.load()`) and the watch's Find a Canary complication (`FindCanaryProvider` in `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWatchWidgets/FindCanaryWidget.swift`), and the watch's notification long-look draws a row from `WristCache.load()` too (`WitnessNotificationController.didReceive` in `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWatch/WitnessNotification.swift`, in the watch app target, which none of those paths covers). Those rows already hold the board token as `hardware` and will hold §6.7's `firmware`, so for these surfaces the rule is held where a row is drawn. The glance views draw only fleet-wide fields today (`headline`, `healthy`, `total`), the complication draws one row's `name`, and the long-look draws a row's name, its chain badge and a figure it resolves with the row's `hardware` (L0's figure). A row's property is not a key literal, so the lint also refuses a read of a row's `firmware` in `FleetGlanceViews.swift`, `FindCanaryWidget.swift` and `WitnessNotification.swift`, and a read of a row's `hardware` in the first two.
- **L1.** `DeviceDetailView.swift` gains an **About** section before History:
  - Model (product name through `DeviceNaming`);
  - Firmware (the version, plus an update word where the app already reads the OTA state. Since #1896 `FirmwareUpdateSection` reads `/api/ota/status` when the page appears, for every display and for a paired WAP, and says "Up to date" or "Version … is ready". The app reads no OTA state for any other family, so their row carries the version alone);
  - Chip ("ESP32-S3 · v0.2");
  - **About This Canary ›**.

  The Health section's Firmware row moves here, and the "Wi-Fi dBm" row stays in Health. The Birth Certificate card at the top of the page (`BirthCertificateCard.swift`) shows a Firmware row of its own, and the key fingerprint in full; item 6 of L2 below says what that leaves open.
- **L2: `DeviceAboutView` (new, `ios/Sources/SecuraCV/Views/Details/`).** An inset-grouped `List`:
  1. **Header.** Small figure, name, and a provenance capsule: "Reported just now" or "Last heard 2 minutes ago" (iOS has no USB serial, `CanarySetupView.swift`; a USB read is the flashers', §6.9 and §6.10). The header never says "Verified": the page mixes parts fetched at different moments, and verification is earned per section (§8).
  2. **This Canary.** Model, Board, Firmware, Chip, Memory ("512 KB built in · 8 MB extra"), Storage ("8 MB flash"). Board is the board's name, never a figure's title: `FleetFigure.forHardware(_:)` (`ios/Shared/FleetFigures.swift`) maps the `hw` token to a product's figure, so `board:seeed_xiao_esp32s3` would read "Canary WAP", and nothing under `ios/` maps a token to a board's name today. The names are the board registry's `name` (`firmware/boards/boards.json`); which output carries them to the app, and how the WAP's token, which is not a registry `id`, finds its row, is open for the maintainer.
  3. **Right Now.** This is live, polled in `.task` only while visible, never from `body`. Rows: Up for ("at least 3 days"), Chip Temperature ("44 °C, the chip, not the room"), Free Memory; Wi-Fi (a word), Lowest Free Memory and Last Restart ("Power was connected") only at the owner tier, because the signal moves with the room (activity) and the low-water mark and the reset cause are posture. Elsewhere each shows the held phrase. Footer: "Live while this screen is open."
  4. **Sensors & Radios.** One `NavigationLink` per part, with an SF Symbol from the vocabulary. A trailing word appears only when there is something to say. A live runtime word from the record ("Not responding" in warning color) takes precedence; otherwise the static ladder word for a part this firmware does not drive ("Built, waiting on a bench check" or "On the board, not used by this firmware", in neutral). Each opens `PartDetailView` **(new)**.
  5. **Details.** Chip ›, Memory & Storage ›, Software ›, Radios ›, Protection ›. Each opens `DetailGroupView` **(new)**. Protection shows posture at the owner tier, which the paired WAP or flagship earns with its bearer; elsewhere it shows the held phrase.
  6. **Identity**, from the pairing record and never from details:
     - the key fingerprint, **collapsed to "Key fingerprint · Show"**: no digits and no randomart until tapped. The reveal shows it in full, grouped 4 × 4, with its randomart, the same reveal pattern the flashers have used for the hardware address since #1970. It has an explicit **Copy Fingerprint** button (the `UnsealView.swift` precedent, its **Copy public key** button) and is never part of Copy Details (§6.4). Nothing derived from it shows collapsed, because a short form still matches: until PR 4 a WAP serves the whole fingerprint to anyone nearby as its Device Information Service serial (`canary_wap.ino`, `bluetooth_channel::set_device_metadata(FIRMWARE_VERSION, g_device.fingerprint_hex)`; read with no security flag, `register_dis()` in `ble_standard_profiles.h`), so even four hex digits pick one device out of the few in range; the BLE name, the chirp and the fleet beacon carry its last two bytes (`firmware/common/fleet_link/fleet_beacon.h`, "last 2 bytes of the pubkey fingerprint"); and randomart is a visual hash of the whole fingerprint, made for matching. One question is open for the maintainer: the app shows the whole fingerprint in two places today, the device page's Birth Certificate card (`BirthCertificateCard.swift`, its "Fingerprint" row) and the Keys tab's Pinned trust list (`KeysView.swift`), and this design does not say whether those rows collapse too. Until they do, the collapsed row hides nothing the app already shows;
     - the Hardware ID, at L4 only, from the provisioning receipt, never in Copy Details.
  7. **What This Canary Can Share ›**: §7.2's lists for this family, generated.
  8. **Footer.** The provenance legend; the held line; **Copy Details**; **Show Technical Names**. The switch is a per-viewer `@AppStorage` convenience. Every SecuraCV target already declares its UserDefaults use in `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`, which `lint_apple_plists.py` holds.
- **L3: `PartDetailView` (new).** About This Part (part, maker, inside, connected by, firmware on the part, identity check), Health, Settings, and What It Can Share (§7.1).
- **Rules the screens keep.**
  - The authed `DeviceAPI` resolves in `.task`, never in `body`. `ios/Sources/SecuraCV/Views/DeviceDetailView.swift` breaks this today, in two places: `try? store.devices.api(for: ref)` sits in `var body` where it mounts `SensingNowSection` and where it mounts a paired WAP's `FirmwareUpdateSection` (#1896). Each call reaches `DeviceStore.token(for:)` (`DeviceStore.swift`) and then `TokenStore.token(for:)` (`Keychain.swift`), a Keychain read on the render path. Yet `lint_ios_keychain_reads.py` exits 0 on this tree, because it derives readers only from `static func`s and its docstring says it cannot see "a reader reached through an instance". PR 5 fixes the view and extends the lint: instance methods that reach a reader (`DeviceStore.token(for:)`, `DeviceStore.api(for:)`) join the derived set, with a planted case in `scripts/tests/test_lint_ios_keychain_reads.py`. The other `api(for:)` and `token(for:)` calls in views on this tree sit in action functions the lint's rule already allows (`DeviceDetailView.verifyNow()`, which since #1972 also calls `store.devices.token(for:)` directly, and `FindCanaryView.chirp()`); the `api(for:)` calls in `UnsealView.swift` sit in methods of `UnsealModel`, a class, and the lint's rule covers only a `struct` that conforms to `View`. Anything else the extended lint finds is fixed in the same PR.
  - Each row is one accessibility element ("Chip, ESP32-S3"). Identifiers are spelled character by character.
  - Static facts are cached in memory, keyed by device id, firmware and `rev`. These screens write no device fact to `UserDefaults` or to a CloudKit record. No gate refuses one yet: `lint_cloudkit_container.py` only makes a new CloudKit field visible, because every field the app writes must be listed in `ios/scripts/cloudkit_schema.sh` (today the pairing list's five fields, from `CloudSync.swift`, and a wake's `sev`), and no lint reads what goes into `UserDefaults` (`lint_apple_plists.py` checks only that a target using it declares it). The watch's `WristCache` already keeps each wrist row's board token in its app group's defaults, and §6.7's firmware will land there with it.
- **Sources.**
  - A firmware that serves `/api/details` is read there, with the paired bearer for a WAP or flagship and with nothing for a display or the trio.
  - Older firmware goes through hand-written legacy adapters, which render every `known_wrong` value (§1.4) as "cannot say":
    - WAP: `/api/status` and `/api/system`, read with the paired bearer (their gate, `api_auth_check`, also takes a dashboard session).
    - Flagship: `/api/status`, `/api/diagnostics`, `/api/thermal`.
    - Display: `/api/status`, `/api/device`.
    - Trio: `/api/status`, mDNS TXT `fw`/`model`/`hw`.

    Contract-belt tests pin each adapter by reading the producer by `#filePath`. Every producer a belt reads joins `ios-selfheal.yml`'s `pull_request` path list in the same PR (that workflow has no `push` list), because a producer-only edit must still compile the Swift that is held to it.
  - The same PR fixes:
    - the BLE console decoder to the WAP's real keys. `BLESnapshot` (`BLEConsole.swift`) decodes `id`, `sev`, `link`, `batt`, `health`, `muted` and `tamper`, and of those the WAP's `ble_console.cpp` writes only `id`. The app does not dial a WAP's console today (ledger A65), and a console's `id` is only its own claim until it proves identity with a nonce signed by the pinned key (`BLEConsole.swift`, since #1972), so the corrected decoder adds nothing to a paired device's Details until both are settled;
    - TXT `fw` carried to fleet rows, so unpaired displays get a firmware row. `Discovery.swift` already reads `txt["fw"]` into `DiscoveredCanary.firmware`, and no row takes it: a `lan:` row is built from `/api/fleet`, which carries no firmware (`FleetMerge.provisionalWitness()`), and `FleetStore.swift`'s advert fold ("Fill gaps, never replace") fills only the type and the hardware, for a row whose id is the advert's device id;
    - the WAP Wi-Fi row from `/api/wifi`'s `rssi`, read with the bearer (`node_status` carries no signal from PR 11);
    - Improv `deviceInfo` shown in setup.

### 6.6 iPad

- The same views run in the `NavigationSplitView` detail column; there is one implementation.
- At regular width, the top of About shows two summary cards (Chip, Right Now), and "What It Can Share" opens as an `.inspector` beside a part page.
- Values get `.textSelection(.enabled)`, and `.help()` shows provenance on pointer hover.
- There is no Mac Apple target (`ios/project.yml` sets `SUPPORTS_MACCATALYST: NO`, and neither Apple project file names a macOS platform or sets `SUPPORTS_MAC_DESIGNED_FOR_IPHONE_IPAD`); on the Mac, Details live in the desktop Flasher and the Lab (§6.9, §6.11).

### 6.7 Apple Watch

- `WitnessDetailView` gains one row, Firmware, plus "More on iPhone".
- `WristWitness` gains one additive optional, `firmware: String?`, which changes only on update. Model already resolves from `publishedType` and `hardware` (`DeviceNaming.productName(published:hardware:)`). The board token rides as `hardware`, and its name waits on the board-name source §6.5 leaves open.
- No counters, uptime or heap ride the snapshot (the push-dedup rule in `WristSnapshot.swift`), and the 24-row cap is untouched.

### 6.8 Apple TV (a shared screen in a living room)

- `DeviceDetailView` shows **Model** from the `product` and `hw` that `/api/fleet` already carries, and **Board** once the board-name source §6.5 leaves open reaches the Wall. Today `hw` maps only to a product and a figure (`DeviceNaming.productName(published:hardware:)` and `FleetFigure.resolve(...)` in `WitnessCore.swift`), never to a board's name.
- It shows **Firmware** only after `/api/fleet` gains an optional `fw`. `fw` is build class and already public in mDNS TXT. The change goes contract-first: `tvos/discovery/DISCOVERY.md` → the vectors → `tvos/witness-core/src/fleet.rs` → `WitnessCore.swift` → `fleet_selfreport.h`.
- Nothing unit, counter or posture ever shows on the TV, and there is no drill-down: "More about this Canary on your iPhone."

### 6.9 Desktop Flasher (`desktop/src`)

- **Connected, before flashing.** Built in #1970: a **This board** card (`renderThisBoard()` in `desktop/src/app.js`), a spec grid of an uppercase key over a monospace value. Every value is set in monospace (`.this-board .tb-v` in `desktop/src/styles.css`), though §6.2 keeps monospace for identifiers; the browser's values (`.flash-fact-val`, `.flash-report-val` in `canary-local/assets/flash.css`) are plain, with only the revealed address in monospace (`.flash-mono`). Whether the desktop card follows §6.2 or the rule makes room for it is open (§12 PR 1, §14).
  - Its first rows are Chip, Flash and "Hardware address · Show" (§5.6).
  - **More about this board** folds out three groups, each under the line that says where its rows came from: "Read from the chip" (the chip, the JEDEC flash size and the allowlisted eFuse fields), "From the installed image" (the app descriptor and the bootloader header, §5.6) and "From the maker’s datasheet" (radios, processor, crystal). The fold also holds the **Show technical names** checkbox (L4) and **Copy these details** (§6.4).
  - There is no sidebar: the groups sit in the fold.
  - The eFuse rows read "Not checked yet" until the person presses **Read the chip’s fuses**, because that read leaves the board in download mode, and the card says so beside the button.
  - After `identify()` reads the chip, the connect line reads `Connected · ESP32-S3 v0.2 · 8 MB flash on <port>` (`aboutBoardSummary()`); while the monitor runs it reads `Connected · ESP32-S3 on <port>` (`trackPresenceWhileMonitoring()`). Neither carries an address or PSRAM; in-package PSRAM is a row in the fold.
  - The health card shows "Hardware address · Show" where it showed "ID (MAC)".
- **After flashing, or in the monitor.** A second section, **From the Board Itself**, from `q` lines (PR 10). `desktop/flash-engine/src/monitor.rs` gains a `serial:details` event for `securacv.canary.details/v1` lines, labeled "Reported"; today it emits `serial:status`, `serial:log`, `serial:receipt` and `serial:vision`. A display in first-boot setup answers no `q` until PR 17, and the section says so (§5.4).
- **Fleet tab.** **Details ›** per book entry, through a new `action_route` entry, `"details"` (PR 18). The frontend picks the part from a closed enum and never names a path (`action_route()` in `desktop/src-tauri/src/fleet.rs`, "not a path proxy"). The call carries the stored bearer the way every authed fleet-book call does, sealed to the board's identity key (`desktop/src-tauri/src/seal.rs`; `fleet.rs` keeps no plain-bearer path), so the owner tier. Only the canary-wap serves that handshake today (`/api/device/seal`), so one question is open for the maintainer: how the drawer reaches a flagship's owner tier. It shows a **Raw Record** disclosure (L4). It says "Verified" per section, only where PR 21's signature checks against the `pubkeyFp` the book holds for that entry (§8): the one in the boot receipt after a flash, or, for an entry that has none, the first key the device proved (`probeIdentityFor()`).
- **The MAC.** Built in #1970. No bytes show by default: the connect line and the health card's chip note carry none, the This board card and the health card show "Hardware address · Show" (`macRevealRow()`), and the fleet list and the session's nursery strip draw none. The reveal shows the whole address on that screen, with no copy button. The saved health report is the share projection (`reportShareProjection()`) unless the person picks **Save full report for your own records** (`reportFullProjection()`), and neither carries the address (§5.6). Reports and backups are named with the local date and a counter (`fileStamp()`, `reportFileName()`, `backupFileName()`; `rescue::backup_filename` takes a day and a counter), and `macStamp()` is gone.
- **Book keys.** The fleet book and the keychain item are re-keyed by device id once it is known, with a one-time migration (PR 18, ledger A133). Today `bookKey()` uses the MAC when the chip reported one, and the stored key is named by it too (`secretStore.key("canary", entry.mac || entry.deviceId, "token")`).

### 6.10 Browser flasher (`canary-local/assets`)

- It shows the same rows, order, words and share text, written in its own code: the two flashers share data, never UI code. Since #1970 the rows come from one model, `aboutBoardGroups()` in `canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`. The desktop keeps a copy of it between the `BEGIN ABOUT-BOARD` and `END ABOUT-BOARD` markers in `desktop/src/app.js`, and each flasher draws the rows in its own DOM code.
- L1 is the hello card (Chip, Flash, "Hardware address · Show"). **More about this board** is L2. The **Show technical names** checkbox is L4. All three are built (#1970). The serial monitor's identity card gains Details from the `q` lines (PR 10).
- Built in #1970: the hello card and the Board report (`renderReport()`) show "Hardware address · Show" (`macRevealFact()`) where they showed "ID (MAC)". The health report object holds no address. The default save is the share projection, and **Save full report for your own records** writes the rest, still with no address. The report and backup names come from `fileStamp()`, the local date and a counter, and `macStamp()` is gone.
- `canary-local/tests/desktop_parity.test.js` pins, since #1970:
  - the About-this-board row sets, their words and their order;
  - the cannot-say phrases of `ABOUT_CANNOT` (§6.3);
  - the eFuse **field** allowlists (key, block, word, bit, width) between `intake.js` and `efuse.rs`. The trim-flip decode is held on each side by its own test: `canary-local/tests/intake.test.mjs` ("a masked bit reached the decode") and `efuse.rs` ("a masked bit reached a field");
  - `TAIL_IDENTITY` (since #1970) and `TAIL_REDACT` (pinned before it), the same lists on both sides, scrubbed in each report builder;
  - the MAC rule: no bytes on screen by default, none in any saved file, report or file name;
  - the saved-report projections, equal on both sides: the default save carries no counter, posture, chain prefix or instant, and the full local save is labeled as such (`flash.test.js` holds the browser's own, "a saved report is a share projection unless the full local save is asked for");
  - the copy text (`aboutBoardText()`) between the two;
  - `flash-native.js` beside `app.js`: the same About-this-board rows (§6.11).
- PR 10 adds to it: `sanitizeLogTail()`'s details rule, fed real `q` fixture lines; the share-text vectors between the two; and the From the Board Itself rows on all three frontends.

### 6.11 Lab (`canary-local` pages and `desktop-lab`)

- **Board Room.** "What this board can report", built from the vocabulary, the board registry (`firmware/boards/boards.json`) and the manifests, labeled "From the board registry" or "From the maker's datasheet". An idea-tier board shows nothing as fact. With no device attached it shows only the static ladder words of §7.1 ("Supported by this firmware", "Supported in bench builds only", "Built, waiting on a bench check", "On the board, not used by this firmware"), never the runtime "Working", and it says which boards are compile-tested only (the registry's `tier` and `tier_evidence`). The Board Room does not read that registry today: the catalog it fetches, `canary-local/devices/boards.json`, carries no tier.
- **Emulator bench.** Every live value is labeled "Emulated". The shim stops claiming an ESP32-S3 with 2 cores and 8 MB for the C3 nightlight (`EmuESP` in `canary-local/emulator/shim/Arduino.h`, whose `getChipModel()` returns "ESP32-S3 (emulated)" for every flavor) and reports each flavor's own MCU, suffixed "(emulated)" (dist PR).
- **`desktop-lab`.** It holds no tokens, so it reads only the lan tier from displays and the trio.
- **Native USB bench (`canary-local/assets/flash-native.js`).** It is a third flasher frontend: it runs the Flasher's engine commands (`detect_chip`, `start_serial_monitor`, the `serial:*` events), and its header promises that every diagnostic the Flasher's frontend "gives on this path is given here too", which `desktop_parity.test.js` pins ("native flashing: the Lab's flash page gives the Flasher's diagnostics, and serial lights only where it works"). Since #1970 it has PR 1's About-this-board rows, drawn from `flash-core.js`'s `aboutBoardGroups()` and fed the same `detect_chip` answer (`ChipInfo`). The Lab app exposes no fuse read and no installed-image read, so the eFuse and installed-image rows say "Read with the Flasher". PR 10's From the Board Itself rows (`serial:details`) are still to come, written in its own code. It stores the MAC from `detect_chip` (`st.mac = info.mac`) and shows none, and keeps showing none.

### 6.12 Home Assistant (`custom_components/securacv`)

- **Device page.**
  - **Built in #1891 (PR 2).** Firmware comes from the health topic's `firmware_version`, and from the status topic until a health has reported one. Model comes from the status topic's `device_type` through the product-name table `CANARY_PRODUCT_NAMES` (`const.py`), which `test_device_registry_versions.py` holds to `DeviceNaming.swift`'s table; a type that is absent or not in the table reads the constant `MODEL_CANARY`. The table is not generated from the vocabulary yet. Every value is a `str`, and broker text is clamped to 64 characters (`broker_text()`).
  - **PR 16.** Hardware (the board's name) and Model ID (the board token) come from the details topic. The `about` part carries the token alone (§4.2), so the name is looked up from it, by the board-name source §6.5 leaves open. Until then the page's Hardware is whatever the status topic names as `hardware` or `board`, and no firmware in this tree puts either key on its MQTT status, so the row stays empty.
  - **Serial number.** The integration sets no `serial_number` on a Canary's device (`entity.py`, "No serial number is set on a Canary's device"), and the vocabulary is to record why. It does set one on the kernel device, from the kernel's own device id (`kernel_device_info()`), which this design leaves alone.
- **Download diagnostics, per device.** A new `async_get_device_diagnostics` (PR 16) returns two **projections**, never a raw payload. `diagnostics.py`'s own rule, in its module docstring, is that "nothing raw is ever copied in", because dumps get attached to public issues.
  - The broker-tier `about` part, projected through the vocabulary's share projection (build and unit facts only).
  - The last health, projected the way `_canary_shape()` projects status: firmware, hardware, online, and the status words from the vocabulary's share projection. Uptime is omitted (or given in whole days). There are no counters and no `activity` values. The raw health bodies carry uptime to the second (`publish_health_retained()` in `canary-sense/src/net/mqtt_mgr.cpp`, `publish_health()` in the WAP's `csi_mqtt.cpp`), the flagship's `boot_count`, `sd_writes` and `http_requests` (`mqtt_publish_health_update()` in `firmware/canary/src/main.cpp`), and the radar's `last_frame_age_ms`. Since #1916 all five keys are in `TO_REDACT`, so those five would come out redacted if a raw health body ever reached a dump. Its other counters and posture would not (the flagship's `records_created`, `chain_persists`, `verify_failures`, `sd_errors` and `min_heap` among them), so the projection is what keeps them out.
  - `TO_REDACT` is the second net, and it is built. #1891 (PR 2) added `hw_token`, `mdns_host`, `serial_number`, `chip_id`, `module_id`, `local_address`, `address` and `peer_address`. #1916 added `uptime`, `boot_count`, `sd_writes`, `http_requests` and `last_frame_age_ms`, ahead of PR 16. #1961 made the set derived: `TO_REDACT` is `INTEGRATION_REDACT_KEYS` (the integration's own keys, with `unique_id`, `serial_number` and `chip_id` among them) plus `REDACTED_DEVICE_KEYS`, every key that `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json` puts in a redacted class, read from the generated `custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py`. Six other uptime spellings a Canary sends (`uptime_s`, `uptime_ms`, `uptime_sec`, `bucket_uptime_s`, the display `/api/device`'s `up_s` and the WAP Bluetooth console snapshot's `up`) are in no redacted class yet; which class each takes is an open decision (ledger HA63).
  - `test_the_redaction_set_names_every_identifier_class` names those thirteen keys and asserts every key of `REDACTED_DEVICE_KEYS`, and `test_the_redaction_set_is_the_integration_s_keys_and_the_vocabulary_s` holds the union, so the set stays derived (#1961). Still to write, with PR 16: a test that projects the health bodies of the flagship, the WAP and a Sense and asserts that none of those keys or values survives. `tests/fixtures/canary_mqtt_payloads.json` has held one health body per family, in the keys its firmware publishes, since #1891.
- **No new entities in the first version.** If chip temperature or last restart are wanted later, they arrive as diagnostic entities that are disabled by default, with names from `translation_key` and icons from `icons.json`.
- **Trust.** Values carry the existing unsigned trust attributes until PR 21's signature verifies. A retained MQTT part that verifies reads "Signed by this Canary · not proven current", never plain "Verified" (§8).
- **The mirror.** The HACS mirror receives all of this only through the sync workflow, which copies `custom_components/securacv/` and the root `conftest.py` (`homeassistant-mirror.yml`, its `rsync -a --delete` and `cp conftest.py _mirror/conftest.py`). The mirror's CI runs `pytest custom_components/securacv/tests` with no `spec/`, so every test here reads its fixtures from `custom_components/securacv/tests/fixtures/` (the generated copies of §2.3 land there), never from `spec/`. No sibling-repo change is needed.
- **Fingerprint truncation.** Gone since #1891 (PR 2; P19, decided in §14). The config-entry dump kept 8 of the 16 hex digits of each Canary's fingerprint, and a WAP serves the full value to anyone nearby until PR 4. The dump now keeps no digit: each pinned Canary's `trust` row carries `key_correlation_id`, a keyed hash of the fingerprint under a salt drawn for that one dump and never written to it, spelled in letters that are not hex digits (`_correlation_ids()`). `test_no_digit_of_a_canary_fingerprint_reaches_the_dump` holds it.

### 6.13 Website (`securacv_website`)

- The `/canary` page sends `q` over WebSerial after the manifest and renders **About This Canary** with labels from the carried `device_details.json`. The data never leaves the page, and the page's own code is what keeps it there: the site's CSP is not self-only. Its `/*` rule in `_headers` allows `connect-src` to the firmware repo's GitHub hosts and the store beside `'self'` (read at the website's `main`, `f70961a`, on 2026-11-16).
- `js/serial.js` gains `findDetails()`. The reader keeps reading after the manifest line: today `readManifest()` returns as soon as `findManifest()` finds a manifest line in the buffer, so the change is in `readManifest()`'s loop.
- Product pages may render "What it can share" from the same file, under the five-things rule for a new page. With no device attached they show only the static ladder words (§7.1), never "Working".
- `scripts/lib/glossary.mjs` gains "Details record" and "Device details vocabulary".

### 6.14 The Canary's own pages and glass

Every on-device page and the glass take their labels from the generated outputs of §2.3, never typed in the page:

- **WAP:** `/companion#about` (lan tier), and `/admin` "About this Canary ›" plus Protection when signed in with the token (§5.3; since #1887 that page's sign-in checks a pasted token by itself).
- **Flagship:** the Settings › Device card is a restart button today (`securacv_webui.cpp`, the card titled "Device" with the subtitle "Restart the Canary"). It gains an About panel, owner tier (the page holds the bearer).
- **Display mirror page:** its "About this device" card reads `/api/device` today and moves to `/api/details` with the generated labels (PR 11). Its "wall panel" and "dB" errors are already gone (#1971): the Kind comes from the device type `/api/status` names, and the signal reads as the glass's own word.
- **Glass:** Settings › About shows Name, Model, Firmware, Chip, Up For, Network (a word), each part's status word, Last Restart, and Protection. The glass is physical, so it carries posture and activity.

---

## 7. Parts: details, deep settings, and "What it can share"

### 7.1 How a part sheet earns its claims

Each `part_kinds` row in the vocabulary holds three lists. The rows are planned (PR 3): the file #1961 built holds wire keys and no part sheet yet (§2.2). Every row cites a file and a symbol (`path#token`), not a line number, so cites do not drift; the lint greps for the token.

- **`can`**: what the part can do, labeled "From the maker's datasheet". The flag `bench: true` is set only where `boards.json` tier evidence or a bench doc backs it.
- **`out`**: what this Canary shares. Each row has `{text, to[], status, cite}`. `to` uses the destination chips the apps draw:
  - *stays on the device*, *your network*, *paired apps*, *hub*, *Home Assistant*, *Apple Home*, *anyone nearby*, *USB*.

  The lint cross-checks each destination against a contract:

  | Destination | Must name |
  |---|---|
  | `apple_home` | a `homekit_projection` signal in the witness dictionary |
  | `home_assistant` | an entity in the generated `canary-local/devices/homeassistant.json` |
  | `nearby` | a fleet-beacon or chirp field |

  So copy cannot claim a share no contract carries.
- **`stays`**: what never leaves, each row tied to its enforcement point (a chokepoint, a barrier, a `never` row).

The **status** of a part on a given board is earned (§10) on the repo's own ladder (`docs/hardware/board_capability_map_43b.md`, "The honesty rule that governs this board"). Each rung has its own evidence:

| Rung | Evidence the lint requires | Static copy (no device needed) |
|---|---|---|
| **Driven** | The family glue `put`s that part's status key in a **shipped configuration** of that board (below) | "Supported by this firmware" |
| **Driven (bench only)** | As Driven, but only in configurations no release ships | "Supported in bench builds only" |
| **Built · bench-gated** | A driver the board's own build can compile: the part sheet's `path#token` cite resolves in a source that board's env compiles; a `FEATURE_*` flag gating it is 0 in the board's shipped configuration; and a build env compiles it with the flag on for that board, with its own pins and part selection (for SD on the 4.3B, `canary-display-dash-sd`) | "Built, waiting on a bench check" |
| **Staged** | The board's pins header declares the part (`HAS_<X> 1`), and no driver for it is compiled, or can be compiled, from the sources that board's env builds, or no build env compiles one with the flag on for that board (the Built rung's last test). A driver this board's configuration compiles out (the QMI8658 reader in `display_1in47.cpp` sits under `#ifdef CD_NIGHTLIGHT`, which only the nightlight's config defines; the Touch 1.69 builds that file without it, and the AMOLED 2.41 builds `display_amoled241.cpp` instead) or one that refuses this board's flavor (`sd_archive.cpp` `#error`s outside the dash and AMOLED flavors) counts as none for this board | "On the board, not used by this firmware" |
| **Absent** | The silicon is not populated | row omitted |

The rung names are the map's, but the lint earns each verdict itself, and the two can differ. The map rates the 4.3B's microSD "Staged (CS blocker)", for an SPI driver that cannot drive a chip-select held by the CH422G expander, while `sd_archive.cpp` mounts that slot over 1-bit SDMMC and `canary-display-dash-sd` compiles it, which the rule above rates "Built, waiting on a bench check" (§7.5).

**Shipped configurations.** Only `canary-display` has `release_envs` in `firmware/flavors.json`. The flagship, WAP, Vision and Sense entries carry none (they hold `build_envs`, `check_env`, `size_guards` and build helpers), `canary-sentinel` is marked `unreleased` ("no release_envs"; `flavor_envs.py` refuses `unreleased` beside `release_envs`, and `scripts/tests/test_flavor_envs.py` holds that), and the other families release from their own steps in `firmware-release.yml`. So the ladder and the disclosure lists read a resolver, `firmware/scripts/shipped_configs.py` **(new)**, which names per family every configuration a release publishes, from the release's own declarations:

- the published OTA manifests, through `check_ota_channels.published_manifests()` (the release's variant index), and the flasher catalog's products (`canary-local/devices/flash.json`, whose compile-tested reach ports are installable too);
- each mapped to its build: the flagship's `release_ha`, the one flagship image the packaging step publishes and the one `build_flash_manifest.py` maps `securacv-canary` to (the job builds `release` too and publishes nothing from it), and the reach-port envs `esp32cam`, `esp32-wroom` and `freenove-s3` (flasher only); the WAP's arduino-cli build of `canary_wap.ino` on the S3 FQBN (the step "Build canary-wap (arduino-cli)" in `firmware-release.yml`), whose `FEATURE_*` flags `check_route_budget.py` already resolves as FULL/S3; Vision's `canary-vision-default`, `-xiao-c3` and `-xiao-s3`, and its reach-port env `canary-vision-c3-super-mini` (flasher only, like the flagship's three); Sense's `canary-sense-default` and `canary-sense-wellbeing`; the displays' `flavor_envs.ordered_release_envs()` plus the Arduino-built flavors named in the signing loop's header.

Before a family's glue reports a part (PR 15), the Driven evidence is the part's driver compiled into a shipped configuration with its gating flag on; from PR 15 the lint also requires the glue's `put`. So PR 3 can seed every rung without waiting for the writer.

A product the resolver cannot map fails it, by name; it never guesses. No family gains `release_envs` for this: `flavor_envs.py` documents it as the envs the release workflows build and publish for the display, `check_ota_channels.py` reads it with that meaning (through `ordered_release_envs()`), and `gen_firmware_sbom.py` records it as written. `lint_build_matrix.py` reads the `unreleased` field, never `release_envs`. **Sentinel has no shipped configuration while it is unreleased**, so none of its parts can earn Driven: its rows read "Supported in bench builds only" at most, and its "What it can share" lists render under "Not released yet: what the bench build does".

The static words describe what the firmware supports. They never say "Working", which is a **runtime** claim and appears only on a live record: Working, Not responding, Stalled, Stopped answering, Turned off (by you), Starting. The Board Room, the website and the generated reference show static words only, because no device is attached and most boards are compile-tested only.

Settings shown on a part page are read-only. Where a writer exists today, the row says "Change in Home Assistant ›", "Adjust in Camera ›" or "Change on the glass", and Details never duplicates it (§9).

### 7.2 The device-level panel: "What this Canary can share"

The panel has six lists, generated per family from the `disclosures` rows and the class policy. Each list is held to the code (§10), so it cannot silently go false.

Each list, and each exception line, is generated **per family and per shipped configuration** (§7.1), with each configuration's `FEATURE_*` flags resolved the way `check_route_budget.py` resolves the WAP's profiles. A row flips only when the fix is compiled ON in every shipped configuration of that family. A fix behind a default-off flag leaves the row as it is.

1. **Anyone nearby can pick up**:
   - the BLE name and SecuraCV service;
   - the fleet beacon's type, flags, battery, health, chain height and two fingerprint bytes, and on Vision, whose beacon is version 2, the watch profile's class (person or animal today) and a 0 to 100 confidence while it holds a detection and, since #1986, only while the owner's Broadcast detection switch is on, which is off by default (`fleet_beacon.h`; Vision's `fleet_beacon_payload.cpp`). Vision sends the same bytes as an ESP-NOW broadcast too (`fleet_espnow.cpp`). `THREAT_MODEL.md` says so since #1986; `SECURITY_MODEL.md` lists the version-1 fields only (ledger F483);
   - on the WAP, the chirp (type, hour field, chain prefix, two id bytes);
   - on the WAP, while Wi-Fi sensing runs, its ESP-NOW probe broadcast, up to 10 a second: 16 bytes holding a marker, a version and a frame counter that starts at 1 when the probe comes up after a boot (`csi_probe.h` `csi_probe_pkt_t`; `csi_integration.cpp` `probe_pump()`);
   - on the WAP's FULL builds, while the Chirp channel is on (it starts off at each boot, and any dashboard session can turn it on: `POST /api/chirp/enable` takes `api_auth_check()`), its ESP-NOW broadcasts: a presence frame every 60 s under an identity drawn new at each turn-on (a session id and its emoji, a random nonce, whether it is listening, the minutes since it last sent a chirp, and a time, which is the wall clock in Unix seconds once the clock is set and the seconds since boot before that), and the template alerts a person sends from it or it relays (`chirp_channel.cpp` `enable()` and `send_presence()`; `ChirpHeader` in `mesh_network.h`). No PR in this plan changes the probe above or this channel, and no finding of §11 covers either. Ledger F481 holds the probe's counter, which no firmware reads, and F482 the channel's fixed source address, against the spec's "unlinkable" claims, and its uptime in place of an unset clock; whether a PR of this plan takes either is open (§14);
   - the Device Information Service before pairing (manufacturer, model, firmware, the hardware target and the build profile as its hardware and software revisions, and the key fingerprint as its serial);
   - the plain-read status service (name, firmware, chain sequence, health score, degradation level, SD usage, whether the microphone is muted on a build with one, and the uptime, in seconds today and in whole hours from PR 8; `ble_status_api.h`);
   - the Battery Service;
   - on the WAP's FULL builds, while it holds an opera (the household mesh) and the mesh is on, the signed ESP-NOW frames it sends each member, which are not encrypted (`mesh_network.cpp` `send_to_peer()`; `add_peer()` and `load_peers()` register every member with `encrypt = false`; `FEATURE_MESH_NETWORK` is 1 in `build_config.h`'s FULL profile only). An opera outlasts a reboot only on a board with flash encryption on: `load_opera_config()` refuses to load one otherwise, so such a board holds no opera and no member after a boot and sends no signed frame until it pairs again (ledger F141). What this item says of a boot is of a board that kept its opera. Every frame's header carries the opera id (the 16-byte id every member of the mesh shares), the whole key fingerprint (the value the Device Information Service serves as its serial until PR 4), the seconds since boot and the send counter. A boot resumes the counter one past the highest reservation stored, and reservations move in steps of 1,024, so the counter's place in its step counts the frames sent since the boot, and one heartbeat dates a boot of the last 8.5 h to within 30 s when nothing but heartbeats went to that member (`reserve_tx_counter()`, `TX_COUNTER_RESERVE_BLOCK` and `load_tx_reservations()`). The heartbeat, one to each member every 30 s, also carries the seconds since the mesh started, the number of other members and five padding bytes its struct leaves unset (`send_heartbeat()`; `HeartbeatPayload` in `mesh_network.h`, whose 12 bytes hold 7 bytes of fields). With BLE Scout compiled in (`FEATURE_BLE_SCAN`, on in the FULL profile only) and a tag paired, every member the WAP holds at `PEER_CONNECTED` or later also gets every arrival and departure of the tag, with the label the owner gave it, up to 23 characters, so a radio in range learns who comes and goes, by name when the label names someone (`ble_scout.cpp` `emit_arrived()` and `emit_departed()`; `csi_integration.cpp` `drain_outbound_beacon_queue()`; `send_beacon_event()`, whose test is `state >= PEER_CONNECTED`). After a boot that is every stored member, heard or not: `load_peers()` loads each as `PEER_OFFLINE`, which the `PeerState` enum orders after `PEER_CONNECTED`, and `send_to_peer()` asks nothing of a member's state, so the events go on the air even with every other member off. The hub-election frame adds the device's own fingerprint again, and the channel-lock frame the next channel. A mesh the owner turned off is not off, until PR 8. Only the heartbeat waits on the setting: `update()` stops early at `MESH_DISABLED`, but the Scout's events, the hub-election frame and the channel-lock frame go to every stored member with the setting off, from the boot on. Their senders ask only whether an opera is held (`send_beacon_event()`, `send_channel_lock()` and `send_hub_election()` each test `g_opera_config.configured` and never the setting), `init()` brings ESP-NOW up whatever the setting, and the sketch's loop drains the Scout's events on every pass and evaluates the hub election every 5 s, both above its power gate (`csi_integration.cpp` `loop()`, "always runs"). The first evaluation after a boot finds no member heard and so sends its frame to every stored member (`evaluate_coordinator()`; from code). In a host build of this tree's `mesh_network.cpp` on its own harness (`tests_host/mesh_net_sim.h`; run on 2026-11-16 at `0a06c14b`, not on a device), with the setting off and the WAP rebooted, each of the three senders put its frame to the stored member on the air: 127 B for the Scout's event, with its label, 104 B for the channel lock and 111 B for the hub election. And any dashboard session can start a pairing on such a mesh (the next item), which the WAP takes with the setting off (`start_pairing_initiator()` and `start_pairing_joiner()` ask nothing of it, and `update()` runs a session's commands before its early return); when that pairing ends, canceled or at its timeout, the WAP goes to `MESH_CONNECTING` whenever it holds a member (`cancel_pairing()`, which the timeout in `update()` calls; `fail_pairing()`), and `update()` then sends the heartbeats too, the setting still off, until the next reboot or until the setting is written off again (the same host build: 5 heartbeats to a member in the 124 s after a cancel, 10 in the 305 s after a timeout). PR 8 sends 0 for the two clocks and the padding, starts each boot's counters past a random skip (P9), and sends nothing, a pairing included, from a mesh turned off; the id, the fingerprint, the heartbeat's member count and the Scout's events stay until the maintainer decides whether the frames are encrypted (§14);
   - on the WAP's FULL builds, while it is pairing, with an opera or without one (a joiner holds none until its pairing completes), the pairing frames, unsigned by design and not encrypted (`mesh_pair_frame.h`; each sent to an address registered with `encrypt = false`, in `ensure_broadcast_peer()`, `handle_pair_discover()` and `handle_pair_offer()`). Any dashboard session can start a pairing, as the initiator (`POST /api/mesh/pair/start`) or the joiner (`/api/mesh/pair/join`): both take `api_auth_check()`, which takes the session the landing page mints for any LAN host (`handle_mesh_pair_start_auth()` and `handle_mesh_pair_join_auth()` in `canary_wap.ino`; `api_auth.h`, "mints that session for whoever loads it on the home network"; #1887 moved only the mesh confirm to the bearer), and a new start is taken as soon as the last one times out, 2 minutes after it began (`PAIRING_TIMEOUT_MS`), with no second WAP needed. While it runs the WAP broadcasts a DISCOVER to every radio every 2 s, up to 61 of them, with its whole public key, its device id and its role (the "Pairing discovery broadcasts" block of `update()`). As the initiator it answers every DISCOVER that claims the joiner's role, from any radio whose key it has not removed (`is_revoked_pubkey()`) and each time one asks, up to the storm gate's 100 frames a second (`STORM_THRESHOLD_PER_SEC`), with an OFFER to that radio carrying a new ephemeral key, its device key, the mesh's name and the number of members it holds (`handle_pair_discover()`; the same host build as the item above: two made-up radios each sent one such DISCOVER, and each got a 99 B OFFER with "Smith house" and the count 1). A joiner answers the OFFER with an ACCEPT that carries its keys and its device id (`handle_pair_offer()`). The device id is `canary-s3-` or `canary-c3-` and four characters computed from the first two bytes of the key fingerprint (`generate_device_id()` in `canary_wap.ino`, handed to `mesh_network::init()`), the four the setup network's name ends in (`generate_ap_ssid()`), so whoever reads the public key in a pairing frame, or the fingerprint in any signed frame's header, has the device id, name field or not (from code: the sketch's and the mesh's `compute_fingerprint()` hash the key under one domain string, and `unambiguous_suffix16()` renders the first two bytes).
