The pairing reads only the joiner's key and role in its DISCOVER, the initiator's device key in the OFFER and the two ephemeral keys. No canary-wap receiver reads a name (the initiator holds its new member as "New Device", in `initiator_complete()`), the OFFER's member count, the ACCEPT's device key (`handle_pair_accept()` reads the ephemeral key) or the initiator's DISCOVER (a joiner acts on none, in `handle_pair_discover()`), and no payload is cleared first, so the last byte of each name field, which `strncpy()` never writes, goes out as whatever the stack held. PR 8 clears the pairing payloads and sends only what the pairing reads: no name, no member count, no device key in the ACCEPT, and no DISCOVER from the initiator. The keys the pairing reads, and with them the device id, stay until the maintainer decides whether the frames are encrypted (§14);
   - the Improv door while it is open;
   - the setup network's name;
   - **the station's factory Wi-Fi address, in every frame it sends** on your network, as every Wi-Fi device does (your router lists it too). No policy in this design changes it;
   - **today, the Bluetooth address and the setup network's BSSID, both fixed by the factory address**: on S3 boards Bluetooth is the factory address plus one and the BSSID is the factory address with its locally administered bit set (the other five bytes unchanged); on C3 and C6 boards, and on the classic ESP32 boards of the flagship's `esp32cam` and `esp32-wroom` reach ports, the BSSID is the factory address plus one and Bluetooth plus two (§5.6; the installed 2.0.17 and 3.3.8 cores both give the classic ESP32 and the C3 four universal addresses and the S3 two, and 3.3.8 gives the C6 four). These lines flip per family only when that family's shipped configurations compile PR 20's address policy ON with an address drawn at random (PR 20 ships it default-off; the display's BSSID line flips only in the dist PR that changes `provision.cpp`).
2. **Anyone on your network can read**:
   - the mDNS TXT keys, per family (§10 item 2): on the flagship and the trio the keys `mdns_txt.h`'s table builds (`device_id`, `name`, `host`, `fw`, `model`, `dt`, `role`, `hw`, `caps`, `coord`, `broker`, `bport`, `tls`, `secure_port`); on the WAP `device_id`, `name`, `host`, `fw`, `model`, `dt`, `role`, `broker` and `bport` (the `MDNS.addServiceTxt()` calls in `canary_wap.ino`); on a display the same nine, and `hw` where its board names a token (`discovery.cpp`);
   - the `/api/fleet` words, and its chain height where a family sends one (the chain sequence on the WAP and the flagship, the chain length on the trio; §3.6);
   - the `/api/status` body on displays and the trio, and on a display `/api/device`'s answer (§3.6);
   - on a display, its mirror's `/api/glass`, to any host whose request names the display (`handle_glass()` refuses only a foreign Host, and sends no CORS header): the wall's clock, its night flag, its worst severity and whether its Wi-Fi and broker links are up, and for each fleet witness it holds, its name, its room, its severity, the seconds since it was last heard and, for a wellbeing Sense, whether that Sense holds a breathing lock (`wb` and `br`, from the Sense's `breathing_locked`); and `/api/settings`, the glass knobs and the time zone, which refuses no Host (`handle_settings_get()` withholds only the CSRF token from a foreign one);
   - on a display, the log lines `/api/log` mirrors from the cable (P22);
   - on Sense, Vision and Sentinel, the peer heartbeat each multicasts every 10 s while it holds a fleet key, to any host on the network that joins its group (§5.5): its key fingerprint, its role, whether it is on its hub, the fingerprint of the member it takes as coordinator, a random byte drawn once per boot, the onboarding, hub and update words, the capability bits and the peer and saved-network counts (`peer_protocol.h`'s frame layout), and, until PR 11, its uptime in seconds, a frame counter that restarts at each boot and so dates the boot as well, and the Wi-Fi signal in dBm (P9, P23). Its tag proves who sent it and hides nothing;
   - on Vision, the fleet beacon itself, multicast with no key and no pairing to any host on the network that joins its group (`239.83.67.86:5867`, TTL 1, the group the peer heartbeat uses): list 1's bytes, the class and the 0 to 100 confidence among them while the Broadcast detection switch is on, every 5 s and at once when a detection starts, ends or changes class (`fleet_udp.cpp`; `fleet_beacon_udp.h`; ledger F483);
   - on the WAP, the public `/api/device-info` fields, and, after the join, `/api/selftest`'s probes: until PR 8 their metrics, the home network's name, address, channel and signal, the microphone's level and whether it is muted, the battery's voltage, charge percent and charge state, the last crash's stage and reason on a build with Bluetooth, the uptime in milliseconds, the card's total and free bytes, and why Bluetooth did not start; and after PR 8 too, unless it also takes them off the other interfaces, which is open (§12 PR 8), each row's `code` and `detail`: the Wi-Fi row's signal in dBm and the network's name, the battery row's charge percent, and, after a start refused for low memory, the Bluetooth row's reason with the largest free block (P5; `selftest_api.h`);
   - **on the WAP, everything a dashboard session reads, because any device on your network can open one (the administrator page's sign-in adds the token to that page; neither it nor #1887 changes who gets a session)**: the system log, the witness records, the chain and the live view, and with them the witness count, the chain sequence, whether the live view is in use and the uptime to the second (§3.2); the camera's, the microphone's, the Wi-Fi sensing's and the Bluetooth presence scan's counters and the sensing's frame rate (`/api/peek/status`, `/api/audio/status`, `/api/status`, `/api/presence`, `/api/csi/stream`); until PR 8, the other counts that summarize them (boot count, SD writes, the event-egress counters); the home, away, quiet or traveling context, whether an override holds it and for how long, and how long since an owner's phone was last seen (`/api/presence`); the household roster, each enrolled phone's label, role and how long ago it was last seen, which no pairing fills today (`/api/household`; `household_api.h`: `household::add_irk()` has no caller but the on-device self-test); the home network's name and address and the seconds since the WAP joined it (`connected_sec`), the setup network's name and address and, while it is up, how many devices are on it, and the nearby networks, the neighbors' among them (`/api/wifi` and `/api/wifi/scan`, which until PR 8 any host reads with no session, P4), and until PR 8 the Wi-Fi signal in dBm (`/api/wifi`'s, each scanned network's, the home network's included, and `/api/status`'s `node_status`'s); once the Wi-Fi sensing's privacy ceiling is P2, which until PR 8 any session can set and from it only the bearer can raise (its first boot lowers one set before it), the raw sensing window (`/api/csi/window`), its motion and breathing bins and the signal's mean, spread, highest and lowest in dBm among them, over every device in range if a session has turned off the foreign-frame filter, which it still can after PR 8; once the ceiling is P1 or above, the breathing rate in the sensing's events (`/api/csi/stream`, `/api/events/today`); until PR 8, on FULL builds, the mesh's id, which every member of the household mesh shares and any radio in range also reads from its frames (list 1), and its name, uptime and message, alert and auth-failure counters, and each mesh peer's name, key fingerprint, signal and last-heard age (`/api/mesh`, `/api/mesh/peers`); on FULL builds, each mesh alert the WAP holds, with the name of the member that sent it, what it says (a member's supply voltage and runtime, the start of its chain head, a tamper alert's text), that member's chain sequence and when the alert arrived (`/api/mesh/alerts`, and the health log); on FULL builds, in the health log, each member's name at every change of its state, the mesh's name after a rename and a removed member's key fingerprint, after PR 8 too (§3.2); during a pairing, a Bluetooth Numeric Comparison's six digits and the mesh confirmation code, which both ends compute and which grant nothing without the confirm, and the confirm takes the bearer (F346); the dashboard strip's status words, its "safe mode" among them, and until PR 8 its "restarted" and "low memory"; whether the last restart was a panic, a watchdog or a brownout, after PR 8 too, in the health log's line from that boot and in the tamper word the sensing's events carry (`/api/events/today`, `/api/csi/stream`), and whether three came in a row (`/api/status`'s safe mode); until PR 8, the reset cause word, the heap's and the PSRAM's low-water marks, the heap's largest free block and the salted Hardware ID (`/api/system`, `/api/status`'s `last_reset`, `last_reset_crash` and `min_heap`, `/api/diagnostics`; P6, P21), and after PR 8 too the largest free block a refused Bluetooth start reports (`/api/bluetooth`, the health log; §3.2); the camera and microphone settings it can also change; and everything the WAP publishes to MQTT (list 4: its status's signal, its health's uptime, the chain length, the witness count, the event-egress and mesh airtime counters), on a broker the session points the MQTT bridge at, which `POST /api/mqtt/config` lets it do (§3.6). Generated from the recorded legacy rows for the WAP's lan tier (§3.6), so it shrinks as they do;
   - on the WAP, whether it is in safe mode, which three crash resets in a row put it in, from `/api/selftest` and the help QR (`/api/help-qr`), after PR 8 too (§3.2);
   - on the WAP, and over Bluetooth rather than the network, a phone with an authenticated bond to it, which reads at the same tier (§5.3): the console snapshot, with the uptime in seconds, the free heap in bytes, the Wi-Fi link's state word and its signal in dBm, the Wi-Fi sensing's motion score, the minutes since an owner's phone was last seen, the home, away, quiet or traveling context and whether an override holds it, the household roster's size, the Bluetooth advert counts, and the key fingerprint's first 15 hex digits and the firmware version (`ble_console.cpp` `build_snapshot()`); BLE provisioning's scan list, with each network's signal, the home network's included; the system log, through the log export, the reset line and each entry's milliseconds since boot among it; and the witness export's latest record, with the chain sequence; after PR 8 as before;
   - the lan tier of `/api/details` (new: from PR 11 on displays and the trio, PR 12 on the WAP).
3. **Your apps that hold this Canary's key can read**: everything in Details at the owner tier.
4. **Your broker and Home Assistant receive**: health and status, and from PR 16 the `about` part. Health and status today also carry the uptime in seconds (and status's `ts_ms`, the milliseconds since boot, on the display and the trio), the Wi-Fi signal in dBm in the WAP's, the display's and the trio's status, the heap's low-water mark on the display and the trio, the radar link's state, frame errors and last-frame age on Sense and, since #1892 (PR 7), on Sentinel, the display's unexpected-reboot flag, and the microphone's counters in the sensing bodies (the WAP's `i2s_read_errors`; the flagship's `acoustic_stats`). Those are recorded broker answers (§3.6): Home Assistant shows the signal, the radar link and the unexpected reboot, and the display's Roll Call the signal.
5. **Whoever holds the USB cable can read**:
   - the boot banner, including the salted Hardware ID on five families (the WAP, display, Sense, Vision and Sentinel; the flagship prints none and computes `hw_token` only for the receipt, in `securacv_network.cpp` `send_provisioning_receipt()`);
   - on the WAP, the provisioning receipt it prints on every boot, with its API token, the setup network's name and password, the key fingerprint and the salted Hardware ID (`canary_wap.ino` `setup()`, "Print full provisioning receipt on every boot"); the flagship's console never prints its token (F410);
   - the serial tier of `q`, posture and activity included;
   - the self-manifest;
   - in a flasher, the chip's ROM facts and the MAC, which stays on that computer. Since #1970 (PR 1) both flashers show none of its bytes until asked and keep it out of saved reports and backup file names.
6. **Never leaves this Canary through a page, an app or an API**, except where a line names who receives it:
   - the keys, tokens and passwords it stores, except two that the setup receipt hands the app that sets this Canary up, on the flagship and the WAP: its API token and its setup network's password, to a bearer or a BOOT tap, and on the WAP also to the setup-door claim (`identity_json.h` `write_receipt()`, `securacv_network.cpp` `send_provisioning_receipt()`). The flagship's API token also leaves inside the dashboard and setup pages it serves, with no receipt: `handle_ui()` and the `/setup` handlers write it into the page (`securacv_network.cpp`, through `send_html_with_token()` and `page_token_inject()`) whenever `page_token_decide()` grants it (`firmware/common/network/provisioning_gate.h`), which is for any page load under a Host that names the device while the first-boot setup wizard is active, for any device on the flagship's own access point while that is up (`from_ap_subnet()`, with no bearer and no tap), for a bearer, and for the one page load that spends a BOOT tap. The WAP writes no token into a page. A credential the device mints for the client that asks, such as the WAP's session cookie or pair token, is that client's, not one of these;
   - on the WAP, a Bluetooth Passkey Entry's six digits, which are the bond (the phone types them and nothing on the WAP confirms), to a bearer while the passkey is shown, through the accounted grant; a session gets `pin_hidden` (F346, since #1887; `/api/bluetooth` `pairing.pin` and `pin_hidden`, in `bluetooth_api.h`). Before #1887 a session read them, and so did every reader of the health log, which the SD card keeps, `/api/logs` serves to a session and the Bluetooth log export serves to every device with an authenticated bond to the WAP (`ble_log_export.cpp`, whose characteristics are `READ_AUTHEN`; `bluetooth_channel.cpp` calls `ble_log_export::init()` in every Bluetooth build, the DEV and FULL profiles of `build_config.h`); since #1887 the health log keeps no pairing digits, and #1836 writes its two lines the same way (P8). A Numeric Comparison's digits and the mesh confirmation code (`/api/mesh` `pairing_code`, in `handle_mesh_status()`) are not on this list: both ends compute them, and the confirm that grants takes the bearer, so a session reads them (list 2);
   - the factory addresses;
   - flash and eFuse unique ids and calibration values;
   - card serials and module ids;
   - neighbors' Bluetooth addresses, except, on the WAP, to the app paired with this Canary's key (`/api/bluetooth` with the bearer, from PR 8; P7);
   - pictures and sound, except where a part sheet says otherwise.

   The cable is list 5's, not this one's: every family prints its setup network's name and password on the console (each through `onboarding_fsm.h`'s `qr_line()`), the WAP its API token too (list 5), and a flasher can read the whole flash, keys included, onto its computer (the browser flasher's backup, `docs/browser_flasher.md`).

   **List 6 carries generated exception lines.** Each `KNOWN_LEAK` row in `lint_identifier_reads.py` (new in PR 4; ledger F430 holds that lint open) prints under list 6 for the families it affects, as an honest exception, until the row is removed. So the WAP's list reads "Except, until PR 8: a dashboard session receives the chip's own Bluetooth address (`/api/bluetooth` `local_address`, in `bluetooth_api.h`) and nearby devices' addresses". A list can only claim what the code already does.

### 7.3 Sensing parts

| Part (where) | About this part (provenance) | Health | Settings (where they change today) | Shares (to) | Stays |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Camera OV2640 / OV3660** (flagship, WAP) | PID (read today on both); VER, MIDH and MIDL (read today on the WAP only). Name, max frame size and JPEG support from `esp_camera_sensor_get_info()` (new read), replacing the WAP's 8-bit name table, which reads an OV3660 as unknown (`canary_wap.ino` `sensor_model_name()`: the driver's `OV3660_PID` is `0x3660`). SCCB address, XCLK 20 MHz, frame buffers and where they live (set). | Init attempts, last init error word, freezes, frames per second (owner) | The 26 sensor values Camera answers (of the driver's 28 status fields; `scale` and `binning` are not sent) are read-only here: "Adjust in Camera ›" (`POST /api/peek/sensor`: flagship bearer, WAP session today) | Live view while watched: on the flagship to a caller with the bearer, on the WAP to any dashboard session, which is LAN trust and needs no sign-in (`/api/peek/start` takes `api_auth_check()`, the stream and the snapshot `api_auth_check_or_query()`), as #1887 left it. Sealed snapshots to the vault (WAP). Both said plainly | Frames otherwise; nothing about images in details |
| **PDM microphone MSM261** (flagship, WAP) | "This part can't tell us" (no ID register). I2S PDM, 16 kHz, 20 ms frames (set). | Latched status word; muted and by what. Activity (owner, serial): frames, read errors, silent-line streak | Loudness thresholds (WAP `/api/audio/config`, session today) | Sound-event categories and loudness on/off (hub, broker) | Sound itself; the live level is a reading and never in details |
| **MR60BHA2 radar** (Sense, Sentinel Standard) | Seeed module; ADT6101P 60 GHz FMCW (datasheet). UART1 at 115,200 (set). Module firmware: "Cannot say" until frame 0xFFFF is decoded (bench). | Latched status word at every tier. Activity, on its link view (serial, glass, owner; held at `lan` and `broker`, where the health topic's `radar` object stays a recorded answer, §7.2 list 4): link OK, last-frame age (a word), CRC errors, **unknown and dropped frames** (counted today, `mr60_uart.h` `unknown_count()` and `dropped_count()`, never published). The parser decodes presence, the target count, distance, breathing and heart rate (the frame table in its header comment). It counts a frame of any other type as unknown when both checksums pass and its payload is at most 32 bytes, and refuses a longer frame of any type (`MR60_MAX_PAYLOAD`), counting it with the CRC errors. Which frames arrive, how long they are (the radar notes give the 3D point cloud, 0x0A08, a record per target) and whether their rate follows presence wait on the bench, so the unknown, dropped and CRC-error counts are all treated as moving with the room. | Presence debounce, clear, stall, near/mid band edges, vitals windows and rate bounds (`sense_config.h`; HA numbers, MQTT cfg, USB tuning console) | Presence, 0/1/2+, near/mid/far (hub, broker, HA; Apple Home through `homekit_projection`); a breathing-lock word on wellbeing builds (hub, broker, HA; and, through any display on that broker, to anyone on the network, §7.2 list 2); rate numerics only on P1 builds | Raw centimeters, per-target data, vitals phases; the 3D point-cloud frame (0x0A08 in `docs/hardware/mr60bha2_radar_notes.md`) is never decoded (no `MR60_TYPE_*` constant names it), and of 0x0A04 (`PRINT_CLOUD` in `mr60_uart.h`) the parser reads only the leading target count; the console's raw mode stays on the attended cable |
| **BH1750 light** (Sense, Sentinel, Lite) | Address 0x23 (board). "Answered" from its power-on acknowledgment; there is no ID register. Mode `OP_CONT_HRES` (set). | Answering. Since #1892 (PR 7) the Sentinel keeps `begin()`'s answer: a sensor that did not answer at boot never votes and is reported as `channel_denied` (`sentinel_links.h` `light_link()`). | — | Lux to HA (Sense); a fusion vote only (Sentinel) | The lux reading never in details |
| **PIR** (Sentinel, Lite) | Pin, polarity, settle time (board, set). "This part can't tell us" whether it is connected. | — | — | A fusion vote only | — |
| **Sentinel fusion** | Which channels this preset fuses (board) | Per channel: latched status word at every tier. Activity, on the link view (serial only on Sentinel, which has no owner credential or glass): quality word, "has reported" yes/no, for each channel the preset enables: six on the door, window and hallway presets, four on Lite's, nine on the demo head's. Only the PIR, the radar and the light have a reader today; no other channel's is built, and those never vote. The light channel votes on a change in the room's light, so its entry moves with the room. | Preset (build) | The fused level, a 0 to 100 confidence and anomaly score, a 0/1/2+ occupancy, a near/mid/far band, which modality classes corroborated, and whether a channel is denied (broker, HA) | Per-channel votes and times |
| **Grove Vision AI V2** (Vision) | Himax HX6538 module, with an OV5647 camera behind it (board). I2C 0x62 at 400 kHz (set). **Answered:** `begin()` and then a fresh `name(false)` round trip (built, #1892: `vision_mgr.cpp` `init()`; the boot line's and the `j` proof record's `module_id` is 1 or 0 from it, and the record carries `module_ok`). **Model matches SecuraCV's catalog:** yes or no, from comparing the module's model card to the name compiled into the firmware, never echoed (planned, PR 15). SSCMA software version: "Cannot say" until a raw `VER?` read is bench-checked; the library defines that command (`CMD_AT_VERSION`) and has no getter for it. Its getters are `ID()`, `name()`, `info()` and `perf()`, and for the module's own Wi-Fi and MQTT side `WIFIVER()`, `WIFI()`, `MQTT()`, `WIFISTA()` and `MQTTSTA()`; of them this firmware calls `name()` alone. | Answered; last invoke OK (since #1892 `sample()` compares `invoke()` with `CMD_OK`, so a failed invoke yields no sample and an empty room still ticks the presence timer). Activity, on the link view (serial only on Vision): preprocess, inference and postprocess ms from `perf()` (`duration: true`), held as activity because the postprocess step is expected to grow with the number of detections (not measured) | Watch profile, class, score floor, lost and dwell timeouts (HA) | To the broker and Home Assistant, in each event and the retained state: presence and dwell, the watch profile, a 0 to 100 confidence, a 3 × 3 cell and the mask of occupied cells, the detection's box (x, y, width, height), and occupancy, posture and proximity words (`main.cpp` `publish_event_json()`; `mqtt_mgr.cpp`). On the v2 beacon: the profile's class, person or animal today (the format also names vehicle and package), and the 0 to 100 confidence, to anyone nearby and, by multicast, to anyone on the network (§7.2 lists 1 and 2). Aim boxes are opt-in and turn off on their own after 10 minutes | Pictures: the firmware calls no image function of the library (`save_jpeg()`, `last_image()`), and no lint holds that yet (PR 4's will, §10 item 3). The module's id: the library's own `begin()` asks the module for it once (`ID(false)`) and keeps the answer in its private buffer; the firmware never reads, prints or stores it, and `desktop_parity.test.js`'s `sscmaIdCalls()` refuses an `AI.ID(` call in Vision's sources. Credentials stored on the module are never asked for |
| **Wi-Fi as a sensor (CSI)** (WAP, flagship) | Bandwidth, filter armed (unit). The channel is household and has no key (§3.1). | Latched status word. Activity (owner, serial): frames received; drops by cause | Sensitivity | Motion and breathing words | Raw channel state; the router's address is compared in place, never copied (THREAT_MODEL Principle 3) |
| **Touch pad** (flagship) | Pin; baseline locked (unit) | Self-calibration state | — | Touch and approach events | Raw counts |
| **IR receiver** (flagship) | RMT channel (set) | Decoded-frame count | — | Protocol category and a per-boot salted bucket | Codes |
| **Tamper contact** (flagship and WAP, in bench builds only: `FEATURE_TAMPER_GPIO` is 0 in every shipped flagship env and every WAP profile) | Pin, active low, debounce (board, set) | Open/closed is an event, not a detail | — | Tamper events; the beacon's tamper flag (nearby) | — |
| **Audio codecs ES7210 / ES8311** (dash 4.3C, built only by the `canary-display-dash-mic` bench env) | "Answered" from the I2C acknowledgment; chip-ID registers "Cannot say" until bench-checked | Listening state, always shown | Microphone off by default (the glass's Settings, microphone) | Smoke-alarm and CO-alarm pattern events, unsigned and local to the glass (`mic_alarm.h`); a loud sound wakes the screen only if the owner opts in | Audio |
| **GNSS L76K** (flagship, WAP) | UART at 9,600 (set). Module firmware: "Cannot say" (`$PCAS06` not bench-checked). | Sentences, checksum errors, fix yes/no (satellites and DOPs are household and never in details) | Coarsening floor (coarser only) | Position rounded to about 110 m in records where built | Precise coordinates |
| **Die temperature** (S3, C3, C6) | On-chip; calibration present (unit) | Whole °C, "the chip, not the room" | — | — | Classic ESP32: "Not on this chip" |

**Open: how fine Vision's share is.** The Grove Vision row records what the firmware sends today. This design first described a class word, a confidence word and a 3 × 3 cell. Main sends more: a 0 to 100 confidence, the detection's box and the occupancy, posture and proximity words to the broker, and the class and the 0 to 100 confidence on the beacon. Whether Details describes that share as it is, or a PR coarsens it first, is the maintainer's to decide (§14), and `SECURITY_MODEL.md` does not yet say what the beacon carries (ledger F483). #1986 made the beacon's detection an owner opt-in, off by default, on every band, and left the broker share as it is (§13).

### 7.5 Storage, power, time and USB

| Part (where) | About this part | Health | Shares | Stays |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **microSD** (flagship, WAP, amoled241, the dash-sd env) | Kind (SDSC or SDHC), capacity, the clock it mounted at (read). Eight display manifests list a slot. `canary-display-amoled241` builds `-DFEATURE_SD_STORAGE=1` (its `build_flags` in `canary-display.ini`), so Driven. The dash defaults the flag to 0, and `canary-display-dash-sd` compiles `src/fleet/sd_archive.cpp` with it on for the dash's 4.3B variant: **"Built, waiting on a bench check"**. The dash's own 4.3 board, for which no build env turns the flag on, and its 4.3C variant, whose pins header declares no SD data pins, read "On the board, not used by this firmware". The other six cannot compile the driver, so they read "On the board, not used by this firmware", each with its reason: `sd_archive.cpp` `#error`s outside the dash and AMOLED flavors, which rules out the nightstand-s3, nightstand-c6 and nightlight-c3 (`CD_FLAVOR_NIGHTSTAND`) and the watch (whose slot also shares the panel's SPI pins); and it `#error`s on a board with no SD data pins, which rules out the dash7 and nightstand7, whose 7-inch board declares none yet (`waveshare-esp32s3-lcd7/pins/pins.h`, "No SD data pins declared yet"; the driver's own message calls that slot "still unverified"). Of the flagship's reach ports, the Freenove S3's manifest lists a slot that is 1-bit SDMMC, which the flagship's SPI driver cannot reach, and `[env:freenove-s3]` builds `-DFEATURE_SD_STORAGE=0`, so it reads "On the board, not used by this firmware"; the `esp32cam` env keeps the flag on through `extends = env:release`. | Used %, wear estimate, replace-recommended; lifetime writes (counter) | — | The card's identity register is never read, because the serial sits in it |
| **Battery** (flagship and WAP divider; the watch, Touch 1.69 and AMOLED 2.41 boards carry one that no display firmware reads) | Divider and ADC calibration scheme (read). Displays and the trio: "This Canary doesn't read its battery yet". Since #1971 (PR 6) the trio's health sends `battery_present: false` and no `battery` value, and Home Assistant shows the charge as unknown; the display's health still sends the hard-coded 100 beside it until PR 17. | Percent, millivolts, charging | Percent on the beacon (nearby), the BLE Battery Service (WAP), HA | — |
| **RTC PCF8563** (watch: not used; 4.3B: "Built, waiting on a bench check", because `canary-display-dash-rtc` compiles the layer with `FEATURE_RTC` on against the 4.3B's pins, whose header declares `HAS_RTC 0` until a bench check confirms the chip) / **PCF85063A** (touch169 and amoled241: used; 4.3C: on the board, `FEATURE_RTC` off, and no build env compiles the layer for its pins with `RTC_CHIP_PCF85063` set) | Presence, by its acknowledgment, and the oscillator-stop bit, read with the time (`rtc.cpp`, `rtc_pcf.h`) | "Kept time while unplugged: yes" / "lost time once" | Nothing | — |
| **USB descriptors** (flagship `usb-onboard`, WAP `usbdrive`) | Manufacturer and product strings (build); serial-number mode (build) | — | **To every computer it is plugged into**, in these two opt-in envs only, which CI compiles and no release ships ("Supported in bench builds only"): the full factory MAC as the serial number (finding P2). Whether the stock images' USB Serial/JTAG controller reports the MAC as its serial number too is unchecked (F376, a bench read) | — |

### 7.6 Radios and links

| Radio | About (class) | Shares | Stays |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Wi-Fi** | Standard, width, maximum transmit power, power save, PHY on IDF 5 (unit); signal as a word (activity: serial, glass and owner only); SoftAP address mode (build) | mDNS TXT, `/api/fleet` and `/api/status` on the LAN; the station address in every frame; the setup network's name and, today, a BSSID fixed by the factory address whenever the setup network is up (nearby; on the WAP that is also every home-network outage, and all the time in standalone mode) | SSID, IP, channel and country are household and never in details; the router's address is compared in place |
| **Bluetooth LE** | Stack and version (build; NimBLE 1.4.3 "Not reported"), transmit power, MTU, address mode (build: "fixed by the chip's factory address" today, §7.2 list 1), bond count (counter) | §7.2 list 1 | Neighbors' addresses (finding P7 on WAP sessions) |
| **ESP-NOW (Opera mesh)** | Version, peer count (unit) | Signed mesh frames to household members, not encrypted, so any radio in range reads them: on FULL builds, while an opera is held and the mesh is on (and, until PR 8, with the mesh turned off too: the Scout's events, the hub election and the channel lock at any time, and, once a pairing a session starts on it ends with a member held, the heartbeats too, until the next reboot or until the setting is written off again), the opera id, the whole key fingerprint, the seconds since boot and a send counter whose place in its 1,024 step counts the frames since the boot in every frame, the seconds since the mesh started and the member count in each member's heartbeat every 30 s, and, with BLE Scout, a paired tag's arrivals and departures with its label, to every stored member after a boot, heard or not (on a board whose opera outlasts the boot, §7.2 list 1). While the WAP is pairing, which any dashboard session can start (until PR 8, on a mesh turned off too), and start again every 2 min, unsigned pairing frames, not encrypted either: a DISCOVER to every radio every 2 s with the device's whole public key and device id (which the key gives anyway), an OFFER with the mesh's name and member count to every radio that sends a joiner's DISCOVER, and an ACCEPT with the joiner's device id (§7.2 list 1; PR 8 sends 0 for both clocks, starts the counter past a random skip each boot, sends nothing from a mesh turned off, and sends in the pairing frames only the keys and the role the pairing reads, and the rest is open, §14). On Vision, ESP-NOW also carries the fleet beacon as a broadcast, the class and the confidence included, and on the WAP the Wi-Fi sensing's probe and, when it is on, the Chirp channel (§7.2 list 1) | Peer addresses |
| **IEEE 802.15.4** (C6 boards) | "On the chip, not used by this firmware" | Nothing | Its EUI-64 is never read |
| **CAN / RS485** (dash, dash7, nightstand7) | 4.3B: "Built, waiting on a bench check": `canary-display-dash-can` and `-dash-rs485` are the only builds that compile the drivers (`src/io/can_bus.cpp`, `src/io/rs485.cpp`) with `FEATURE_CAN` or `FEATURE_RS485` on, on 4.3B pins, and the released dash and dash-modes images leave both off; bitrate and error counters there. Plain 4.3 and the 7-inch board: "On the board, not used by this firmware", because their pins headers declare `HAS_CAN_RS485 1` and no CAN or RS485 pins, which the drivers need | Nothing | — |

LoRa is a concept only and has no row.

### 7.7 The chip itself

The chip has the `chip`, `memory`, `image`, `radio` and `now` parts above, plus `security` at the owner and glass tiers.

- **What it can do (datasheet):** Wi-Fi 4 (Wi-Fi 6 on the C6); Bluetooth LE 5 on the S3, C3 and C6, and Bluetooth 4.2 on the classic ESP32 of the flagship's `esp32cam` and `esp32-wroom` reach ports; 802.15.4 (C6); native USB on the S3, C3 and C6 (the classic ESP32 has none, and reaches a computer through a separate USB-to-serial chip).
- **What this Canary uses:** each radio with its status word.
- **Said plainly on the Chip page:**
  - the station address is the chip's factory address, and the Bluetooth advertising address and the setup network's BSSID are fixed by it (§5.6, P1, P3; THREAT_MODEL Principle 3 calls the BSSID "the radio's factory MAC"), until this family's shipped images compile PR 20's address policy on (the generated line says which, and that the station address stays);
  - on the flagship and the WAP the factory MAC is an input to the API token's derivation (`derive_api_token()`) and is never shown;
  - a crash record's presence is shown to the owner, on the glass and over USB (it is posture), and its image never is.

### 7.8 Invariant II, structurally

- **The word guard.** Every vocabulary text (label, explanation, term, part name, `can`, `out`, `stays`, status words) is checked against a whole-word list of identity-inferring capabilities. The list is enumerated once in `lint_device_details.py` (new in PR 3), mirroring AGENTS.md rule 1, and **refuses negative mentions too**: a part sheet talks about data ("Pictures", "Sound itself"), never about recognition.
- **The Grove module's own model text never reaches a screen.** That covers its model card (`info()`), name, model list and algorithm list; the SSCMA version a flasher reads from the module is shown (§5.6). Today the firmware asks the module only whether a name comes back (`name(false)`, #1892) and echoes none of it; from PR 15 it compares the card against the catalog name it was built with and reports yes or no.

  The module's stock catalog includes models that would be forbidden capabilities (`docs/hardware/grove_vision_ai_v2_guide.md`, "Person/face/gesture detection out of the box"). So #1970 (PR 1) took the card's model name off the browser flasher's bench line (`we2-flash.js` `phaseModuleBench()`, "and never names the model"): the card now decides only the label map, and a model is named only by the flasher that just wrote it, after its SHA-256 matched the pinned release.
- **Class words come from the `ObjectClass` set only:** Person, Vehicle, Animal, Package, Unknown. The set is the enum in `src/detect/result.rs`, which AGENTS.md rule 1 names; the firmware's `FLEET_BEACON_DETECT_*` tokens and the dictionary's `homekit_projection` signals `motion_person`, `motion_vehicle`, `motion_animal` and `motion_package` spell the same four.

## 8. Honesty labels

**Provenance on each section or row:**

| Label | When |
|---|---|
| **Read from the chip** | A flasher's ROM or allowlisted eFuse-field read, and the JEDEC flash reading, no firmware involved |
| **From the installed image** | A flasher's read of the bootloader header or the app descriptor: what the image declares, which the chip's own reading can contradict |
| **Reported** | The firmware said so over USB, LAN, Bluetooth or the broker, unsigned. Per fact, the vocabulary says whether the firmware *read* it, *set* it, or it is *as built*. |
| **As built** | A pins-header or board-registry fact. Every display board is `compile-tested`, so "as built" never says "measured". |
| **From the maker's datasheet** | Part sheets, and in the flashers the chip's radios, cores and crystal, listed by chip model. "Bench-checked" only with tier evidence. |
| **Emulated** | The Lab's twin |
| **Verified · signed by this Canary's key** | Only on the app's own screen, **per section**: a section reads Verified only while every value in it came from a part fetched over HTTP with the app's fresh nonce, whose signature checked against the pinned key (PR 21) and whose signed request (part, id, view, page) is the one the app asked for. A part answered unsigned (rate-limited, or polled live) makes its section read "Reported". The footnote is required: "It is still the Canary's own account of itself." |
| **Signed by this Canary · not proven current** | A retained MQTT part whose signature checked against the pinned key. It proves authorship, not that the part is this boot's (below). |
| **Reported by this Canary · signature checked by SecuraCV <app> <version> on <day>** | Shared text only (Copy Details, §6.4). The recipient cannot check the signature, so shared text never says "Verified". |

Three of these labels are built: since #1970 both flashers' About-this-board card puts "Read from the chip", "From the installed image" and "From the maker's datasheet" on its groups ("This chip", "Installed image", "What this chip can do"; `aboutBoardGroups()` in `canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`, copied into `desktop/src/app.js`). The other labels are planned.

**Signed details (PR 21).** This is a ninth kind in the `securacv-canary-sig|v1` family, beside `whoami` and the seven others that `firmware/common/identity/device_signature.h` lists:

```
securacv-canary-sig|v1|details|<device_id>|<nonce_hex>|<part>|<id or ->|<view or ->|<page or 0>|<sha256_hex(body bytes)>   (HTTP, the client's nonce)
securacv-canary-sig|v1|details|<device_id>|boot:<bootnonce_hex>|<part>|-|-|0|<sha256_hex(body bytes)>   (MQTT retained; the about part only)
```

The request is bound twice: in the canonical string, and in the body's own header (`part`, and `id`, `view` or `page` where the part has them, §4.1). The app checks that both name what it asked for, so a LAN machine in the middle cannot answer a request for one sensor's page, or index page 2, with a correctly signed different page.

- **The nonce.** It passes `whoami_nonce_ok()`: 16-64 lowercase hex.
- **Who hashes what.** The device hashes its own body, so the key never signs a byte the client chose.
- **Where the signature travels.** It rides `X-Canary-Sig` response headers, so a verifier hashes exactly the bytes it received.
- **Rate limit.** One signed answer per source address per 2 s. A request over the limit gets the unsigned part, never an error, and its section reads "Reported". So the app asks for each at-boot part (`about`, `chip`, `image`, `security`, `radio`, the index pages, each sensor's identity page) once when About opens, one request every 2 s in the background, each with its own fresh nonce, and each section turns Verified as the Canary's signature on its part checks: about 20 s for ten parts. The live sections (Right Now, Memory, the link views) poll every 5 s, outrun the limit, and stay "Reported".
- **The MQTT envelope binds a per-boot random nonce, not the boot counter.** `bootnonce_hex` is 16 random bytes drawn with `esp_random()` at boot, never stored, and published in the clear beside the part. A counter would put `boots` on the broker, where §3.3 holds counters, and it would claim a freshness the verifier cannot check: the verifier has no trusted current boot number, and the flagship's health `boot_count` is unsigned and writable by any broker client (`mqtt_publish_health_update()` in `firmware/canary/src/main.cpp`).
- **What it proves.** Over HTTP: authorship by the paired key, and freshness against the client's own nonce. Over MQTT: authorship only. A broker client can republish an older retained `about` from an earlier boot, and it still verifies. So Home Assistant labels a verified retained part "Signed by this Canary · not proven current". It also keeps a per-device high-water mark on the verified firmware version, and shows a verified part naming older firmware than one it has already verified as "older than one already seen" rather than replacing it. Neither form proves the hardware is what it says.
- **Who can earn it.** The display holds no signing key, so its details are "Reported" forever.

**Capability words.** These come in two sets that never mix (§7.1).

- **Static, earned from code:** Supported by this firmware; Supported in bench builds only; Built, waiting on a bench check; On the board, not used by this firmware.
- **Runtime, only from a live record:** Working, Not responding, Stalled, Stopped answering, Turned off (by you), Starting.

A peripheral whose driver the board's own build can compile, while the shipped configuration leaves its default-off `FEATURE_*` flag off, reads "Built, waiting on a bench check" only when a build env compiles that driver with the flag on for that board, with its own pins and part selection (§7.1). "On the board, not used by this firmware" is for a part that no source this board's env builds can drive: there is no driver, or the driver sits behind a define this board's configuration never sets (the QMI8658 reader on the Touch 1.69), or it refuses this board's flavor, or no build env turns its flag on for this board (the dash's plain 4.3 microSD, §7.5). Neither reads "Not present".

**Absence:** the seven phrases of §6.3, never zero, empty or "fine". The flashers' About-this-board card (#1970) keeps its own five in `ABOUT_CANNOT` (§6.3).

**Time:** a duration, never "restarted at hh:mm"; a build day, never a build time.

**No bare "secure."** Posture words state the fact: "Key stored unencrypted in settings storage". They never imply that flash encryption protects settings storage (`firmware/common/identity/key_at_rest.h`, "FLASH ENCRYPTION DOES NOT COVER NVS").

---

## 9. Read-only now, adjustable later

Everything in Details is read-only now.

| Setting | Changes today | In Details now | May become adjustable from Details | Gate when it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Camera sensor values | WAP session / flagship bearer, `POST /api/peek/sensor` | read-only, "Adjust in Camera ›" | yes | Owner: the bearer, which the WAP's administrator page (`/admin` and `/settings`, `web_ui.h`) sends once its owner has signed in with the token (since #1887 that sign-in checks a pasted token by itself). WAP camera writes move from session to owner. |
| Microphone loudness levels | WAP session, `/api/audio/config` | read-only | yes | Owner |
| Sense's radar knobs and Vision's detection knobs | MQTT cfg topics and the HA numbers over them, on both; on Sense also its USB tuning console (Vision's console takes only `j` and `h`) | read-only, "Change in Home Assistant ›" | over the broker as today, and on Sense over USB as today; over the LAN only where an owner credential exists | Broker, physical; never a lan-tier write |
| Display glass knobs | on the glass, and from the display's own mirror page over `POST /api/set` (Host + Origin + per-boot CSRF token, `write_blocked()` in `glass_web.cpp`). Every glass-knob write the iPhone app makes (the glass settings sheet's and the lamp color control's through `GlassAPI.set()`, in `GlassSettingsSheet.swift` and `LampColorControl.swift`, and the Nightlight section's directly, in `NightlightSection.swift`) posts to the same route through `NightlightAPI.set()`, which since #1986 reads the token from `GET /api/settings`, sends it as `X-CSRF-Token` and retries once on a `csrf` refusal (`GlassSettingsTests` pins the request; read in the code, not run on a device) | already editable on the glass and its mirror page; the app's writes carry the token | — | Unchanged. #1986 took the app's side of the choice and left `write_blocked()` as it was. Open (ledger A134): the Nightlight section posts with `try?` after it has set the value, so a refusal the token cannot cure (`on_glass_only`, `host`, `origin`) or a dropped connection leaves the screen showing a value the glass never took, where the glass settings sheet and the lamp color control re-read the glass and say the refusal. |
| Wi-Fi transmit cap, power save; BLE transmit power; CPU power policy | set by the firmware at build or at boot, and on the flagship and the WAP by the power policy as the power mode changes; no firmware asks a radio what it applied. One exception: the WAP's BLE transmit power is a stored setting that a session reads (`tx_power` on `GET /api/bluetooth` and `GET /api/bluetooth/settings`) and changes (`POST /api/bluetooth/power`, `POST /api/bluetooth/settings`); all four routes take `bt_auth_gated`, which takes a session | read-only | maybe | Owner + Host check + "Keep this setting?" with an automatic revert after 10 minutes, because a wrong value can drop the device off the network. Open: whether the WAP's two session writes of the BLE transmit power move to the bearer with it, as the camera's do, is the maintainer's to decide. |
| GNSS coarsening | fixed floor | read-only | coarser only | Owner |
| BLE advertising address mode | build (PR 20) | read-only | maybe | Owner |
| Crash record | — | "Saved crash: yes" (posture: owner, glass and USB only) | "Clear crash record" | Owner + a BOOT tap |

**Never adjustable from any app:**

- eFuses (no firmware and no app writes one; the only eFuse writes in the tree are the opt-in provisioning kit's `espefuse` `burn_key` and `burn_efuse` calls in `firmware/provisioning/provision_canary.sh`, run by hand over USB; PR 4's `lint_identifier_reads.py` is to keep the firmware so; both flashers stay read-only, `desktop/src-tauri/src/efuse.rs` and `canary-local/assets/intake.js`);
- secure boot and flash encryption (a provisioning ceremony, not a toggle);
- the partition table;
- any factory address;
- module-stored credentials;
- the radar's P1 vitals build gate;
- raw microphone access.

No new lan-tier or open write is ever added. Displays and the trio have no owner credential, so their new adjustments stay on the glass, USB or the broker until one exists.
