## 10. Lints and tests that keep it true

1. **`scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py --check`** (`lint.yml`; built in #1961, and `scripts/tests/test_gen_device_vocabulary.py` pins it). Today it holds its one output, `custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py`, byte-current, and refuses a row whose named firmware source does not emit its key. As the planned outputs join the script (§2.3), every generated output is byte-current, including the label regions in the page sources (a missing, duplicated or hand-edited region fails) and the HA fixture copies.
2. **`scripts/lint_device_details.py` (new in PR 3, `lint.yml`; not built at `62bc0bc7`):**
   - **Schema and ids.**
     - The schema is closed. The generator's `validate()` already refuses a field it does not know in the built file (#1961).
     - `families` equals the `firmware/flavors.json` names.
     - Every modality is a witness-dictionary id.
     - No key in the generated header has class `household`, `handle`, `reading`, `identifier` or `secret`.
     - Every reset word has an `idf_since`.
     - Every `gaps` entry names a fact in `facts` and families in `families`, and says what ends it.
   - **Text.**
     - The Invariant II word guard (§7.8).
     - The labels at L0-L3 pass `firmware/scripts/microcopy_banned_terms.txt`; those words live only in `term`.
     - **Time.** No unit `timestamp` or `epoch`, and no key ending in `_at`, `_ts` or `_epoch` (points in time). A key may end in `_ms`, `_s` or `_h` only when its row says `duration: true`. Every uptime fact is `_h` and names `coarse_uptime.h` (new in PR 8) as its source. Vision's `perf()` timings are therefore spellable (`infer_ms`, `duration: true`, class `activity`).
   - **Status earned** (§7.1), rung by rung, per board. Driven requires the part's driver compiled, with its gating flag on, into a shipped configuration of that board, from `firmware/scripts/shipped_configs.py` (new in PR 3); once the family's glue reports the part (PR 15), it also requires the glue's `put` there (§7.1). Driven (bench only) requires the same in unshipped configurations only, and is the ceiling for an `unreleased` family. Bench-gated requires a driver cite that resolves in a source the board's env compiles, a flag that is 0 in its shipped configuration, and an env that builds the driver for the board's flavor with the flag on. Staged requires `HAS_<X> 1` and no driver compiled or compilable from the board's own sources. The static copy may never contain a runtime word.
   - **Legacy answers may not grow** (§3.6), and each key reaches only the tier its row says.
   - **`held` is honest** (§4.1): no fact is counted as held for a tier that a recorded legacy row gives it on that family, and a part kind's held count covers only its own sheet's facts.
   - **Serial stays serial.** The display's `details_glue.cpp` (new in PR 10) names neither `log_line` nor `g_log_sink`, so a serial-tier line never reaches `/api/log` (§5.4).
   - **Disclosures held to the code** (§7.2), **per family and per shipped configuration** (§7.1), `FEATURE_*` flags included, the way `firmware/projects/canary-wap/tests_host/check_route_budget.py` resolves the WAP's profiles. A tree-wide grep is not enough: PR 20 adds the address-policy calls default-off, so the calls exist while every shipped image still uses the factory addresses.

     | Disclosure | Held to |
     |---|---|
     | mDNS keys | per family: for the flagship and the trio, parsed from `firmware/common/network/mdns_txt.h`, which `securacv_network.cpp` and each trio `src/net/mdns_mgr.cpp` build their TXT from; for the WAP, from the `MDNS.addServiceTxt` calls in `canary_wap.ino`; for the displays, from those in `src/net/discovery.cpp`. Neither of the last two includes `mdns_txt.h`, so the header's keys they never send (`caps`, `coord`, `tls`, `secure_port`, and on the WAP `hw`) are not listed for them |
     | The beacon | `firmware/scripts/check_fleet_beacon_sync.sh` |
     | The chirp | parsed from `firmware/common/fleet_link/fleet_chirp.h`, the one header that spells the advert since #1961; the WAP's `ble_chirp.h` sends what its `fleet_chirp_build()` writes, and the same sync script holds the WAP's staged copy |
     | "Bluetooth address: fixed by the factory address" | true for a family exactly while its shipped configurations do not compile `ble_addr_policy.h` ON (`FEATURE_BLE_RANDOM_ADDR` resolved per configuration) |
     | The setup BSSID row | the same per-family test for `ap_mac_policy.h`, whose host test proves the address is random (§12, PR 20); for the displays, whether `src/net/provision.cpp` calls it |
     | The station-address row | always true; no fix in this design flips it |
     | The USB serial row | every `ARDUINO_USB_MODE=0` env defining `USB_SERIAL` |
     | The public WAP fields | `identity_json.h write_device_info` |
     | List 6's exception lines | from PR 4, generated from `lint_identifier_reads.py`'s `KNOWN_LEAK` rows, per family |

     A fix that turns a row's condition true for a family lands with that row's flip in the same change, or the lint fails. A fix behind a default-off flag changes no row.
   - **Address-valued answer keys (from PR 4, which brings `KNOWN_LEAK`).** It fails when a value derived from `NimBLEDevice::getAddress`, `ble_hs_id_copy_addr`, `WiFi.macAddress`, `WiFi.softAPmacAddress`, `esp_read_mac` or `esp_wifi_get_mac` reaches a JSON answer key or a print path, unless a `KNOWN_LEAK` row names that site. A keyed one-way derivation that item 3's allowlist names is not such a value: the API token, an HMAC over the station address keyed from the private key (`derive_api_token()`, in `canary_wap.ino` and in the flagship's `securacv_crypto.cpp`), which the WAP prints over USB. The WAP's `local_address` (`doc["local_address"]` in `bluetooth_api.h`'s `handle_bluetooth_status()`) is a `KNOWN_LEAK` row until PR 8.
   - **Part sheets.** Every `out` destination names a contract (§7.1), and every `path#token` cite resolves.
   - **App side.**
     - Any string literal shaped like a details key in `ios/`, `tvos/`, `canary-local/assets/`, `desktop/src/`, the WAP and display pages, or `custom_components/securacv/` must be in the vocabulary.
     - No details key literal, and no reference to `DeviceDetailsVocab`, under `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWidgets/`, `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWatchWidgets/` or `ios/Sources/SecuraCVNotificationService/`, or in `ios/Shared/FleetGlanceViews.swift`, `ios/Shared/FleetActivityAttributes.swift` and `ios/Shared/WakePayload.swift`; no read of a row's `firmware` in `FleetGlanceViews.swift`, `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWatchWidgets/FindCanaryWidget.swift` and `ios/Sources/SecuraCVWatch/WitnessNotification.swift`, and no read of a row's `hardware` in the first two (§6.5). The rule is scoped by source path, not by target, because `ios/project.yml` compiles `ios/Shared` into both widget targets. The generated `ios/Shared/DeviceDetailsVocab.swift` is on no scanned path, so it needs no exemption, and a planted widget file and a planted Shared glance view must each fail.
     - The details views may contain no literal row labels.
3. **`scripts/lint_identifier_reads.py` (new in PR 4, `lint.yml` and `firmware.yml`; not built at `62bc0bc7`, ledger F430).** One deny exists that refuses a read wherever it appears: `desktop_parity.test.js`'s `sscmaIdCalls()` (F384, #1916), which fails on any call of a method named `ID` in Vision's sources; PR 4 folds it in. The other identifier check, `regression_check.sh`'s three MAC patterns (the last bullet), is narrower: it refuses the eFuse MAC formatted as a MAC string, and `WiFi.macAddress()` or `ESP.getEfuseMac()` on a line that names no derivation.
   - It greps `firmware/**` (every tree, `.ino` included) for each `never` API, including every eFuse trim and calibration field descriptor (`ESP_EFUSE_K_RTC_LDO`, `ESP_EFUSE_TEMP_CALIB`, `ESP_EFUSE_OCODE`, `ESP_EFUSE_ADC*`, and the others in §2.2).
   - **Allowlist.** Today's legitimate uses are named by file, function and reason, for example the API-token derivation's `esp_read_mac` (`derive_api_token()`), the CSI HAL's one held copy of the router's BSSID (`s_assoc_bssid` in `csi_hal.cpp`, compared against each frame's sender), and the opt-in provisioning kit's `espefuse` `burn_key` and `burn_efuse` calls (`burn_flash_encryption_key()` and `burn_security_efuses()` in `firmware/provisioning/provision_canary.sh`, run by hand over USB). An entry that stops matching fails. The grep also reaches bench sketches no release builds, which need a row of their own here or in `KNOWN_LEAK`, or a stated exclusion: `firmware/projects/canary-tincan/arduino/tincan_bringup/tincan_bringup.ino`, which is not in `firmware/flavors.json`, calls `WiFi.macAddress(mac)`, keeps the last two bytes as `g_my_id`, and prints them over USB ("my id: %04x") and on its screen ("me: %04x").
   - **The `never` rows' `except` entries** (§2.2) are read from the rows and printed as list 6's sanctioned lines (§7.2). They are part of the design, so they are neither allowlist entries nor `KNOWN_LEAK` rows, and the lint fails if one site appears in two of the three lists.
   - **`KNOWN_LEAK`.** Today's leaks are listed with the finding and the families they belong to. The list must shrink to empty, family by family, as the fixes are compiled on in released envs. Its rows feed list 6's exception lines (§7.2).
   - It also fails on:
     - any eFuse write or burn API;
     - any assignment to `boot_info_t.mac_address`;
     - `NimBLEDevice::getAddress` in an answer or print path;
     - `AI.ID(`, or an echo of `AI.info(` or `AI.name(`;
     - a call of the SSCMA library's image functions, `save_jpeg(` or `last_image(`, in Vision's sources (§7.3);
     - any `ARDUINO_USB_MODE=0` env without `USB_SERIAL`.
   - Planted self-tests run every time.
   - It supersedes the three patterns in `firmware/scripts/regression_check.sh`'s section "Privacy: MAC address handling (F-03)" as the primary gate. Those stay as a second net.
4. **`firmware/tests_host/test_device_details.cpp` (new in PR 9):**
   - exact bytes per part per tier;
   - each part's worst case, **per sensor kind**, is at most its cap, and `about` is at most 512;
   - refuse-whole on a short buffer;
   - an absent fact is omitted;
   - **`held` is the generated constant.** Two `Facts` that differ only in posture values, or in which posture facts are present (no crash record against a saved one, no AP against an AP), produce byte-identical `lan` and `broker` output;
   - **the scene cannot move a `lan` page.** A scripted MR60 stream (present, then empty, then present) produces byte-identical `lan` sensor pages and link views, and a scripted RSSI trace that crosses a signal-word threshold back and forth (a body in the path) produces byte-identical `lan` and `broker` `now` parts while the same trace moves the word at the serial tier, so the test proves the writer holds the signal rather than that the trace missed a threshold;
   - **`held` per kind.** A BH1750 or PIR page at `lan` carries `held` 0 and the kind answers no link view; the MR60's link view at `lan` carries no value and a `held` equal to its sheet's activity facts;
   - the `lan` and `broker` tiers carry no counter, posture or activity key;
   - the reset-word map compiled under an IDF 4.4 shim and an IDF 5.5 shim, with `ESP_RST_USB` mapped on the second;
   - escaping of untrusted text;
   - measured length equals written length;
   - the broker part plus its topic fits the MQTT buffer of each family that publishes through PubSubClient (every family but the WAP, which publishes through esp_mqtt), computed with the bound in `PubSubClient::publish()`, `5 + 2 + strlen(topic) + plength`.

   Beside it, `test_coarse_uptime.cpp` (new, PR 8) proves that `up_h` never exceeds the true uptime, that its step instants are not aligned to the boot, and that every reader on one device steps at the same instant.

   It compares the firmware writer's bytes to `spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json` **(new)**, which `gen_device_vocabulary.py`'s reference writer (new in PR 9) writes (§2.3); the test writes nothing. The same fixture is decoded by Swift (`ios/Tests/SecuraCVTests/DeviceDetailsTests.swift`, new), JS (`canary-local/tests/details.test.js`, new), Rust (`desktop/flash-engine` and `tvos/witness-core`), and Python, which reads the generated copy at `custom_components/securacv/tests/fixtures/details_v1.json` so the HACS mirror's CI can run it (`custom_components/securacv/tests/test_details.py`, new). Format, share-projection and provenance-wording vectors from the vocabulary run in each language.
5. **WAP.** The scripts and tests below exist unless marked new; what each gains for this design is planned:
   - `firmware/projects/canary-wap/tests_host/check_route_budget.py`, with every config at a margin of 8 or more (its `MARGIN`);
   - `firmware/projects/canary-wap/tests_host/check_route_security.py`: the seam as a gate and the `Tier::Owner` branch rule with plants (PR 8), and no `SECRET_ANSWERS` entry for the details handler (PR 12);
   - `firmware/scripts/check_wap_json_answers.py` J13 (new in PR 12, §5.3) and its mutation self-test;
   - `check_details_sync.sh` (new in PR 8: the staged `coarse_uptime.h` from PR 8; `self_manifest.h`, `device_details.h` and the keys from PR 9), `check_improv_sync.sh` (the staged `host_guard.h`, from #1848), `check_wap_loop_commands.py`, `firmware/tests_host/test_dashboard_route_match.test.js`;
   - `test_auth_logic.cpp`: the seam's order, and on `/api/details` the Host before any grant;
   - `gen_web_assets_gz.py --check` (`firmware.yml` runs it) with the ceiling, which is planned: today the check compares bytes only, and `/admin`'s gzip is already past the ceiling (§5.3, ledger F479);
   - `microcopy_lint.sh`;
   - run `gen_wap.py` and diff `canary-local/devices/wap.json` (it has no `--check`; `canary-local.yml`'s "WAP data drift" step does exactly this, and the "Hub data drift" step after it does the same for `gen_homeassistant.py`, which reads `wap.json`), then `gen_csp.py --check`.
6. **Flagship:** `check_route_budgets()` in `firmware/canary/scripts/check_route_security.py` gains a `MIN_SPARE = 2` assertion per variant, with a planted case that must fail (PR 14). It does not read the slot comment, so the comment's arithmetic is corrected by hand and reviewed. F338's rule landed in #1852 as that script's `SECRET_ANSWERS` table; the details handler gets no row in it, because it answers no secret.
7. **Displays and the trio:** `firmware/scripts/check_lan_routes.py` **(new in PR 11)** is these servers' first route-posture check. It checks five rules:
   - only `/api/status` and `/api/fleet` may send CORS headers (true of `glass_web.cpp` and `node_http.cpp` today);
   - `/api/details`, `/api/device`, `/api/log` and `/api/glass` refuse a foreign Host first (`/api/glass` does, in `handle_glass()`, and since #1986 `/api/device` and `/api/log` do too; PR 11 adds the route and the Origin half, §13);
   - every POST goes through the existing write guards;
   - `/api/log`'s lines carry no time stamp (P22). (The rule that the display's `q` glue never writes to the log sink is `lint_device_details.py`'s, item 2.)
   - `peer_link.cpp`'s `begin()` seeds the heartbeat's frame counter from `peer::seq_start()`, so `seq` does not count up from 0 at each boot (P9, PR 11).

   Plus `firmware/tests_host/test_host_guard.cpp` (exists), and `firmware/tests_host/test_node_status.cpp` (exists), which from PR 11 asserts that the body carries no `rssi`, no signal word and no `wall_polled_s_ago` for any RSSI, the same threshold trace included (P23). Today `node_status.h` writes both keys.
8. **Apple.** The four lints exist and run in `lint.yml`: `lint_apple_parity.py`; `lint_ios_keychain_reads.py`, to be extended in PR 5 to instance methods that reach a reader (`DeviceStore.token(for:)`, `api(for:)`), with a planted case in `scripts/tests/test_lint_ios_keychain_reads.py` (today it derives readers from `static func`s only); `lint_apple_plists.py`; `lint_cloudkit_container.py`. With them: contract belts that read the producers by `#filePath` (new ones in PR 5), and the WristSnapshot pinned-JSON tests (`WristSnapshotTests.swift`).
9. **Flashers.** What #1970 (PR 1) added to these existing tests, unless marked:
   - `canary-local/tests/desktop_parity.test.js` (§6.10).
   - `canary-local/tests/flash.test.js`: `sanitizeLogTail()`, with `TAIL_REDACT` (credential lines) and `TAIL_IDENTITY` (identity lines), fed the banner lines as the firmware's own format strings print them and a self-manifest (`j`) line, and from PR 10 real `q` lines; no MAC bytes on screen by default; saved and shared reports carry no MAC bytes; the default save is the share projection (no counter, posture, chain prefix or instant); file names carry no MAC digits. Ledger A132 lists the serial lines the scrubber still passes.
   - `cargo test` for `flash-engine` and `src-tauri` (`desktop-hub-core.yml`). The eFuse decoders keep only allowlisted fields, and a word with every masked trim bit set decodes byte-identically to one with them clear, per chip (`efuse.rs`). `rescue::backup_filename` takes no MAC.
10. **Home Assistant:**
    - the redaction set derived from the vocabulary (built, #1961: `test_the_redaction_set_names_every_identifier_class` and `test_the_redaction_set_is_the_integration_s_keys_and_the_vocabulary_s` in `test_diagnostics.py`);
    - registry values are `str` (built, #1891: `test_device_registry_versions.py`);
    - a details-topic test (PR 16);
    - a device-diagnostics test that projects real health fixtures from the flagship, the WAP and a Sense and asserts that no uptime, counter, activity or identifier survives (PR 16; the health bodies it reads have been in `tests/fixtures/canary_mqtt_payloads.json` since #1891);
    - every fixture read from `custom_components/securacv/tests/fixtures/`, so the mirror runs the same tests; a test that also holds a fixture to the firmware or the apps skips in the mirror, as `test_device_registry_versions.py`'s four monorepo-only checks do;
    - `test_entity_translations.py` and `test_icons.py` (both exist) if entities come later.
11. **CI path filters.** Every PR that adds a reader of a file outside its workflow's trees adds that path to **both** the `push` and `pull_request` lists of every workflow that runs the reader (`.github/CI.md` R5 and R6).
    - `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json` (the block's file since #1961, §2.1): `canary-local.yml` from PR 3, because `gen_flash.py` then reads the block and the "Flash catalog drift" step runs there. `ios-selfheal.yml` lists `spec/witness_dictionary.json` and not this file, so PR 5 adds it with the first Swift test that reads the block by `#filePath`. `lint.yml`, which runs the generator's `--check` and its test, has no path filter.
    - `ios-selfheal.yml` has only a `pull_request` list. PR 5 adds the producers its contract belts read by `#filePath` (`sys_monitor.h`, `canary_wap.ino` for the WAP's `/api/status` body, `glass_web.cpp`, `node_status.h`), and `spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json` joins it with the first Swift test that reads the fixture.
    - `spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json`: `firmware.yml` from PR 9 (the host test that compares against it); `canary-local.yml` and `desktop-hub-core.yml` (flash-engine's `cargo test` runs there, not in `rust.yml`, which is the kernel's) from PR 10; `tvos.yml` from PR 19.
    - `canary-local/devices/device_details.json`: both path lists of `desktop-hub-core.yml` from PR 10, because `desktop/src-tauri/build.rs` is to bake it beside `flash.json`, which both lists already carry.
    - The HA tests need no filter change: `python.yml` already watches `custom_components/**`, and the fixtures live there.
    - Gates: `scripts/check_path_filter_reads.py` (canary-local's logic tests, and `viewer.yml`) and `.github/scripts/ci_policy_check.py` (`workflows-lint.yml`). `scripts/gates.py` (#1923) lists the gates a diff trips, read from the workflows' own path filters.
12. **Everywhere:**
    - `lint_spelling.py`, `lint_fleet_word.py`;
    - `lint_docs_index.py`, `lint_md_links.py`, `lint_glossary_status.py`;
    - `gen_agent_entrypoints.py --check` when AGENTS.md's index gains the entry;
    - `firmware/flavors.json` size guards;
    - the checks PR 6 (#1971) and PR 7 (#1892) built for the names and module facts a Details screen inherits: `firmware/scripts/check_board_registry.py` check 10 (the names and board tokens a build reports come from its board), `firmware/tests_host/test_honest_names.test.js` (the pages' Firmware, Kind and signal rows), `custom_components/securacv/tests/test_absent_battery.py`, and `firmware/tests_host/test_vision_module_link.cpp` and `test_sentinel_links.cpp`;
    - for the display PRs, the Dist drift check and the page logic tests (`canary_local.test.js`, `vision.test.js`).

---

## 11. Privacy findings, and where each stands

The design's privacy review produced 24 privacy findings, P1 to P24. Of them, 18 are open in the backlog, `docs/audit/repo_todo_sweep_2026-09.md` (**F357, F375 to F377, F379 to F383, F389, F390, F393 to F396, F430, A132 and A133**), and 6 are fixed (**P8, P11, P13, P18, P19 and P24**). `scripts/tests/test_ledger_numbers.py`, which landed with this document, holds that sentence, the table below, the backlog and the `docs/README.md` index line to each other.

How to read the table:

- **Home.** `ledger <id>` names the open backlog item that holds the finding. The item carries the evidence as file paths and quoted phrases, what remains and its tag, and it cites its P-number back. `fixed in #N` names the PR or PRs that fixed the finding; a fixed finding has no backlog item, because the backlog holds open items only. `ledger <id>; part fixed in #N` means that PR fixed part of the finding and the item holds the rest.
- **PR n** is this plan's PR (§12). The backlog calls it "device-details PR n".
- **Numbers.** Fourteen of the open items were filed when this document landed, numbered from F375 to F396 in P order. Four were on main before it: F357, which #1848 filed for the wildcard P4 also found; F430, which #1919 filed for P15's lint; and A132 and A133, which #1970 filed for what it left of P14 and P12. Merged PRs and code comments call three fixed findings by the numbers the design's branch gave them: P11 is F384, P18 is F391 and P19 is F392. No backlog item carries those numbers.
- **Evidence.** Each row was read from the code at `0a06c14b` on 2026-11-16 and again at `62bc0bc7`, after the 2.4.20 train, and the SDK facts from the sources the header lists. Nothing here was checked on a device.

| # | Home | Finding | What a fix did, and what remains |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | ledger F375 | Every Bluetooth advertiser sends the chip's public address, which the factory MAC fixes: base + 1 on the S3, base + 2 on the C3 and C6. `docs/security/THREAT_MODEL.md` and `docs/security/SECURITY_MODEL.md` call it the vendor's OUI and nothing more. | Open. PR 4 corrects the prose per chip. PR 20 adds a random static address per boot for broadcast-only advertisers and a resolvable one for the WAP's bonded console, behind `FEATURE_BLE_RANDOM_ADDR`, off until a reader audit and a bench run. |
| P2 | ledger F376 | `canary-wap-usbdrive` and the flagship's `usb-onboard`, two opt-in envs that CI compiles and no release ships, put the full factory MAC in the USB serial-number descriptor. | Open. PR 4 sets `-DUSB_SERIAL=\"0\"` in both and adds the env rule to the identifier lint. Whether the stock images' USB Serial/JTAG controller reports the MAC too is a bench question. |
| P3 | ledger F377 | The setup network's BSSID is fixed by the factory address. On the S3 it is `esp_derive_local_mac()` of the base MAC: the locally administered bit set, the other five bytes the factory's. On the C3 and C6 it is the base MAC plus one. The two security docs say it "carries Espressif's OUI", which is wrong for an S3 SoftAP, and `scripts/tests/test_docs_claims.py` holds that sentence twice. | Open. PR 4 corrects the prose per chip and the test's two strings. PR 20 draws an address at random per boot or per setup session, never a function of the eFuse MAC, and sets it before any SoftAP starts. The locally administered bit proves nothing on its own: the S3's address already has it. |
| P4 | ledger F357 | The WAP's `GET /api/wifi` and `GET /api/wifi/scan` answer on the home network with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. A page the owner opens can read the home network's name and IP, the seconds since the WAP joined it (`connected_sec`) and the neighbors' network names if it can address the device, and a scan request can move the radio. | Open, as a dated note inside F357, which #1848 filed for the same wildcard. PR 8: no CORS header; the full answer only on the SoftAP interface or to a session, and status words otherwise; the signal (`/api/wifi`'s `rssi` and each scanned network's) and the three SoftAP keys of P21 only on the SoftAP interface or to the bearer (§3.2); on a standalone (AP-only) WAP that interface is every caller's, so a session there still reads them (§3.6). `/api/fleet`'s wildcard and the Host question stay F357's own. |
| P5 | ledger F379 | The WAP's `GET /api/selftest` gives any host on the home network, after the join, the network's name, IP, channel and signal, the microphone's level, the battery voltage, the uptime in milliseconds and the last crash's stage and reason. It is allowlisted as a wizard route "on the AP" and answers on every interface. | Open. PR 8: full metrics only on the SoftAP interface, verdict words elsewhere, and the microphone level on no open route; on a standalone (AP-only) WAP that interface is every caller's, so a session there still reads the crash's cause and stage (§3.6). The Wi-Fi row's `code` is the signal in dBm and its `detail` repeats the name and the signal, and the battery row's `code` is the charge percent; whether a row's `code` and `detail` leave the other interfaces with its metrics is open (§12 PR 8, §14). |
| P6 | ledger F380 | The WAP's public `GET /api/device-info` serves `hw_token` and the uptime in milliseconds, and no page reads either there. `/api/system` serves `hw_token` to any session, for the administrator page's `#sysMac` row. | Open. PR 8 drops `hw_token` from both (it stays in the provisioning receipt, which no session reaches, and which the WAP's console prints at every boot, §7.2 list 5) and `uptime_ms` from `/api/device-info`, and moves the pinned bytes in `test_identity_json.cpp` and the `RECORDED` row in `check_wap_json_answers.py`. |
| P7 | ledger F381 | A WAP dashboard session receives nearby and paired devices' Bluetooth addresses and names, their signal and distance, when each was last seen or connected, and the WAP's own Bluetooth address. | Open. PR 8 drops `local_address`, gives a session per-boot salted row ids that the device maps back for forget, trust and block, and sends names, addresses, signal, distance and each device's times and counts to the bearer only. The administrator page's Bluetooth panel changes with it. |
| P8 | fixed in #1887 | The WAP wrote a pairing passkey's six digits into its health log, which the SD card keeps, `/api/logs` serves to a session and the Bluetooth log export serves to a bonded reader. | #1887 writes both lines ("Pairing PIN displayed" and the "awaiting user confirmation" line) with no digits, and #1836, merged after it, keeps them so. Nothing is left for PR 8. The digits on `GET /api/bluetooth` were sweep F346, which #1887 also closed: a Passkey Entry's reach only the bearer, and a Numeric Comparison's stay in every answer, a session's included, because both ends compute them and the confirm that grants takes the bearer. When a bond is owner proof is a separate open question (§14). |
| P9 | ledger F382 | Uptime to the second or the millisecond on open answers and broadcasts dates every reboot: `uptime_s` on the CORS-open `/api/status` of the displays and the trio; `up_s` on the display's `/api/device`; `uptime_ms` on the WAP's public `/api/device-info` and `/api/selftest`; the WAP's plain-read Bluetooth uptime characteristic (DEV and FULL builds) and its chirp's hours since boot; the trio's multicast peer heartbeat, every 10 s, with `uptime_s` and a `seq` that restarts at each boot; and a FULL WAP's opera frames, with the seconds since boot, the seconds since the mesh started and a send counter whose place in its 1,024 step counts the frames since the boot. | Open; every value is still sent. #1961 corrected only the chirp's comment, which had called its hours since boot an epoch bucket. PRs 8 and 11: wherever an uptime stays, whole hours from a per-boot random offset through one host-tested helper, because a plain hour floor still shows the step at boot + N hours to anyone polling; the rest dropped, or sent as 0 where no receiver reads it; and each boot's opera counters resumed past a random skip. How the heartbeat keeps #1932's reboot window beside a `seq` and an `uptime_s` that do not date the boot is the maintainer's decision (§5.5, §14). |
| P10 | ledger F383; part fixed in #1986 | The display's `/api/device` and `/api/log` skip the Host guard that `/api/glass` and every write ask. `/api/device` serves the home network's name, the IP, the signal in dBm, the device id, the uptime to the second and `heap_min_kb`, the heap's low-water mark, which §3.1 classes as posture. | Part fixed: #1986 refuses a foreign Host first on both routes (`host_is_foreign()`, pinned by `test_glass_web_host_guard.cpp`). Open in F383: PR 11 refuses a cross-site Origin on both and drops `ssid`, `signal`, `heap_min_kb` and `up_s`. The mirror page, the one reader, moves to `/api/details` **(new)**, whose lan tier carries no signal (§5.4). |
| P11 | fixed in #1892 | Vision's `module_id` was a RAM address (the SSCMA library's cached buffer, cast to an int), so the desktop's live-boot-line proof passed a dead module, and the obvious fix would have published a per-unit module id. | #1892 (PR 7; sweep F384 in main's CHANGELOG and code comments): `module_id` is 1 or 0 and `module_ok` says the same, the firmware makes no `ID()` call, the `invoke()` inversion is fixed, and the desktop's proof needs both values. #1916 and #1919 added the deny on any `ID(` call in Vision's sources (`sscmaIdCalls()` in `desktop_parity.test.js`). One read is left outside this tree's code: the pinned SSCMA library's own `begin()` asks the module once at boot, and nothing reads the answer (§12 PR 7, §14). The general identifier lint is P15's. |
| P12 | ledger A133; part fixed in #1970 | Both flashers showed the board's full MAC, wrote it into saved reports and named files with its unit-unique half. The desktop keys its fleet book and each board's stored API key by it. The help text said the firmware never broadcasts it, when it is the station address in every Wi-Fi frame. | #1970 (PR 1): no address bytes on screen until asked ("Hardware address", "Show"), none copied, none in a saved report or a file name (a day and a counter instead), the fleet list included, and the help text corrected; `desktop_parity.test.js` and `flash.test.js` pin it. Open in A133: the desktop still keys the fleet book and the keychain item by the address. PR 18 re-keys both by device id and moves what is stored. |
| P13 | fixed in #1970 | Both flashers sent `ID?` to the Grove Vision AI module. The browser showed the answer and the module's own free-text model name, which can name a capability the project forbids, and the desktop returned the answer in the install receipt. | #1970 (PR 1): neither flasher nor the Lab's native bench asks for the id (`VER?` only), the module's own model text is never shown, and a model is named only when the install was the project's pinned one. `desktop_parity.test.js` pins it. |
| P14 | ledger A132; part fixed in #1970 | The flashers' shareable diagnostic report kept the boot banner's "Hardware ID" line and the self-manifest line (fingerprint, `device_id`, `boots`), and once the flashers send `q` it would pass serial-tier details lines. | #1970 (PR 1): `sanitizeLogTail()` in both flashers replaces the lines `TAIL_IDENTITY` names, those two among them, with a visible marker, tested against the firmware's own format strings. Open in A132: a line no pattern names still passes, so the tail is not public-only by construction, and each details line is replaced by its share projection once firmware answers `q` (PR 10). |
| P15 | ledger F430 | No build check stops a new read of a chip's other unique ids (the eFuse optional unique id, the flash chip's id, the calibration trims, the 802.15.4 EUI-64, an SD card's identity register, the Bluetooth address) or an eFuse write, and `boot_info_t` still carries a `mac_address` slot that `boot_banner.cpp` would print. | Open. `regression_check.sh` checks three MAC reads, and the one identifier deny that exists is P11's. PR 4 builds `scripts/lint_identifier_reads.py` **(new)** (§10 item 3). PR 17, the dist PR, removes the field. F430 was filed for the lint alone and now holds the whole finding. |
| P16 | ledger F389 | `creds_at_rest` is a posture word on the CORS-open `/api/status` of the displays and the trio. It is harmless while every build says `plaintext-nvs`, and a differential once a shipped build can report another word. | Open, as a gate. Decided (§14): it stays as an honest warning while every build says `plaintext-nvs`, and moves behind owner proof in the first PR after which a shipped build can report another word; §14 says what owner proof is on each family (the displays and the trio have none, §3.7). By `docs/PARITY.md` (footnote 1) that is the NVS-encryption migration; the secure kit does not change the word. The maintainer confirms the trigger's wording. |
| P17 | ledger F390 | The WAP's Device Information Service, readable before pairing with no security flag, serves the whole key fingerprint as its serial number, and falls back to the Bluetooth address when the fingerprint is empty. No app, flasher or firmware reads that serial. | Open. PR 4 makes the serial empty: never the fingerprint, never the address. That removes one of the sources the fingerprint rules in §6.4, §6.5 and P19 guard against, not all of them: the Bluetooth name and the beacons keep the fingerprint's last two bytes on air for good, a FULL WAP that holds an opera still sends the whole fingerprint in every opera frame, and a WAP that is pairing sends its whole public key in the pairing frames (§7.2 list 1, §14). |
| P18 | fixed in #1891, #1916 and #1961 | Home Assistant's diagnostics redaction set lacked the identifier keys a details projection would carry. | #1891 (PR 2; sweep F391 in main's CHANGELOG) named eight keys: `hw_token`, `mdns_host`, `serial_number`, `chip_id`, `module_id`, `local_address`, `address` and `peer_address`. #1916 added five health counters as a second net: `uptime`, `boot_count`, `sd_writes`, `http_requests` and `last_frame_age_ms`. #1961 derived the set from `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json`; `serial_number` and `chip_id` stay literals in `diagnostics.py`, because no firmware source emits them (ledger HA62). Six other uptime keys are in no redacted class yet. That is ledger HA63, which #1961 filed, and it is not part of this finding. |
| P19 | fixed in #1891 | Home Assistant's config-entry diagnostics dump, which its own docstring says gets attached to GitHub issues, kept 8 of the 16 hex digits of each Canary's key fingerprint, while a WAP's Device Information Service serves the whole fingerprint to anyone nearby (P17). | #1891 (PR 2; sweep F392 in main's CHANGELOG), as decided (§14): the dump keeps no digit and gives each Canary a per-dump salted correlation id, spelled in letters that are not hex digits. Fewer digits would not have been a fix: four still pick one device out of the few in range, and the last four are on air in the Bluetooth name for good. |
| P20 | ledger F393 | A WAP dashboard session, which any host on the network can mint, reads counters, activity and the status strip's two posture reasons: the boot count, the witness count, the chain sequence, whether live view is in use, the SD card's writes, the egress counters, the uptime in seconds, the strip's `restarted` and `low_memory`, and on FULL builds the mesh's id and peers. | Open. Decided (§14): recorded, not moved, until the owner-authority PR. That PR, #1887, moved none of them, so PR 8 answers them only to the bearer, which the signed-in administrator page sends (§3.2, §3.7). The health log, the witness records, the chain and the live view stay on the session, so a session still reads the witness count, the chain sequence, the live view's state and the uptime to the second in other answers (§3.2, §7.2 list 2). PR 8 also takes a raise of the sensing's privacy ceiling to the bearer and, on its first boot, lowers a stored ceiling above P0 once; the maintainer confirms the lowering (§14). |
| P21 | ledger F394 | The WAP serves raw posture to any session, and the SoftAP's protection to any host on the network. A session reads the reset cause word, the crash flag, the heap's low-water mark and its largest block on `/api/system`, `/api/status` and `/api/diagnostics`. With no session, the public `/api/device-info` gives `ap_auth` and `chain_length`, and the public, CORS-open `/api/wifi` gives `ap_auth`, `ap_auth_reason` and `sta_pmf`. The crash's cause and stage on the public `/api/selftest` are P5's. | Open. PR 8: every raw posture value these answers carry only to the bearer, but `/api/status`'s `safe_mode` and `node_status`'s `creds_at_rest`, which stay with a session (P16, §3.2), and `/api/wifi`'s three also on the SoftAP interface; `ap_auth` and `chain_length` dropped from `/api/device-info`, which nothing reads. The administrator page's Reset row returns in the owner-tier Protection panel, which PR 12 adds once `/admin` has room for it (§5.3). Open for the maintainer (§14): the health log's reset line and the tamper word on `/api/events/today` and `/api/csi/stream` still tell a session whether the last restart was a crash, `/api/status`'s `safe_mode` whether three came in a row, and `/api/selftest`'s `safe_mode` and `/api/help-qr`'s QR tell any host on the network; and on FULL builds the health log's flash-encryption lines tell a session whether flash encryption is off. |
| P22 | ledger F395; part fixed in #1986 | The display mirrors its console to the network: `/api/log` serves the last 48 lines, each stamped with the uptime to a tenth of a second, household and update events among them, with no Host guard. | Part fixed: #1986 refuses a foreign Host first (`host_is_foreign()`, as on `/api/device`, P10). Decided (§14): the route stays on the lan tier, and the glass switch is not taken now. Open in F395: PR 11 adds the Origin refusal and drops the stamps. The `q` output never goes through `log_line()` or `g_log_sink` (§5.4). |
| P23 | ledger F396 | The CORS-open `/api/status` of the displays and the trio carries the Wi-Fi signal in dBm, which moves with bodies in the room, and `wall_polled_s_ago`, which says when something last polled `/api/fleet`. The trio's peer heartbeat multicasts the same signal every 10 s (§5.5). | Open. PR 11 drops `rssi` with no word in its place (a word is the same reading cut at thresholds, so the signal is activity, §3.1), drops `wall_polled_s_ago` (the `wall` word stays), and sends 0 in the heartbeat's signal byte, which receivers only store. No app reads either key from `/api/status`. |
| P24 | fixed in #1970 | Both flashers' saved health reports carried the lifetime boot counter, the witness record count, the firmware's tamper flag, a chain-head prefix, crash-record and update state and a millisecond timestamp, and nothing on either save button said the file describes one board. | #1970 (PR 1): the default save is the share projection (§6.4), a list of what it keeps, with the day in place of the timestamp and without the eFuse security scan. A separately labeled "Save full report for your own records" keeps the rest, still with no hardware address, under a note that it describes this one board. `flash.test.js` and `desktop_parity.test.js` hold both flashers to it. |

---

## 12. PR plan

Each PR is small enough to review alone. Four have landed, each host-tested only: PR 1 (#1970), PR 2 (#1891), PR 6 (#1971) and PR 7 (#1892). #1961 built one slice of PR 3, and one case of PR 4's lint exists (ledger F430). The section of each below says what main built, where that differs from the plan, and where the rest of its scope went. **PRs 1, 2, 3 and 5 each ship something a person can see, and none of them changes firmware.** PRs 1 and 2 changed no file under `firmware/`. PR 3's generator writes nothing there, because it gains its firmware outputs only in the PRs that first compile them (§2.3); the one file PR 3 adds under `firmware/` is a script no image compiles, `firmware/scripts/shipped_configs.py` **(new)**, and since `firmware.yml` watches `firmware/**`, PR 3 does start that workflow. PR 4 is a lint, two build-flag fixes, one DIS string and one THREAT_MODEL principle. The privacy fixes a Details screen would otherwise inherit land before the first new firmware route.

Every PR that adds a reader of a file outside its workflow's trees also adds that path to both path lists of each workflow that runs it (§10 item 11). The ledger and the commits call these "device-details PR n".
