### PR 1: Both flashers, About this board read from the chip

**Landed as #1970** (commit `c190b1fe`). No firmware change. Host-tested only; nothing ran on a device. §5.6 is the account of what the card reads and what it never keeps, and §6.9 to §6.11 say where each frontend draws it. What shipped:

- An **About this board** card in both flashers' own UI code and in the Lab's native USB bench (`flash-native.js`, §6.11), from one row model, `aboutBoardGroups()` in `canary-local/assets/flash-core.js`, which `desktop/src/app.js` carries as a copy. The browser's hello card and the desktop's This Board card are L1, "More about this board" is L2 and "Show technical names" is L4. Chip, eFuse-field and JEDEC rows are labeled "Read from the chip". Bootloader-header and app-descriptor rows are labeled "From the installed image", and the image's declared flash size sits beside the chip's JEDEC size.
- **Desktop.**
  - `detect_chip` keeps espflash's chip line, revision, crystal and features (`ChipInfo` in `desktop/flash-engine/src/flash.rs`; `canonical_chip()` keeps its guard role).
  - The card shows the IDF version the passport already parsed.
  - The passport reads `secure_version` and the ELF SHA-256 prefix from the app descriptor (`parse_app_descriptor()` in `desktop/flash-engine/src/health.rs`).
- **Both flashers.**
  - They parse the bootloader header's flash mode, speed and size.
  - They decode the per-chip eFuse **field** allowlist (§5.6). Each word is reduced to its allowed fields as it is read, so no BLOCK1 or BLOCK2 word is stored, serialized, shown or saved, and "Factory calibration" comes from the block version only.
- **Privacy.**
  - No MAC bytes on screen by default ("Hardware address · Show", §5.6), with an explicit reveal, never copied. That covers the browser's hello card and Board report (`macRevealFact()` in `flash.js`), the desktop's This Board card and health card (`macRevealRow()` in `app.js`), its connect line and fleet list, and both nursery strips.
  - No MAC bytes go into any saved file or shared report. The ROM tools print the address unasked, so every console that draws their output, and every error the flash engine builds from espflash's output, masks a MAC-shaped token (`maskHardwareAddress()`).
  - "Save report" writes the share projection (`reportShareProjection()`: no counter, posture, chain-head prefix or instant); "Save full report for your own records" writes the rest (`reportFullProjection()`), with a note that it describes this one board (P24).
  - Reports and backups are named by the local date and a counter (`fileStamp()`; `rescue::backup_filename(day, n)` on the desktop). `macStamp()` is gone.
  - The `settings_help.mac` copy is corrected.
  - Both `sanitizeLogTail()`s replace an identity line with a visible marker, the boot banner's Hardware ID line and the self-manifest line among them (P14).
  - Neither flasher sends `ID?` to the Grove module or shows its model text. A model is named only after a write whose SHA-256 matched the pinned release (P13).

**Where it differs from the plan:**

- The identity lines are a list of their own, `TAIL_IDENTITY`, beside `TAIL_REDACT`, which stays the credential list. The plan had `TAIL_REDACT` gain the hardware-id line. `TAIL_IDENTITY` names many more lines than the plan's two: device ids, key fingerprints, `.local` names, setup-network names and peer names among them (#1970's CHANGELOG entry lists them).
- The radios, cores and crystal are a third group, "From the maker's datasheet". The plan had the crystal and features read from the chip; #1970 found that for the S3, C3 and C6 both ROM tools print fixed strings there (§5.6).
- The card's groups stack in one column with one "Copy these details" button, and the desktop's connect line names the chip, its revision and its flash. The plan drew a sidebar, a copy per row, and the PSRAM size and bus mode on that line. §6.9 describes the card as built.
- It fixed two things the plan did not name: the browser's Grove proof, which `btoa()` stopped on the model card's em dash, and the desktop serial monitor, which now decodes the board's bytes as one UTF-8 stream.
- The desktop card sets every value in monospace (`.this-board .tb-v` in `desktop/src/styles.css`), where §6.2 keeps monospace for identifiers; the browser's values are plain, with only the revealed address in monospace. Whether the desktop card follows §6.2 or the rule makes room for it is the maintainer's to decide (§6.9, §14).

**Not shipped, and where it went:**

- The diagnostic report's log tail still passes any serial line no pattern names, so it is not public-only by construction: ledger A132. Replacing each details line with its share projection is PR 10's.
- The desktop's fleet book and stored device keys are still keyed by the hardware address: PR 18 (ledger A133).
- A copy action per row (§6.2) is not built. No later PR in this plan and no ledger item holds it; whether one takes it or §6.2 drops it is the maintainer's to decide (§14).

**Gates it added:**

- `desktop_parity.test.js` pins:
  - the rows, words and order, by running both flashers' row models over the same fact sets;
  - the per-chip eFuse field tables (block, word, bit, width) between `intake.js` and `efuse.rs`;
  - the no-MAC-by-default rule, the no-MAC-in-files rule, the saved-report projections, and `TAIL_IDENTITY` on both sides, beside the `TAIL_REDACT` pin that was already there;
  - `flash-native.js`'s About-this-board rows beside `app.js`'s.
- `cargo test` (`desktop-hub-core.yml`, for `flash-engine` and for `src-tauri`): per chip, a word with every masked trim bit set decodes like one with them clear (`efuse.rs`); the bootloader-header and app-descriptor parse tests; `backup_filename` takes no MAC.
- `flash.test.js` renders the banner and manifest lines from the firmware's own sources, and checks that copied, saved and shared reports and file names carry no MAC bytes, no hardware id and no manifest line. `intake.test.mjs` holds the browser's trim-flip decode.
- `gen_flash.py --check`.

### PR 2: Home Assistant shows every Canary's firmware, and the redaction set is ready

**Landed as #1891** (commit `2b500be4`). Integration only. Host-tested only. What shipped:

- The device registry takes `sw_version` from the health topic's `firmware_version`. Until a health has reported one it takes what the status topic carries: `firmware_version`, `fw_version`, or the flagship's `firmware`.
- The model comes from the status topic's `device_type` through a product-name table (`CANARY_PRODUCT_NAMES` in `const.py`). A type that is absent or not in the table reads "SecuraCV Canary".
- Every value is a `str`, and broker text is cut to 64 printable characters (`mqtt_payload.broker_text`). A fact a publish leaves out no longer clears the one the registry stored.
- `TO_REDACT` gained the eight keys in P18, **named literally**.
- The config-entry dump keeps no fingerprint digits and gives each pinned Canary a per-dump salted `key_correlation_id` in their place (P19, decided in §14).

**Where it differs from the plan:**

- Beyond the plan, every Canary with a device page has a row of its own in the dump, and the security docs say what Home Assistant itself adds to a downloaded diagnostics file.
- The plan left the derived set to PR 3. #1961 built it: `TO_REDACT` is `INTEGRATION_REDACT_KEYS` plus the generated `REDACTED_DEVICE_KEYS` (`custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py`, §2.3). Six of the eight keys are vocabulary rows now.

**Not shipped, and where it went:**

- Nothing of PR 2's own scope is left.
- `serial_number` and `chip_id`, the other two of the eight, stay literals in `diagnostics.py`'s `INTEGRATION_REDACT_KEYS`: no firmware source emits them, and the vocabulary holds only keys a source emits (ledger HA62).
- The device page's Hardware and Model ID come with the details topic, as planned: PR 16.

**Gates it added:** `test_device_registry_versions.py` (new in #1891), and in `test_diagnostics.py`, `test_no_digit_of_a_canary_fingerprint_reaches_the_dump` (new) and `test_the_redaction_set_names_every_identifier_class`, which now names the eight keys. Their fixtures live under `custom_components/securacv/tests/`, so the HACS mirror's CI runs these tests with no new deselect; the checks that also read the firmware or the Apple apps skip there. #1891 also added real-core tests to `tests/ha/test_diagnostics.py` and `tests/ha/test_entities.py`, among them `test_every_canary_with_a_device_page_has_a_row`, `test_an_identifier_key_is_replaced_wherever_it_lands` and `test_canary_device_page_takes_its_firmware_from_health`. They run in `python.yml`'s real Home Assistant job only, since the mirror carries no `tests/ha/`. Both of `python.yml`'s path lists gained the firmware and Apple sources `test_device_registry_versions.py` reads, and other root files the integration suite reads. The mirror receives the integration's change by sync only (ledger U6).

### PR 3: The device-details vocabulary, and the generated reference

**One slice is built: #1961** (commit `32fd161d`, sweep HA56), the answer keys a diagnostics dump redacts. At `62bc0bc7`, as at `0a06c14b`:

- `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json` holds `vocabulary_version` 1, two classes (`identifier` and `health_counter`, both redacted) and eleven `keys` rows, each naming the firmware sources that emit its key (§2.2). This file is the block (§2.1).
- `scripts/gen_device_vocabulary.py` is the generator. It writes one file, `custom_components/securacv/device_vocabulary.py`. It refuses a row whose named source does not emit its key, and it will not write a module that stops redacting a key the committed one redacts. `lint.yml` runs its `--check`, and `scripts/tests/test_gen_device_vocabulary.py` pins it.
- **Home Assistant.** `diagnostics.py` unions the module's `REDACTED_DEVICE_KEYS` into `TO_REDACT`, so the redaction set is derived (P18). `test_diagnostics.py` holds it (`test_the_redaction_set_is_the_integration_s_keys_and_the_vocabulary_s`).
- **The order.** `docs/GENERATED_FILES.md` and `.claude/rules/generated.md` carry the one-link chain (§2.4), and `spec/README.md` lists the file.

**Where it differs from the plan, and what that leaves open:**

- The vocabulary is a file of its own. This design first put a `device_details` block in `spec/witness_dictionary.json`; the plan now grows the file (§2.1).
- The generator and the module are `gen_device_vocabulary.py` and `device_vocabulary.py`. This plan had called them `gen_device_details.py` and `device_details_vocab.py`; it uses main's names from here on.
- Its rows are wire keys in two classes named for what a dump redacts. This plan has fact rows in §3.1's ten classes. How the two meet is the maintainer's to decide before PR 3 writes a row: the row shape and row state (ledger HA62), and the classes (§2.2). Ledger HA63, six uptime keys in no redacted class, waits on the class decision.
- Two of P18's eight keys, `serial_number` and `chip_id`, are not rows, because no firmware source emits them. They stay literals in `diagnostics.py`'s `INTEGRATION_REDACT_KEYS` (HA62).

**Still to build.** Everything below. No file it marks new exists at `62bc0bc7`.

**Visible:** `docs/device_details_reference.md` **(new)**, "What a Canary can tell you, and who can see it", generated per family.

- **The block** (§2.2), grown in the same file beside `keys` and seeded with:
  - the facts that exist today and their `legacy` mappings, including the `known_wrong` values;
  - the part sheets with statuses earned on the full ladder, per board, against `firmware/scripts/shipped_configs.py` (**new here**, §7.1; a planted unmappable product must fail its test);
  - the `disclosures` and `never` lists, with the eFuse trim and calibration field descriptors named, and each `never` row's `except` entries (§2.2);
  - the `gaps` list (§2.2), opened with the flagship's figure: the board token (`hw`) stays omitted on the flagship until `figures.json` maps a flagship figure, and PR 6 (#1971) did not invent one (decided, §14; ledger F398 records it). The entry closes in the PR that adds that figure.
- **The generator** (§2.3). `gen_device_vocabulary.py` gains only the outputs that have a reader now: the Swift vocabulary, what the Python module still lacks, the reference doc and the HA vectors (`details_vectors.json`, new). The flashers' help text is `gen_flash.py`'s, read from the block (below). The key table, the golden fixture and its HA copy join in PR 9; the JSON vocabulary (`canary-local/devices/device_details.json`) in PR 10, where the flashers, the Lab's USB bench and the website's serial page first read it; the page regions in PRs 11, 12 and 14; and the glass label header in PR 17. So the generator writes nothing under `firmware/` in this PR, and no gzip, sketch-sync or dist gate is touched.
- **The lint** (§10 item 2): `scripts/lint_device_details.py` **(new)**.
- **Help text.** `gen_flash.py` takes the flashers' help topics, PR 1's among them, from the block, and `flash.json`'s bytes do not change.
- **Docs.**
  - This document and its `docs/README.md` entry landed in a PR of their own, after PRs 1, 2, 6 and 7 and #1961; PR 3 brings both up to date with what it builds.
  - GLOSSARY gains "Details record" and "Device details vocabulary", which differ from Self-manifest and Device manifest.
  - An AGENTS.md "Where do I look for X?" line (so `gen_agent_entrypoints.py`).
  - The generated-files order gains each new link, in `docs/GENERATED_FILES.md` and `.claude/rules/generated.md`, which hold it now (§2.4).
- **tvOS.** `tvos/WitnessWall/project.yml` lists the Swift vocabulary.
- **CI.** `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json` joins both path lists of `canary-local.yml`, whose "Flash catalog drift" step runs `gen_flash.py`.

**Gates:** `gen_device_vocabulary.py --check` and `scripts/tests/test_gen_device_vocabulary.py` (both exist), `lint_device_details.py` (new), `lint_apple_parity.py`, `lint_docs_index.py`, `lint_md_links.py`, `lint_glossary_status.py`, `lint_spelling.py`, `lint_fleet_word.py`, `gen_agent_entrypoints.py --check`, `gen_flash.py --check`, `scripts/check_path_filter_reads.py`, `.github/scripts/ci_policy_check.py`, the HA pytest suite. `lint_dictionary_sync.py` is no longer one of them: it holds `spec/witness_dictionary.json` and its copies to each other, and never reads `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json`, the only file under `spec/` that PR 3 edits.

### PR 4: The identifier guard

**What it adds.** `scripts/lint_identifier_reads.py` **(new)** (§10 item 3), whose deny-list comes from the block's `never` rows, and its self-test fixtures. One case of it exists: `desktop_parity.test.js`'s `sscmaIdCalls()` (F384, #1916), which fails on any call of a method named `ID` in Vision's sources. The lint folds it in. Ledger F430 holds the lint, with the rest of finding P15 (§11): its scope is §2.2's `never` rows with §10 item 3.

**Fixed in the same PR:**

- `USB_SERIAL` in both opt-in USB envs (P2).
- The WAP's DIS serial becomes empty: never the fingerprint, which `canary_wap.ino` passes to `bluetooth_channel::set_device_metadata()` today, and never the Bluetooth address, its fallback (P17). No app in this tree reads it.
- THREAT_MODEL Principle 3 is rewritten to say, per chip, what is on air: the station address is the factory address in every Wi-Fi frame; Bluetooth is base + 1 on the S3 and base + 2 on the C3 and C6; the setup BSSID is the factory address with its locally administered bit set on the S3 (so not Espressif's OUI) and base + 1 on the C3 and C6. The Bluetooth and BSSID lines are permanent per-unit identifiers until each family's shipped configurations compile PR 20's policy on (P1, P3). It gains the USB-descriptor rule (P2).

`KNOWN_LEAK` **(new, in the lint)** lists every remaining leak with its finding and families, and its rows become list 6's exception lines (§7.2). PR 3's `lint_device_details.py` gains both of its `KNOWN_LEAK` readers in this PR: the address-valued answer-key rule and list 6's exception lines (§10 item 2). The `firmware/**` grep also reaches a bench sketch no release builds, `firmware/projects/canary-tincan/arduino/tincan_bringup/tincan_bringup.ino`, which prints the last two bytes of `WiFi.macAddress()` over USB and on its screen; this PR gives it an allowlist or `KNOWN_LEAK` row, or excludes bench sketches by a stated rule (§10 item 3).

**Gates:** the lint's planted self-tests, `firmware.yml` builds of `canary-wap-usbdrive` and `usb-onboard`, the WAP arduino-cli builds, `regression_check.sh`, `lint_md_links.py`, PR 3's `lint_device_details.py` (the exception lines).

### PR 5: iPhone and iPad, About This Canary from what devices already say

**Visible.** §6.5 and §6.6, rendered only through the generated vocabulary. It was ordered after PR 6, which has landed (#1971) and is released in fw-v2.4.20, so firmware built from main and fw-v2.4.20 no longer send most of §1.4's wrong values. Its legacy adapters render every `known_wrong` value as "cannot say": the ones §1.4 lists as still wrong (ledger F398), and all of them from firmware older than #1971, fw-v2.4.19 and every earlier release among it.

- **Sources.** The legacy adapters, with contract belts.
- **Fixes in the same PR.**
  - the BLE console decoder, which adds nothing to a paired device's Details until ledger A65 is settled and the console proves identity with a nonce signed by the pinned key (§6.5);
  - TXT `fw` carried to fleet rows, where no row takes it today (§6.5);
  - the WAP Wi-Fi row;
  - Improv `deviceInfo` in setup;
  - the Keychain reads moved off the render path: `try? store.devices.api(for: ref)` sits in `body` in `ios/Sources/SecuraCV/Views/DeviceDetailView.swift` in two places, where it mounts `SensingNowSection` and where it mounts a paired WAP's `FirmwareUpdateSection` (§6.5).
- **Identity.** The fingerprint collapsed to "Key fingerprint · Show", with no digits and no randomart until the reveal, and its own Copy Fingerprint button. Copy Details carries no handle. Whether the two rows that show the whole fingerprint today, the Birth Certificate card's and the Keys tab's, collapse too is open (§6.5).
- **Watch.** It gains the optional `WristWitness.firmware` **(new)** and a Firmware row.

**Gates:**

- `ios/Tests/SecuraCVTests/DeviceDetailsTests.swift` **(new)**:
  - vectors read from the block by `#filePath`;
  - a share projection that omits counters, posture, activity, handles and unknown keys;
  - the provenance wording;
  - contract belts for `sys_monitor.h get_json`, `glass_web.cpp handle_device` and `node_status.h`.
- `lint_ios_keychain_reads.py`, **extended** to instance methods that reach a reader, with a planted case in `scripts/tests/test_lint_ios_keychain_reads.py`. Unextended, it passes this tree with the violation present.
- The source-path widget rule of PR 3's `lint_device_details.py` (§6.5, §10 item 2): its row-property refusals (a row's `firmware` in the two glance files and the watch long-look, its `hardware` in the first two), a planted widget file and a planted Shared glance view.
- `lint_apple_parity.py`, `lint_apple_plists.py`, `lint_cloudkit_container.py`.
- The WristSnapshot tests: an additive optional, with no schema bump.
- The iOS, watch and tvOS builds.
- `ios-selfheal.yml`'s `pull_request` list gains the producers the contract belts read, and `spec/device_details_vocabulary.json`, which the vectors are read from (§10 item 11). It lists `spec/witness_dictionary.json` today and not that file, and it has no `push` list.

### PR 6: Honest names

**Landed as #1971** (commit `0a06c14b`). Host-tested; not run on a device. #1971 made no `FIRMWARE_VERSION` bump. The 2.4.20 train (#1972) bumped every family, and fw-v2.4.20, tagged at `62bc0bc7`, is the first release that carries #1971 (the Sentinel has no release). These are the §1.4 values, fixed at their source; §1.4 says what each one reads now. What shipped:

- **Flagship.**
  - The mDNS `model` is the board's name (`CANARY_BOARD_NAME` in `canary_config.h`), and `mdns_txt.h` omits `model` when a build names no board.
  - Home Assistant discovery's `mdl` is the product (`CANARY_PRODUCT_NAME`), and its `hw` is the board.
  - The web UI's `2.0.1` fallback is `--`.
  - The watchdog self-test row has no probe function and reads "not a probe", and the score counts the nine real probes.
- **WAP.**
  - The chip fallback is empty: the serial monitor prints "not reported" and `/admin` shows `--`.
  - The banner, the serial monitor, the Improv hardware string and the mDNS adverts' `model` read `HARDWARE_BOARD_NAME` and `HARDWARE_CHIP_NAME`, which `build_config.h` sets per target.
  - `web_ui.h`'s firmware placeholders and the footer's version are gone. `/admin`'s gzip went from 67,528 B to 67,494 B (the `B raw ->` comment in `web_assets_gz.h`, at `7185559c` and at `0a06c14b`).
  - The S3 target defines its board token, `CANARY_FIGURE_HARDWARE "board:seeed_xiao_esp32s3"`. The C3 target defines none.
- **Display mirror page.** The Kind comes from the device type. `mirror_html.h` is not emulator-compiled, so the dist did not move.
- **Trio.**
  - The three missing board tokens are defined, and each trio project's `mdns_mgr.cpp` and `ota_mgr.cpp` now include `pins.h`, so the token reaches the mDNS TXT and the OTA engine. The OTA binding is inert under version-1 manifests (§5.5).
  - Health sends no `battery` value when there is none, and Home Assistant shows such a battery as unknown.

**Where it differs from the plan:**

- **The `/admin` budget gate was not built.** The plan had `gen_web_assets_gz.py --check` print each asset's gzip size and headroom, and fail when `/admin`'s gzip grows past a budget the script records. `--check` still only byte-compares the generated header. The gate is still owed (§5.3, ledger F479), and which PR adds it is open (§14). PR 8, the next PR in this plan to edit `web_ui.h`, was to land behind it.
- **The page is past the ceiling.** The plan gave `/admin` as 64,938 B at `377bbc6f` and 65,348 B at `e4d07edb`. It was 67,494 B at `0a06c14b`, and it is 67,638 B at `62bc0bc7`, 2,102 B over the 65,536 B ceiling (§5.3), after #1919, #1920, #1951 and the 2.4.20 train (#1972) grew it.
- **The mirror page's signal is a word, not "dBm".** It reads "strong signal", "ok signal" or "weak signal" (`sigWord()` in `mirror_html.h`, in `theme.cpp` `signal_word()`'s bands), because `microcopy_lint.sh`'s glass plain-words audit keeps "dBm" out of that page.
- **Beyond the plan.** `check_board_registry.py` gained checks 10 and 11 and moved to `lint.yml`; `/api/selftest` and `/api/diagnostics` spell the watchdog row with `"probe":false`; the battery blueprint and the Lab's Sense and Vision panes follow the health change.

**Not shipped, and where it went** (ledger F398 holds all but the last, which F479 holds):

- The display's health still sends `"battery":100` beside `"battery_present":false`, and the Touch 1.69's boot banner still describes another panel. Both sources are emulator-compiled: PR 17.
- Ten display envs, built on the 4.3B and 4.3C boards, define no board token.
- A WAP on mains power still sends `"battery":100` beside `"battery_present":false`. Whether to drop it is a decision F398 holds.
- The flagship defines no board token until a flagship figure exists (decided, §14).
- The website's checkup copy counted ten probes when the ledger item read it (the website's `main` at `c7efe8b`); it was not read again for this landing.
- The `/admin` budget gate, above (ledger F479, filed when this document landed).

**Gates it added or runs:** `firmware/scripts/check_board_registry.py` checks 10 and 11 (`lint.yml`), `firmware/tests_host/test_honest_names.test.js`, `test_canary_diagnostics.cpp`, `test_console_scene.cpp`, `test_mdns_txt.cpp`, `test_node_status.cpp` and `test_fleet_selfreport.cpp` with `hw` present, `custom_components/securacv/tests/test_absent_battery.py`, `gen_web_assets_gz.py --check` (bytes only), `setup.sh regen` then `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`, and `check_sentinel_net_sync.sh`.

### PR 7: Vision and Sentinel tell the truth about their modules

**Landed as #1892** (commit `89cf5608`; finding P11, sweep F384). Host-tested only; nothing ran on a device. No `FIRMWARE_VERSION` bump, and the emulator dist did not move. What shipped:

- **Vision.**
  - `module_id` is 1 or 0, from the library's `begin()` and then a fresh `name(false)` round trip (`init()` in `vision_mgr.cpp`), which keeps old Flashers' `> 0` gate.
  - The boot line keeps its exact format, `Grove Vision AI ID=%d` (`vision_mgr.cpp`). `gen_vision.py` `must()`s that string and `monitor.rs` parses it, so only its value changed.
  - The `j` proof record gained `module_ok`.
  - `invoke()` is compared with `CMD_OK`: a successful invoke always gives a sample, and a failed one gives none.
- **Desktop.** `monitor.rs`'s receipt and its live-boot-line path, and the `serial:vision` handler in `desktop/src/app.js`, read `module_ok` when a proof carries it. **No browser flasher change:** it proves a Vision through the camera bench, never the boot log, and `desktop_parity.test.js` holds it to having no such path. `canary-local/assets/vision.js`, the Lab's Vision page, reads neither field; #1892 changed one sentence of its copy about the ID line.
- **Sentinel.**
  - Both health publishes carry the `radar` object it already computed (`radar_link_health()`, by the rules in `include/canary/sentinel_links.h`), and Home Assistant gives a Canary the Radar link sensor when its health reports one.
  - The BH1750 `begin()` result decides "absent" from "blinded". A sensor that did not answer at boot never votes, and the state's `channel_denied` still reports it.

**Where it differs from the plan:**

- **The library still asks for the id once, at boot.** The plan said `ID?` is never sent. No code of the firmware's own asks for it, and `desktop_parity.test.js`'s `sscmaIdCalls()` (#1916) holds that. But Seeed_Arduino_SSCMA 1.0.3's I2C `begin()` ends `return ID(false) && name(false);` (the pinned library's `Seeed_Arduino_SSCMA.cpp`), so the library asks once at boot and keeps the answer in a private buffer, which no firmware code reads, prints or stores. Whether to accept that one read or replace `begin()` is open, and the maintainer's to decide (§14).
- **The radar object's frame age and error count ride the broker.** They are `activity` (§3.1), which the policy keeps off the broker tier. They ride the owner's broker as a legacy exception, the health object canary-sense already publishes (the note on ledger F22).

**Not shipped, and where it went:**

- The general identifier lint: PR 4 (ledger F430).
- A sensor unplugged while the unit is off boots as absent, so on the wire only the anomaly score tells it from a blinded one, until the record carries the part's status: PR 15.
- `last_frame_age_ms` in the diagnostics redaction was left for PR 16. #1916 has since added it to the redaction set, and it is a redacted `health_counter` row of #1961's vocabulary now (§2.2).

**Gates it added:** `firmware/tests_host/test_vision_module_link.cpp` and `test_sentinel_links.cpp` (55 checks each, run with g++ on this tree on 2026-11-16), six `monitor.rs` tests (old and new proof records; a dead module is refused), a `desktop_parity.test.js` case, and two tests in the HA `test_modality_and_radar.py`. `gen_vision.py` has no `--check`: `canary-local.yml`'s "Vision data drift" step runs it and diffs `canary-local/devices/vision.json`. The page logic tests' "Vision generated data is stale against its firmware core" is a different gate, which `canary-local/emulator/web/vision-core.js` throws when `vision.test.js` loads the core. `firmware/flavors.json` guards five images of the two projects: `canary-vision-default`, `canary-vision-xiao-c3` and `canary-sentinel-door` at 0x1E0000, `canary-vision-xiao-s3` at 0x330000, and `canary-sentinel-lite` at 0x140000.

### PR 8: WAP, identifiers, posture and room readings off LAN-trust and open answers

This covers findings P4 to P9, P20 and P21 on the WAP. For P20 it serves the session's recorded reads on the bearer branch, since the owner-authority PR (#1887) moved none of them (§3.2), and takes a raise of the Wi-Fi sensing's privacy ceiling to the bearer; it also takes the name fields (the device id and the mesh's name, spelled out) and the other fields no receiver reads out of the mesh's pairing frames, and sends nothing, a pairing included, from a mesh the owner turned off. It edits `web_ui.h`, so it needs the `/admin` budget gate PR 6 was to add and #1971 did not build (§5.3, ledger F479): it lands behind that gate or carries it, and which is open (§14).

- **The audience seam** (§3.5): `api_auth_audience()` **(new, in `api_auth.h`)** and its pure decision `audience_decide()` **(new, in `auth_logic.h`)**, with new cases in `test_auth_logic.cpp`. The tiered legacy routes below call it once per request, so a wrong bearer gets the seam's 403 and is never served at the lan tier on a cookie (§3.5 says what the two-call shortcut does instead). A `Sealed` header the WAP cannot open gets today's 401 with its `seal` word, with no strike for `no_session` or `expired`, which the desktop Flasher's sealed `GET /api/status` depends on: `audience_decide()` takes the seal word as an input (§3.5). On `/api/wifi` and `/api/wifi/scan` the seam's third outcome is the route's open answer, not 401 (§3.5 step 3). Their Host behavior is unchanged (F344's).
- **`firmware/common/time/coarse_uptime.h`** and `test_coarse_uptime.cpp` (both new, §4.1), with the WAP's staged copy and the first `firmware/scripts/check_details_sync.sh` **(new)**.
- **`/api/device-info`** drops `hw_token`, `uptime_ms`, `ap_auth` and `chain_length`; no page reads any of them. `tls_enabled` stays, recorded (§3.6).
- **`/api/system`** drops `hw_token`; its `reset_reason`, heap `min_free` and `largest_block`, PSRAM `min_free` and uptime in seconds reach only the bearer. Its `#sysMac` and Reset rows go from `/admin` (the Reset row returns in Protection, which PR 12 adds once `/admin` has room for it, §5.3).
- **`/api/status`**: `last_reset`, `last_reset_crash` and `min_heap` reach only the bearer, and so do P20's `uptime_sec`, `boot_count`, `chain_seq`, `witness_count` and `peek_active`, and `node_status`'s `rssi`: the WAP builds `node_status` for a session with `rssi` 0, which `node_status.h` omits (PR 11 then drops the key for every family). Its `csi` counters and `node_status`'s `uptime_s` stay for a session (§3.2), and so does `node_status`'s `creds_at_rest` while every shipped build reports `plaintext-nvs` (P16, §14). The strip's `status_tier` and `status_reason` stay for a session, evaluated without the crash flag and the heap floor, so they never say `restarted` or `low_memory`; the bearer's are evaluated whole (P20, `test_status_tier_logic.cpp`). No page that holds the bearer reads `status_reason` today, so after this PR no page draws the two reasons; whether a signed-in page should, before the Protection panel reaches `/admin`, is open (§3.2). `safe_mode` stays for a session, and so does the strip's `safe_mode` reason, since `/api/selftest` and `/api/help-qr` give safe mode to any LAN host (§3.2); whether it moves is §14's question. The administrator page's Status rows that read the moved counts (`refreshStatus()` in `web_ui.h`) show the WAP's held phrase in the boot count's row to a page without the token, and leave out the uptime, witness count and chain sequence rows there: a session reads those in other answers (§3.2), and the phrase is only for what it cannot read anywhere (§3.4). Their bytes count against §14's rule as the Bluetooth panel's do.
- **`/api/diagnostics`**: the heap low-water and largest block, the SD card's lifetime writes and the event-egress counters reach only the bearer over HTTP (`wap_diagnostics.h` gains the tier; its `kJsonMax` only shrinks). A session that points the MQTT bridge at a broker of its own still reads the egress counters there (`publish_egress()`, §3.6); whether this PR moves a broker change to the bearer is open (§14). Its `uptime_sec` stays for a session, which reads the uptime in other answers too (§3.2).
- **`/api/mesh` and `/api/mesh/peers`** (FULL builds): the mesh's id, name, uptime and message, alert and auth-failure counters, and every peer row reach only the bearer (P20). The pairing's state and `pairing_code` stay for a session (F346). The pages that read the moved fields show the held phrase to a page without the token.
- **`/api/wifi` and `/api/wifi/scan`** send no CORS header. They give the full answer on the SoftAP interface (the provisioning gate's `request_on_softap`) or to a session, and words elsewhere; `ap_auth`, `ap_auth_reason` and `sta_pmf` only on the SoftAP interface or to the bearer, and `/api/wifi`'s `rssi` and each scanned network's too, with P20's reads (§3.2), so a session's scan rows carry each network's name, channel and security, and come in name order: today they come in the core's order, which the code says is by signal (the comment on `wifi_scan_cache_fill()` in `canary_wap.ino`: "it sorts by RSSI"), and a rank is the signal cut at thresholds (§3.1). Which networks the list keeps when a sweep hears more than 20 (`SCAN_CACHE_MAX`) is still set by signal, as the core reports them. The administrator page's Wi-Fi bars (`loadWifiStatus()` in `web_ui.h`) show the held phrase to a page without the token, and its network list (`scanWifi()`, which sorts and labels the rows by `rssi` today) lists the networks without a signal; the companion's list (`renderNets()` in `companion_pwa.h`) already shows none where `rssi` is absent, and its sort by `rssi` (`pollScan()`) is stable, so it keeps the name order.
- **`/api/selftest`** gives full metrics only on the SoftAP interface, and never the microphone level elsewhere, so the companion's "Why it went into recovery" text (`last_crash_reason`, `last_crash_stage`) then shows only there (§3.2). One question is open, and the maintainer's to decide: each probe row also carries a `code` and a `detail` text. The Wi-Fi row's `code` is the signal in dBm (`probe_wifi()`: `r->code = rssi`) and its `detail` repeats the name and the signal, the battery row's `code` is the charge percent (`probe_power()`), and the Bluetooth row's `detail`, after a start refused for low memory, gives the largest free block (`probe_bluetooth()` in `selftest_api.h`, §3.2). The companion reads `code` (`companion_pwa.h`). Whether a row's `code` and `detail` go off the other interfaces with its metrics is not decided.
- **`POST /api/settings`**: a `privacy_ceiling` above the one in force is taken only on the seam's bearer branch. A session's is refused with 403, and nothing in its body is stored; a session may still lower the ceiling or send the one in force. Today any session raises it, which opens `/api/csi/window` at P2 and lets the breathing module's P1 event, with its rate, through at P1 (§3.2). A raise is stored and applied again at every boot, and an update keeps it, so this PR's first boot also lowers a stored ceiling above P0 to P0, once (`retire_unowned_privacy_ceiling_once()`, new in `csi_settings_nvs.cpp`, called by `csi_integration::init()` just before `apply_privacy_ceiling_from_nvs()`): nothing tells a raise the owner made from one any LAN host made, and a marker row beside `csi.ff`, written only once the stored ceiling is P0, keeps every later boot from lowering the owner's own raise. `/api/csi/window` keeps its P2 gate and stays on the session, recorded (§3.6): from this PR a session reads the raw window, the signal in it included, only while the owner holds the ceiling at P2. The breathing rate is held less tightly: a session reads it in `/api/events/today` only while the owner holds the ceiling at P1 or above, and on `/api/csi/stream` until the first event after the ceiling comes down, because the stream keeps its last snapshot. Whether this PR also clears that snapshot when the ceiling is lowered is open (§3.2). No page writes the ceiling today, so the lowering costs only an owner who raised it by hand, who raises it again with the token. `filter_foreign` stays with the session (§3.2).
- **The BLE status service's** uptime goes to whole hours through `coarse_uptime.h` (P9).
- **The opera frames** (P9; FULL builds):
  - `send_to_peer()` writes 0 in the header's `timestamp`, and `send_heartbeat()` clears its payload before setting it, so `uptime_sec` and the five padding bytes the struct leaves unset (`HeartbeatPayload` in `mesh_network.h`) go out as 0. No receiver reads either clock: the receive path discards the timestamp (`handle_received_message()` in `mesh_network.cpp`, "intentionally unused as of v0.2"; the spec's receivers "MUST NOT reject solely on timestamp", `spec/canary_mesh_network_v0.md` §3.3), and `handle_heartbeat()` reads no field ("the fields are not read yet").
  - `load_tx_reservations()` draws one skip below 2^20 each boot with `esp_fill_random()` and adds it, saturating, to the highest reservation it reads, so every member resumes one past that (its last loop sets `high + 1`), and a member added later starts past it too (`add_peer()`, from `g_tx_high_signed`), on a device with nothing stored included. Without it a boot resumes on the 1,024-step grid of its reservations (`TX_COUNTER_RESERVE_BLOCK`, in `reserve_tx_counter()`), and the counter, written in the clear in every signed frame's header (`send_to_peer()`), counts the frames since the boot or the pairing. One skip for every member leaves F72's gap as it is, the counter stays above everything signed (F71), and the first send reserves through `reserve_tx_counter()` as now. The skip spends less than 2^20 counters a boot, and PR 8's comment on `TX_COUNTER_FLOOR_UNREADABLE`, which says the counters stay far below 2^48, counts it: 2^27 boots that send add less than 2^47.
  - The pairing frames (P20): the DISCOVER (built in `update()`, under "Pairing discovery broadcasts"), the OFFER (built in `handle_pair_discover()`) and the ACCEPT (built in `handle_pair_offer()`) are each cleared before they are set, and each carries only what its receiver reads. `handle_pair_discover()` reads a joiner's key and role, `handle_pair_offer()` the OFFER's two keys, and `handle_pair_accept()` the ACCEPT's ephemeral key. No canary-wap receiver reads a name (the initiator holds its new member as "New Device", in `initiator_complete()`), the OFFER's member count, the ACCEPT's device key (the initiator holds the joiner's from its DISCOVER, in `handle_pair_discover()`) or the initiator's DISCOVER (a joiner acts on none: `handle_pair_discover()` answers only as the initiator). So none carries a name or a count, the ACCEPT carries no device key, and only the joiner broadcasts a DISCOVER. No canary-wap firmware reads any of them, and the PlatformIO tree, whose joiner reads the OFFER's name, cannot pair with canary-wap yet (below), so the change needs no wire version. Without it every DISCOVER, the initiator's included, spells out the device id to every radio in range every 2 s, the OFFER gives the mesh's name and member count to any radio that asks, and each carries the last byte of its name field, which `strncpy()` never writes. The device id itself stays: it is a fixed board prefix (`DEVICE_ID_PREFIX`, `canary-s3-` or `canary-c3-`) and four characters computed from the first two bytes of the key fingerprint (`generate_device_id()` in `canary_wap.ino`), which anyone computes from the public key the joiner's DISCOVER and the OFFER keep, or from the fingerprint in the header of every signed frame. The keys the pairing reads stay with §14's question, and so does the OFFER the initiator sends to every radio that asks.
  - A mesh turned off stays off (P20). `send_to_peer()` refuses every signed frame while the setting is off, so the Scout's events, the hub election and the channel lock, whose senders ask only whether an opera is held and which the loop sends whatever the setting, stop with the heartbeat, and so does OFFLINE_IMMINENT, whose sender asks the same and which nothing calls yet; `start_pairing_initiator()` and `start_pairing_joiner()` refuse while the WAP holds an opera with the mesh off, as the PlatformIO tree's `pair/start` and `pair/join` answer `mesh_disabled` (spec §8.3); and `cancel_pairing()` and `fail_pairing()` leave `MESH_DISABLED` whenever the setting is off, as `init()` does, so `update()` runs neither the heartbeat nor the receive path. Without it those three go to every stored member with the mesh off, and any session's pairing start runs on it and, when it ends, leaves the heartbeats running until the next reboot, the setting still off (§7.2 list 1). A WAP with no opera is off until its first pairing (`NVS_ENABLED` defaults to false, in `load_opera_config()`) and still starts one, which turns the mesh on as now (the initiator at the start, in `start_pairing_initiator()`; the joiner when it completes, in `handle_pair_complete()`): refusing every start while the setting is off, as the PlatformIO tree does, failed 33 of the liveness suite's 78 tests and 4 of the command suite's 18 in the prototype on `e4d07edb` (that tree's 73 liveness tests and PR 8's five), each on a device with no opera that could no longer pair. The route answers a refused start with its `pairing_failed`, as now; a word of its own, such as the PlatformIO tree's `mesh_disabled`, is the PR's to add.
  - The PlatformIO tree's pairing frames are its own, and this PR leaves them as they are: its initiator broadcasts one DISCOVER whose name field carries the opera's name, its OFFER carries the name too, and its joiner adopts it, saves it and serves it on `GET /api/mesh` (`start_initiator()`, `initiator_handle_discover()` and `joiner_handle_offer()` in `firmware/canary/lib/securacv_mesh/src/mesh_pairing.cpp`; `dispatch_action()` and `build_status_view()` in `mesh_session.cpp`; `on_pairing_succeeded()` in `firmware/canary/src/main.cpp`). Its mesh is in `[env:full]` alone (`-DFEATURE_MESH_NETWORK=1` in `firmware/canary/platformio.ini`), which CI builds and no release ships (`firmware-release.yml` builds `release`, `release_ha` and three boards that extend `release`), and the two trees cannot pair with each other yet (spec §4.5's registry). When they can, a WAP OFFER with no name leaves a PlatformIO joiner with an empty opera name (host build: canary-wap's OFFER fed to that tree's joiner gave it "Smith house", and the same OFFER with its name cleared gave it ""), so the PR that lets them pair decides how such a joiner learns the name.
  - The spec's frame (§4.1, §4.5) and HEARTBEAT (§4.2) say the zeros, its replay section (§3.3) the skip, its pairing flow (§5.2), in its canary-wap paragraph, what canary-wap's pairing frames carry and that only the joiner broadcasts a DISCOVER, and its `enable` notes (§8.3) canary-wap's refusal beside the PlatformIO tree's. The opera id, the fingerprint, the heartbeat's member count, the keys the pairing reads and the Scout's events stay; every receiver checks membership and finds the sender by the first two (`handle_received_message()`), and whether the frames are encrypted is open (§14).
  - Host-tested in a private clone of this design's merge onto `e4d07edb`, with the five cases in the gates below: the zeros, the clearing, the skip, what the pairing frames keep, and a mesh turned off. Each case failed on that tree as it was and passed with the change, and each of the eight changes behind the last two cases, reverted alone, failed one of those two. The draw goes through a seam the harness holds at 0 for the tests that pin the reservation arithmetic: with a real draw, 21 of `test_mesh_liveness_wap`'s 73 tests on `e4d07edb` and 17 of `test_mesh_address_wap`'s checks failed on their exact counters. With all of them, the eight host suites that built or read `mesh_network.cpp` on that tree passed, the liveness suite with 78 tests. None of it was run again on `0a06c14b`. There the liveness suite has 83 tests before PR 8 adds its five (#1836 and #1920 added five each) and the command suite still has 18 (both built with g++ and run from this tree on 2026-11-16), and nine targets in `tests_host/Makefile` build or read `mesh_network.cpp`.
- **`/api/bluetooth`**
  - drops `local_address`;
  - gives sessions salted row ids;
  - keeps names and addresses, the signal and distance of the connected peer and of each scanned device (`rssi`, `distance_m`, `distance_label`), and each device's times and counts (`age_sec`, `paired_timestamp`, `last_connected_sec`, `connection_count`) for the bearer. A session keeps each row's id, type and flags.
  - changes the administrator page's Bluetooth panel with it in `web_ui.h`. The panel reads those keys today (the connection card's name, address, signal and distance, in `refreshBtStatus()`; each scan row's name, address, signal and distance, in `renderBtScanList()`; each paired row's name and address, in `loadBtPairedDevices()`), and a page signed in with the session alone sends no bearer (`secureFetch()`). So without the change every scan row would name an "Unknown device" at "? dBm", and a paired row's Remove button, which sends `DELETE /api/bluetooth/paired` with `{ address: bond.address }` from the list the page last drew (`btRemovePaired(i)`, F407), would send a body with no address. A session's rows show their type and flags, and in place of name, address, signal and distance the WAP's held phrase (§3.4), once per panel. Remove sends the row's id, and the forget route (`handle_bluetooth_paired_remove()` in `bluetooth_api.h`) maps it back to the bonded address on the device; so do the trust and block routes (`handle_bluetooth_paired_trust()`, `handle_bluetooth_paired_block()`), which take an address today too. The panel's bytes count against §14's rule that the page shrinks or holds until PR 12, which the budget gate enforces once it exists (§5.3).
- **Passkey.** Nothing is left: since #1887 neither health-log line carries the digits, and #1836 (F191's fix) writes them the same way (P8).
- **Chirp.** Its hour field is 0 without a trusted clock.

**Gates:**

- `test_mesh_liveness_wap.cpp`, five new cases: a heartbeat built at uptimes from 0 to 4,000,000 s carries 0 in the header's `timestamp` and in `uptime_sec`, and its member still hears it; a heartbeat sent over a stack filled with 0xA5 carries 0 in its five padding bytes; eight boots from one flash resume at eight different counters, not all at one place in the 1,024 step, each above the stored reservation and at most it plus 2^20, at one counter for every member and heard at once, while a first member added on two boots of an empty flash starts at two different counters; the initiator broadcasts no DISCOVER, and the joiner's DISCOVER, the OFFER and the ACCEPT, each sent over a stack filled with 0xA5, carry 0 in every byte of their name fields and member counts, and the ACCEPT in its device key, beside the joiner's public key and role, the OFFER's device key and both ephemeral keys, and the pairing still completes, the initiator holding the joiner's real key; and a WAP whose owner turned the mesh off, before a reboot and after, refuses a pairing start of either role, sends nothing for 125 s and sends no Scout's event, hub election, channel lock or OFFLINE_IMMINENT, each of which reaches the member again once the mesh is on, one turned off during a pairing and then canceled stays off and silent, a WAP with no opera still pairs either way, and a joiner with no opera whose pairing ends by timeout or failure goes back to `MESH_DISABLED`, sending nothing after the timeout. And the other host suites that build or read `mesh_network.cpp`.
- `test_identity_json.cpp` (new pinned bytes), `test_auth_logic.cpp` (the seam: `OK`, `HELD`, `WRONG`, a case for each seal word, the cookie, and the open outcome on the two Wi-Fi routes), `test_status_tier_logic.cpp` (a session's strip never says `restarted` or `low_memory`, whatever the inputs), `test_coarse_uptime.cpp` (new), `test_wap_diagnostics.cpp` (both tiers), `check_route_security.py` (the seam as a gate, the new `Tier::Owner` only in the `OK` branch with plants, allowlist reasons updated), `check_wap_json_answers.py` (`RECORDED` updated) and its self-test, `check_details_sync.sh` (new).
- PR 3's `lint_device_details.py`: the legacy rows for these producers shrink in this PR; P20's reads and the WAP's Wi-Fi signal (`/api/wifi`'s and the scan's `rssi`, and `handle_status`'s `node_status` input) reach only the owner tier, the signal on the two Wi-Fi routes the SoftAP interface too; the answers that still give a session what PR 8 moves elsewhere, or an activity count, are recorded (§3.6); and the WAP's list 2 and `held` rules read them (PR 3 lands first: PR 8 depends on PR 4, which depends on PR 3).
- `gen_web_assets_gz.py --check` with the budget gate, which #1971 did not build and this PR needs first (§5.3): `/admin` does not grow; `selftest_ui.test.js` (the wizard on the AP still gets every probe), `wizard_logic.test.js`.
- `test_wap_tune_lab.cpp`: a session's settings body that raises the privacy ceiling is refused and stores nothing, its other keys included; the bearer's raise applies at once; a session's lowering applies; and a ceiling of P1 or P2 stored before the PR is P0, stored and applied, after its first boot, the bearer's raise after that survives every later boot, a device with nothing stored gets no ceiling row, and `init()` runs the lowering once, just before the apply. ### PR 10: The USB console answers `q` everywhere

**Firmware** (§5.2-5.5):

- `q` on every family. Sense's tuning console reads whole lines, so it takes `q` and `j` each as a one-letter line, through a new command hook (§5.5).
- `j` on the WAP, Sense and Sentinel.
- The display reads `q` in `glass_web_tick()`, so it answers whenever no setup wizard is up (§5.4); its output goes through `dbg_serial()` only, never `log_line()` or the LAN log sink.

**Readers:**

- `flash-core.js` gains `parseDetailsLines()`, a line-wise collector.
- `flash.js` renders From the Board Itself.
- `desktop/flash-engine/src/monitor.rs` emits `serial:details`.
- `gen_device_vocabulary.py` gains its JSON output, `canary-local/devices/device_details.json` (§2.3), which these readers are the first to read.
- `desktop/src-tauri/build.rs` bakes `device_details.json` beside `flash.json` for the Flasher's `app.js`, and the Lab's `flash-native.js` reads the same-origin copy its page fetches (§2.3); each renders it in its own code.
- When no details line comes from a display, as none does while a setup wizard is up, all three say "This display answers when its setup screen is not showing. Finish setup, or wait for it to rejoin Wi-Fi, then read again." rather than showing nothing (§5.4).
- **Both `sanitizeLogTail()`s** replace details lines with their share projection, before any diagnostic report includes the tail; the manifest marker is PR 1's, built in #1970 (§6.4, P14).
- The website's `js/serial.js` and `canary.html` (sibling-repo PR), with `upstream-contract.json` carrying `device_details.json`.
- `spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json` joins the path lists of `canary-local.yml` and `desktop-hub-core.yml`, and `canary-local/devices/device_details.json` joins both of `desktop-hub-core.yml`'s, beside `canary-local/devices/flash.json`.
- **The flash catalog moves.** `canary-local/tools/gen_flash.py` derives `serial_receipt` from each firmware's own sources (`supports_serial_receipt()`), so this PR re-runs it. That field decides whether a flasher asks for `j`, and it is false today for the WAP, the Sense and every display. `supports_serial_receipt()` finds a `j` only as `case 'j'` or a `{'j', "self_manifest"` row in a project whose sources name `attest/self_manifest.h` and `emit_self_manifest`. The WAP's staged copy and Sense's one-letter line must be shapes it reads, or the flashers go on not asking those two for `j`. The `monitor` record is not derived: its kind and chips come from `MONITOR_VOCAB`, a table written by hand in `gen_flash.py` that `_monitor_record()` holds to the firmware, and `desktop_parity.test.js` keeps a second hand table (`A86_ROLE_FIRMWARE`). Both say the display is `"kind": "none"`, whose placeholder reads "this firmware reads no serial commands", and the test asserts that for every display product and again for three display descriptors. So this PR edits both tables, those assertions, and the comments in both files that say the display reads nothing. The checks force that edit for only one form of read: `_monitor_record()` refuses kind `none` ("give it its vocabulary") and the test's walk of the display tree fails on a direct `Serial.read(`, `Serial.available(` or the like, but a read through `dbg_serial()` matches neither, and the catalog would go on saying the display reads nothing. Kind `keys` needs a `handle_serial_commands()` in the named handler that answers `h`, because its placeholder says "h shows its menu", and the display reads only `q`, in `glass_web_tick()`. Whether the display gains such a handler with an `h`, or the generator and the test gain a kind for a one-key console, is the maintainer's choice.

**Gates:**

- `test_self_manifest.cpp`: the release-shaped worst case with `q` is pinned at 1,294 B, under 1,600 B (a host build of `self_manifest.h` at `0a06c14b`, 2026-11-16, §1.2); Vision's worst case is pinned too.
- `test_test_console.cpp` (`table_is_safe()` with `q` as Diag).
- `check_details_sync.sh`, `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`.
- The Dist drift check shows no change, and the size guards on every env.
- `desktop_parity.test.js`: both flashers and `flash-native.js` parse the fixture lines identically, and both scrubbers turn real `q` lines into the same report text (the `j` line's marker is PR 1's gate).
- `lint_device_details.py`'s rule that `details_glue.cpp` names neither `log_line` nor `g_log_sink`.
- `lint_usb_console.py`, the website's tests that load `js/serial.js`, `test_site_contract.py` after the carry.
- `gen_device_vocabulary.py --check` (the JSON vocabulary now among its outputs).
- `gen_flash.py --check` (the derived `serial_receipt`, and the `monitor` record held to the firmware; `desktop_parity.test.js` reads each firmware's answered commands a second time and holds each product's chips to them).
- `check_path_filter_reads.py`, `ci_policy_check.py`.

### PR 11: Displays and the headless trio, `/api/details` at the lan tier

- **Display.**
  - `GET /api/details` (§5.4).
  - `/api/device` and `/api/log` gain the Origin refusal (#1986 added the foreign-Host refusal on both; §13), and `/api/device` loses `ssid`, `signal`, `heap_min_kb` and `up_s` (P10, P9). The same change moves both routes' `legacy` entries from `open` to `lan`, because `lint_device_details.py` fails on a key that reaches a tier its row does not say (§3.6).
  - `/api/log`'s lines lose their uptime stamps, and the route is recorded as a legacy answer that stays on the lan tier (P22, decided in §14).
  - The mirror page moves to `/api/details`, and its signal word (`sigWord()`) goes with `/api/device`'s `signal` (§5.4). Its label region joins the generator here, so this PR runs §2.4's chain for it: `gen_device_vocabulary.py`, `setup.sh regen`, `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`.
- **Trio.** `node_http`'s `fill_details` hook and route.
- **`node_status.h`** (P9, P23): `uptime_s` from `coarse_uptime.h`; no `rssi` and no signal word in its place (the signal is activity, §3.1); no `wall_polled_s_ago`.
- **The trio's peer heartbeat** (P9, P23, §5.5): its `rssi` byte sent as 0; its `uptime_s` from `coarse_uptime.h`, with the same offset as `node_status`, in `stamp()` and `election()`; its `seq` starting each boot at `peer::seq_start(esp_random())` **(new, pure)**, a value in [0, 2^31); `elect()` comparing whole hours with no age term. Without this the coarse `node_status` uptime hides nothing from a host that joins the group. One question is open (§14): since #1932 `accept()` takes a claimed reboot only from a boot's first frames and seconds (`REBOOT_SEQ_WINDOW`, 8, and `REBOOT_UPTIME_WINDOW_S`, 120, in `peer_protocol.h`), and a counter that starts anywhere in [0, 2^31) beside an uptime in whole hours does not fit that window as it is written (§5.5).
- **First route check.** `firmware/scripts/check_lan_routes.py` **(new)**.
- **Docs.** `docs/FIRMWARE_MAP.md`; `docs/PARITY.md`, whose row C12 lists `rssi` in the `/api/status` body; and `docs/TRUST_MODEL.md`, whose sections 3 and 4 state the heartbeat's replay rule and the coordinator rule.

**Gates:** `check_lan_routes.py` (with the no-stamp rule, and a rule that `peer_link.cpp`'s `begin()` seeds the frame counter from `peer::seq_start()`), `check_onboarding_sync.sh` (the WAP sketch's staged `node_status.h`; the display sketch's copy is held by `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`, below), `test_host_guard.cpp`, `test_node_status.cpp` (new bytes, and no signal key for any RSSI, §10 item 7), `test_fleet_selfreport.cpp`, `test_coarse_uptime.cpp`, `test_peer_protocol.cpp` (the signal byte is 0 whatever the RSSI; the uptime field steps at the same instants as `node_status`'s; two nodes in the same hour elect the same coordinator whatever the seconds since each was heard; `seq_start()` is below 2^31 for every draw, and frames counted up from it are accepted in turn and refused when replayed; and a rebooted peer is taken in again by whatever rule the open question above settles on: at `0a06c14b` a reboot whose counter starts past `REBOOT_SEQ_WINDOW` is a replay until its row expires, `STALE_MS` after the row last took a frame, and is then a new peer, by a host build of `peer_protocol.h` on 2026-11-16), `test_device_details.cpp` lan-tier bytes, `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`, `gen_device_vocabulary.py --check` (the mirror region), the Dist drift check (no change: `glass_web.cpp`, `node_http.cpp`, `peer_link.cpp` and `peer_protocol.h` are outside the emulator set), size guards.

### PR 15: Sensors report their part, link and health

| Part | What it adds |
|---|---|
| Camera | `esp_camera_sensor_get_info()`, and a 16-bit PID table on the WAP |
| MR60 | its latched status word at every tier; on its link view, unknown and dropped frames as `activity` (serial on Sense and Sentinel; never `lan` or `broker`) |
| Vision | on its link view, `perf()` timings as `activity` (`duration: true`, `_ms`); the catalog-match flag on its identity page |
| Sentinel | on the fusion link view, per-channel quality and "has reported" as `activity`, one entry per compiled channel |
| GNSS | link state, never coordinates |
| SD | kind and capacity, never the identity register |

Each part's status moves from `planned` to `read` only when the lint sees its `put`.

**Gates:** `lint_device_details.py` (status earned rung by rung; `_ms` only with `duration: true`), `test_mr60_uart.cpp`, the `test_device_details.cpp` sensor pages and link views including the present-then-empty replay and `held` per kind, the generated bound per (part, kind), the Invariant II word guard on every new part sheet, size guards.

### PR 16: The MQTT details topic and the Home Assistant device page

**Firmware:**

- The flagship, WAP, Sense, Vision and Sentinel publish a retained `<prefix>/<id>/details` carrying `about`, on connect and on change, never on a timer.
- In Sense's and Sentinel's `src/net/mqtt_mgr.cpp` the publish is byte-identical, because `check_sentinel_net_sync.sh` pins those files byte for byte outside the regions it names: `publish_heartbeat()` and `publish_state_retained()`, masked in both copies, the identify and radar-dial code it excises from canary-sense's copy only, and one comment line it swaps.
- HA discovery device objects gain `hw_version` on the WAP, Sense, Vision and Sentinel. The flagship's has carried it since #1971, as `hw`, on a build that names a board (`add_device_info()` in `securacv_mqtt.cpp`).

**Integration:**

- Hardware and Model ID come from the topic.
- `async_get_device_diagnostics` is added (§6.12), returning projections only. `TO_REDACT` is the second net behind it, and is built: it has held `uptime`, `boot_count`, `sd_writes`, `http_requests` and `last_frame_age_ms` since #1916, and since #1961 through the generated `REDACTED_DEVICE_KEYS`.
- `docs/homeassistant_setup.md` is a hand-written **input**. If its entity catalog or topic table changes, it is edited by hand and `canary-local/tools/gen_homeassistant.py` is re-run, which writes `canary-local/devices/homeassistant.json`.

**Gates:**

- The broker bound host test: 584 B or less against 1,536, 1,792 and 2,048, computed with PubSubClient's `5 + 2 + strlen(topic) + plength`. The WAP publishes through esp_mqtt and is outside that bound (§5.7).
- `check_sentinel_net_sync.sh`.
- `scripts/tests/test_ha_discovery_binary_sensors.py` (device-object size).
- The HA pytest suite: registry values are `str`; diagnostics projected from real flagship, WAP and Sense health fixtures carry no uptime, counter or identifier; unknown keys are dropped.
- Run `gen_homeassistant.py` and diff `canary-local/devices/homeassistant.json`, after `gen_wap.py` if the WAP's topics moved (neither has a `--check`; `canary-local.yml`'s "WAP data drift" and "Hub data drift" steps run them and diff); builds and size guards.

No emulator-compiled file includes `details_esp.h`, or an IDF header the shim does not carry.

Order, §2.4's chain for the display, with the push the rebuild needs before its dispatch (step 4, which `docs/GENERATED_FILES.md` does not state) and that file's notes on the bot's push (steps 9 and 10):

1. edit;
2. `gen_device_vocabulary.py`;
3. `setup.sh regen`;
4. commit the edit and the regenerated files and push them, because the rebuild builds the branch as it is on GitHub (`emulator-dist-refresh.yml` checks out the dispatched ref);
5. dispatch "Rebuild emulator dist (pinned emsdk)" on the branch. From this PR on, any change to `device_details_keys.h`, `device_details.h`, `self_manifest.h`, `include/canary/details_facts.h`, `emu_details.cpp` or a glass label moves the dist;
6. pull the bot's dist commit;
7. `gen_flash.py` and the catalogs;
8. commit;
9. push a path the workflows watch;
10. check for `action_required` runs before pushing again.

**Gates:**

- The Dist drift check.
- The page logic tests (`canary_local.test.js`, `vision.test.js`).
- `check_boot_sync.sh`, `check_display_arduino_sync.sh`.
- `gen_flash.py --check` after the pull.
- The display size guards as they exist when it lands. At `62bc0bc7` `firmware/flavors.json` guards eight display envs, all seven of its `release_envs` among them: `canary-display-nightstand-c6` (0x1F0000, the tightest guarded image, §1.2), `canary-display-nightlight-c3` (held at the old 0x1E0000 ceiling), `canary-display-watch` (0x330000), and `canary-display-dash7`, `-nightstand7`, `-nightstand-s3`, `-touch169` and `-amoled241` (0x640000). Two released display products have no guard: `firmware-release.yml` builds `canary-display-dash` and `canary-display-dash-modes` with the Arduino CLI, and `check_slot_budget.py` passes an env that declares no guard, with a warning. Their OTA images are 2,291,424 B and 2,350,576 B in fw-v2.4.20, and were 2,287,216 B and 2,346,288 B in fw-v2.4.19 (the releases' assets, read on 2026-11-16). The two 4 MB envs, `canary-display-nightstand-c6` and `canary-display-nightlight-c3`, already build `CD_LEAN_BUILD`, and the lean form is sized against nightstand-c6; the dash images are 16 MB Arduino CLI builds without it. The PR description also reports the measured delta for every display release env, guarded or not.
- Home Assistant: `custom_components/securacv/tests/test_absent_battery.py` (#1971) already reads a battery reported absent as unknown whatever its value. Its monorepo-only check of the firmware's health bodies, which holds the trio's today, gains the display's.
- The emulator links the setup-phase hook through `emu_details.cpp`'s stub (the shim has no serial input), and a bench step reads `q` from a display in first-boot setup.

### PR 19: TV, website and the Lab Board Room

- **TV.**
  - The optional `fw` on `/api/fleet`, contract-first (§6.8), through `fleet_selfreport.h`. `docs/GENERATED_FILES.md` records that this header does not move the dist. It is the one addition rule 3 allows.
  - The TV's Model and Firmware rows, and Board once the board-name source §6.5 leaves open reaches the Wall (§6.8).
  - `spec/fixtures/device_details_v1.json` joins `tvos.yml`'s path lists if `witness-core` decodes it.
- **Website.** `/canary`'s About section, glossary entries, and product-page "What it can share", with static ladder words only.
- **Lab.** The Board Room's "What this board can report", with static ladder words only. It reads the tiers from `firmware/boards/boards.json`, which the catalog it fetches today does not carry (§6.11).

**Gates:** `tvos/witness-core` cargo tests on the vectors, `DeviceParityTests`, `test_fleet_selfreport.cpp`, `check_fleet_selfreport_sync.sh`, the kernel's `fleet_peers.rs` tests (unchanged: the kernel's own rows), the website's copy-honesty, upstream-contract and generated-docs tests (the last holds the glossary), `test_site_contract.py`, `gen_csp.py --check`, `ci_policy_check.py`.

### PR 20: Radio addresses that do not follow the chip (bench-gated, default off)

- **Wi-Fi.** `firmware/common/network/ap_mac_policy.h` **(new, host-tested)** sets the SoftAP address before every setup AP on the WAP, the flagship and `setup_portal.cpp`. The address is **drawn at random per boot or per setup session** (`esp_random()`), locally administered and unicast, and never a function of the eFuse MAC. Setting only the locally administered bit would change nothing on the S3, whose SoftAP address already has it (P3). The display's portal AP lives in emulator-compiled `provision.cpp` and follows in a dist PR.
- **Bluetooth.** `ble_addr_policy.h` **(new)** gives a random static address per boot for broadcast-only advertisers, and an RPA for the bonded WAP console. It ships behind `FEATURE_BLE_RANDOM_ADDR`, default off until a reader audit covers:
  - the display's chirp and fleet-link scans;
  - the iOS beacon merge;
  - CoreBluetooth caching;
  - the flasher BLE console.
- **Disclosures.** Because the lint resolves each family's released envs (§10 item 2), adding these default-off calls flips **no** row: every released image still uses the factory addresses, and list 1 keeps saying so.
  - A family's Bluetooth row flips only in the PR that turns `FEATURE_BLE_RANDOM_ADDR` on in every one of that family's shipped configurations (§7.1).
  - Its BSSID row flips only when the AP policy is compiled into its released images; for the displays, only in the dist PR that changes `provision.cpp`.
  - THREAT_MODEL changes in the same PR as each flip, never before. This PR says plainly that the change is opt-in.
  - The residual is stated: the `SCV-XXXX` name and two fingerprint bytes still link a unit locally.

**Gates:** `test_ap_mac_policy.cpp` (two seeds give different addresses; the low five bytes never equal the base MAC's, nor base + 1; the locally administered bit set and the multicast bit clear), the `lint_device_details.py` per-family disclosure rule (no row moves in this PR), `check_fleet_beacon_sync.sh` (bytes unchanged), `check_improv_sync.sh`, `lint_feature_flags.sh`, and a bench run with the flag on, following `docs/hardware/fleet_link_bench_checklist.md` (the beacon on air, the display's scan and the GATT pull). The tree has no BLE bring-up runbook; that checklist is the bench procedure nearest the readers the audit names.

### PR 21: Signed details

The `details` canonical kind (§8):

- the dictionary's `canonical_kinds`;
- `device_signature.{h,cpp}` and the WAP's copy;
- the WAP's `&nonce=` and the `X-Canary-Sig` response header **(new)**, with the request (part, id, view, page) bound in the canonical string and echoed in the body;
- the MQTT envelope bound to a **per-boot random nonce** (never the boot counter), published beside the part;
- verifiers in `custom_components/securacv/signature.py`, the desktop (`details_verify.rs` **(new)** beside `whoami.rs`) and the iPhone.

The labels are exact. "Verified" appears only on the app's own screen, per section, for HTTP parts fetched with the app's nonce and checked against the request the app made; a rate-limited answer is unsigned and its section reads "Reported". A retained MQTT part reads "Signed by this Canary · not proven current", and Home Assistant keeps a per-device high-water mark on the verified firmware version. Shared text says "signature checked by SecuraCV <app> <version> on <day>" (§6.4, §8), never "Verified".

**Gates:** `lint_dictionary_sync.py` (the `canonical_kinds` union rule), golden vectors with the same bytes in C++, Python, Rust and Swift (the Python copy generated into `custom_components/securacv/tests/fixtures/`), a nonce-gate test, a rate-limit test (the over-limit answer is unsigned, and the app's section reads Reported), a substitution test (a signed sensor page answered for another id or page is refused), a replay test (an earlier boot's retained part verifies but is labeled not current, and older firmware than the high-water mark does not replace it), `check_signature_sync.sh`.

### PR 22: Owner authority hooks and the first adjustable settings

Owner authority landed as #1887 with no owner session, so on the WAP the owner predicate is the bearer, which the seam's owner branch reads (§3.7). This covers what §3.7 and §9 leave to this PR:

- the WAP Protection panel's activity rows (PR 12 adds the panel, once `/admin` has room for it);
- the bonded characteristic's tier, once it is decided when a bond is owner proof (open, §14);
- owner-only POSTs for the first settings, with Origin and Host checks, a health-log entry and the automatic revert. §9 records two things this inherits: a session already writes the WAP's BLE transmit power, and whether those two routes move to the bearer is open; and the iPhone app's Nightlight section keeps a glass-knob value the display refused (read in the code, not run on a device; ledger A134; since #1986 the app sends the CSRF token, §13);
- "Copy details (owner)" (§6.4);
- an owner session's branch in the seam, only if a PR gives the WAP an owner session.

The session's recorded reads (P20) are PR 8's: #1887 did not move them, and PR 8 serves them on the bearer branch, with their legacy rows' tier moved from `lan` to `owner`, so §7.2 list 2 shrinks.

**Gates:** `check_route_security.py` (each owner-only POST behind the bearer, and an owner session's branch if one is added, with plants), a host test per setting (bounds, revert), `check_route_budget.py` for every new POST, and the iOS tests.
