## 14. Risks, and the questions decided

**Risks.**

- **WAP margins.**
  - DEV/S3 stays at 10 free only because the budget line is itemized. Any other feature landing first still eats the shared margin.
  - The `/admin` page has no gzip headroom: it is 67,638 B at `62bc0bc7`, 2,102 B past the 65,536 B ceiling (§1.2). Every `web_ui.h` edit in this plan must shrink or hold the page, its generated label region included, moving content to the companion page where it needs room (the 64 KiB decision below). No gate holds that today: the `--check` budget the plan gave PR 6 was not built (§5.3), and four PRs outside the plan grew the page after #1887 (#1919, #1920, #1951 and #1972).
- **Flagship slots.** The route table has 0 spare today (§1.2). Any flagship route that lands before PR 14 must raise `kRouteTableSlots` itself, and PR 14's 78/66 assumes it is the next route.
- **Small images.** In the `.bin` bytes the guards read, as CI's size guard measured them on `8daabedc`, whose firmware `62bc0bc7` keeps (§1.2), `canary-display-nightstand-c6` has 42,128 B left in its 0x1F0000 slot, `canary-sense-wellbeing` 97,984 B (the default Sense image 101,824 B) and Sentinel Lite 148,416 B in its 0x140000 slot; the published fw-v2.4.16 nightstand-c6 image left 2,832 B (ledger F367), and v2.4.6 overflowed a display slot. PlatformIO's "Flash used" line is not the `.bin`: in the same CI run it read 58,972 to 67,928 B under it on the nightstand-c6 and the two Sense images. `canary-display-nightlight-c3` is guarded at the old 0x1E0000 ceiling on purpose, so raising it would strand old-table boards. The code estimate is unmeasured until PR 9 links it.
- **Load-shaped unit facts.** Heap free and die temperature are `unit` and reach `lan`. Both move a little with load, and load can follow activity (a live view, a burst of events). The coarse forms (KiB, whole °C) and on-request sampling keep this small. If a bench run shows them tracking the scene on any family, they move to `activity`.
- **Core split.** The two cores split APIs. A shim mistake shows a wrong value rather than "cannot say", so both compile paths are host-tested and absence is emitted rather than a guess.
- **Live-bus reads.** Reads on a live bus can stall:
  - the flash JEDEC read and touch or IMU reads during the dash's RGB scanout run once at boot;
  - SSCMA queries can block for 3 s against a 30 s watchdog, so they also run at boot.
- **Unverified registers.** Datasheet-only registers are not bench-verified: the CST816 version, the GT911 firmware and config, the FT6336 firmware, the QMI8658 revision, the ES7210/ES8311 ids, the L76K firmware query and the MR60 version frame. They read "Cannot say" until a bench read lands.
- **Emulator dist.** The display glass PR moves the emulator dist. The bot's push can park at `action_required`, and a push outside the watched paths starts no runs (`docs/GENERATED_FILES.md`), so plan human approval time into PR 17.
- **Random BLE addresses** can break bonded reconnects, Improv setup and app find flows. That is why they ship behind a flag after a reader audit.
- **Two HA device pages.** By the code (not seen in a running Home Assistant), a Canary found through MQTT discovery gets two device pages: the MQTT integration's, from the firmware's discovery (`("mqtt", "securacv_<id>")`, as the docstring of `custom_components/securacv/rollout.py` records), and this integration's (`(DOMAIN, f"canary_{device_id}")` in `entity.py`). Details may appear on one and not the other until the identifiers are unified.

**The questions revision 3 left open, decided.** Each was decided on 2026-10-22, while the design was being written, under the maintainer's standing instruction to fix the remaining open items in best engineering practice and best value for the user and security. The decisions were recorded with the design's branch; the tree carries one of them with its date, the flagship's figure (ledger F398). Where main has since built a decision or moved past it, its bullet says so.

- **Session reads (P20).** Decided: recorded, not moved, until owner sign-in lands. The session is the dashboard: it already reads the health log, the witness records, the chain and the live view these counts summarize, so moving them protects nothing (true of the witness count, the chain sequence, the live view's state and the uptime; a session reads the boot count and the SD writes nowhere else, and the egress counters only on a broker it points the MQTT bridge at, §3.2), and the strip's "restarted" and "low memory" words are the owner's everyday crash signal. They were to move behind the owner predicate in the owner-authority PR, once owner sign-in had landed. The raw posture values beside them move in PR 8 (P21). The owner-authority PR landed as #1887 and moved none of them: at `0a06c14b` `/api/status`, `/api/system`, `/api/diagnostics`, `/api/mesh` and `/api/mesh/peers` still take `api_auth_check()`. So the plan gives the move to PR 8, onto the bearer branch the signed-in administrator page sends (§3.2, §3.7); the decision named the owner-authority PR, which landed without it. The health log, the witness records, the chain and the live view stay on the session, so a session still reads the witness count, the chain sequence, the live view's state and the uptime to the second in other answers: the move takes the boot count, the SD writes and the strip's two words off a session's answers, and the egress counters off its HTTP answers but not off a broker it points the MQTT bridge at (§3.6), and the other summaries off `/api/status`'s top level and `/api/system` only, which hides little more. No page draws the two words after PR 8: the headline page has no sign-in, and the administrator page does not read them (§3.2 says what an owner reads instead, and what is open). The health log's reset line still tells a session whether the last restart was a crash (§3.2).
- **The display's `/api/log` (P22).** Decided: it stays on the lan tier, with no stamps, behind the Host and Origin refusal (PR 11). The glass switch is not taken now; it is revisited if a bench run shows the log carrying household events a LAN host should not read.
- **P19 (F392).** Decided: the recommendation is adopted. The config-entry dump keeps no fingerprint digits and gives each Canary a per-dump salted correlation id, because no support flow needs to correlate one dump with another. It is Home Assistant only, so it landed with PR 2 (#1891: `_correlation_ids()` in `diagnostics.py`, §6.12).
- **`creds_at_rest` (P16).** Decided: it stays on the open `/api/status` as an honest warning while every build is `plaintext-nvs`, because it is then the same word on every unit and tells a LAN host nothing about one unit over another. It moves behind owner proof in the first PR after which a shipped build can report a `creds_at_rest` other than `plaintext-nvs`; the finding (§11) stays open as that PR's gate. Owner proof is not the same on every family: the flagship's copy already takes the bearer, and the WAP's, which a session reads, would move to the bearer; the displays and the trio, whose open `/api/status` carries it, have no owner credential (§3.7), so on them it leaves the open answers, and only the glass and USB may carry it, unless the family has gained one by then. The decision named the PR that ships the first hardened kit, but no kit changes the word: at `0a06c14b` `docs/PARITY.md` (footnote 1, "Plaintext NVS, reported honestly") says the path is NVS encryption, which needs the arduino-as-IDF-component migration (`firmware/ESP32S3_OPTIMIZATION_ROADMAP.md` item 9), and that the secure kit does not encrypt NVS; and `key_at_rest.h`'s `classify()` gives `plaintext-nvs` whenever NVS encryption is off. The trigger keeps the decision's intent; the maintainer confirms its wording.
- **A flagship margin rule.** Decided: `MIN_SPARE = 2` (PR 14), held by `check_route_budgets()`. The flagship's table is sized by hand, so two spare slots leave room for the next route, and the assertion fails the build two routes before a registration could be dropped.
- **64 KiB.** Decided: a hard ceiling. CI fails a WAP gzip asset at 65,536 B or more (PR 12 adds the gate). Until that gate lands, every `web_ui.h` edit in this plan must shrink or hold the `/admin` page's size. PR 6 was to give that rule a gate, a `--check` that fails when `/admin`'s gzip grows past a budget the script records, and #1971 did not build it (§5.3). Main has since passed the ceiling with neither gate in place: `/admin` is 67,638 B at `62bc0bc7` (§1.2). What that leaves open is in the list below.
- **The flagship's figure.** Decided: `hw` stays omitted on the flagship until a flagship figure exists. PR 6 (#1971) did not invent one (ledger F398 lists the flagship's missing board token among what it left), and PR 3 files it as the first entry of the block's `gaps` list (§2.2, §12 PR 3), which the generated reference shows.
- **`AT+ID?` uniqueness.** Decided: the Grove module's id is treated as a per-unit identifier, and no code of ours asks for it. Built: neither flasher sends `ID?` since PR 1 (#1970), and since PR 7 (#1892) Vision's firmware makes no `ID()` call and its `module_id` is 1 or 0. One read is left: the pinned SSCMA library's own I2C `begin()` sends `ID?` once at boot and keeps the answer in a private buffer nothing reads. Whether that is accepted or `begin()` is replaced is open (§12 PR 7).
- **The MR60's unknown frames.** Decided: they are `activity` until a bench read shows which frames they are and whether their rate follows presence (§3.1, §7.3).
- **`host_guard.h` on the WAP.** Decided: PR 12 reuses the copy #1848 (F344, F337) staged with its `PAIRS` line in `check_improv_sync.sh`, and adds no second copy. #1848 has since merged, so the copy is in the tree (§3.5, §13).

**Open, for the maintainer.**

- **The opera frames (P9, P17, P20).** On FULL builds every signed opera frame the WAP sends is not encrypted, so any radio in range reads all of it (§7.2 list 1): in the header, the opera id and the whole key fingerprint; in each heartbeat, the number of other members; and on a WAP with a paired BLE Scout tag, every arrival and departure of the tag with the label the owner gave it. While the WAP is pairing, which any dashboard session can start and start again, the pairing frames, unsigned by design, are not encrypted either, and carry the keys the pairing reads: the joiner's whole public key and role in its DISCOVER, broadcast every 2 s, the initiator's in the OFFER it sends to every radio that sends a joiner's DISCOVER, each time one asks, and the two ephemeral keys. From either public key anyone computes the device's fingerprint, and from that its device id. Every receiver checks membership and finds the sender by the id and the fingerprint (`handle_received_message()` in `mesh_network.cpp`), and the pairing reads those keys, so they cannot simply be dropped. PR 8 sends 0 for the two clocks and the heartbeat's padding, starts each boot's counters past a random skip, clears the pairing frames and sends in them only those keys and the joiner's role, and sends nothing, a pairing included, from a mesh the owner turned off. Whether the frames are encrypted, payload and header, or carry values that name neither the household nor the device, is a change to the mesh protocol and is the maintainer's decision, and so is whether `pair/start` and `pair/join` take owner proof, which a session is not and which #1887 left them without (§3.2): until one of them changes, any LAN host can have the WAP send its public key to whoever asks. Encrypting the header alone would leave who comes and goes on the air, and encrypting payload and header would still leave when: a Scout event goes out on the loop pass after the tag is seen or lost (`drain_outbound_beacon_queue()`, which `loop()` in `csi_integration.cpp` calls on every pass), off the heartbeat's 30 s cadence, and is 127 B where a heartbeat is 114 B (a host build of this tree's `mesh_network.cpp` on 2026-11-16, not run on a device), so a radio in range would still date each arrival and departure unless the event were padded to the heartbeat's length and held to its cadence. Until the decision is made, list 1 names all of it, PR 4's empty Device Information serial does not take the fingerprint off the air on such a WAP, and PR 8's move of `/api/mesh` takes the id off the LAN answers only.
- **When a bond is owner proof (F346, §3.7).** Since #1887 a session confirms no Bluetooth bond and reads no Passkey Entry's digits, and since #1836 a Just Works bond no owner asked for is deleted. A bond a session confirmed before #1887 stays until the owner removes it, and a Just Works bond that escapes #1836's delete stays in NimBLE's store, left out of the rebuilt paired list since #1920 (ledger F446, open and bench-gated). So the bonded `about` characteristic stays at the lan tier (PR 13). Whether it moves to the owner tier, and on what condition (every bond the WAP holds made with the bearer, or the owner clearing the older ones), is the maintainer's decision.
- **The crash on a session's answers, and safe mode on the open ones (P21).** After PR 8 a session reads no reset cause word, but it still learns whether the last restart was a panic, a watchdog or a brownout from the health log's line from that boot and from the tamper word, the row and the standing `tamper.kind` on `/api/events/today` and the snapshot on `/api/csi/stream`; once three crash resets have come in a row, `/api/status`'s `safe_mode` and the strip's `safe_mode` reason tell a session, and `/api/selftest`'s `safe_mode` and `/api/help-qr`'s QR tell any LAN host (§3.2). Hiding the crash from a session takes all of them, and each has its own cost:
  - **The log.** Leaving the cause out of the line would not hide it, because the line is written only after those resets; leaving the line out of a session's `/api/logs` and the log export would leave a gap in the `seq` the log numbers its entries by (`log_health()` in `canary_wap.ino`: `entry.seq = ++g_device.log_seq`), which says as much. So the choice there is between a line at every boot, whatever the cause, with the cause for the bearer, and the line on the serial console only.
  - **The tamper word.** Its boot kinds on the seam's bearer branch only, or off the LAN altogether (the broker's `tamper` topic and the SD event log keep them, through `csi_event_egress::on_committed()`, which `csi_event_on_committed()` in `csi_integration.cpp` calls). The broker's copy stays within a session's reach while a session can point the MQTT bridge at a broker of its own, because `publish_tamper_bridge()` publishes to whichever broker the bridge points at (§3.6, and the broker question below). Either takes "Rebooted unexpectedly", "Recovered from a system hang" and "Power was cut" off the CSI dashboard for an owner who is not signed in, as PR 8 takes the strip's `restarted`.
  - **Safe mode.** `/api/status`'s `safe_mode` and the strip's reason for the bearer, and the safe-mode verdict on `/api/selftest` and `/api/help-qr` only on the SoftAP interface, where the setup wizard reads them. That would leave an owner who is not signed in with no word for why the WAP came up with its peripherals off, while its retry, `POST /api/safe-mode/retry`, already takes the bearer (#1887).

  - **The flash-encryption lines.** On FULL builds the health log's two "flash encryption disabled" lines tell a session and a bond whether flash encryption is off, and a session can bring the second back on demand (§3.2). No PR in this plan moves the health log; whether PR 8 changes these lines, as it might the reset line, is open too.

  Until the maintainer decides, all of these are recorded (§3.6), and `held` counts neither the reset cause nor, on a FULL build, flash encryption.
- **The privacy ceiling's lowering (P20).** Any session can raise the Wi-Fi sensing's privacy ceiling today, and PR 8 takes a raise to the bearer and, on its first boot, lowers a stored ceiling above P0 to P0, once, because a raise any LAN host made before it would otherwise outlive the move (§3.2, §12 PR 8); the maintainer confirms the lowering, which costs an owner who raised the ceiling by hand one raise with the token. Ledger F393 holds it with P20's move; whether it takes an item of its own is the maintainer's call.
- **A broker move on the WAP (§3.6).** Any dashboard session can point the WAP's MQTT bridge at a broker of its own: `POST /api/mqtt/config` takes the session, and `move_needs_password()` (`csi_mqtt.cpp`) lets a move through whose body carries a password. That broker then receives everything the WAP publishes, its status's signal, its health's uptime, the chain length, the witness count, the event-egress and mesh airtime counters and the tamper word among them. Whether PR 8 takes a broker move to the bearer is the maintainer's decision; until it does, `held` counts none of those facts at the WAP's lan tier (§3.2).
- **The `/admin` page, its ceiling and its budget gate (§5.3).** `/admin`'s gzip is 67,638 B at `62bc0bc7` (#1972 added 144 B to `0a06c14b`'s 67,494 B), 2,102 B past the 65,536 B ceiling decided above (ledger F479), so PR 12's gate cannot land until the page has lost at least 2,103 B (to 65,535 B) or the ceiling is decided again. Which of the two happens is the maintainer's decision. The budget gate PR 6 was to add is still owed, and PR 8, the next plan PR to edit `web_ui.h`, needs it: whether PR 8 carries the gate or a PR of its own lands it first is open too.
- **The trio's peer heartbeat and #1932's reboot window (P9, §5.5, §12 PR 11).** PR 11 starts the heartbeat's `seq` each boot at a random value below 2^31 and sends its `uptime_s` in whole hours, so that neither dates the boot. Since #1932 `accept()` in `firmware/common/network/peer_protocol.h` takes a claimed new boot only from a frame whose `seq` is at most `REBOOT_SEQ_WINDOW` (8) and whose `uptime_s` is under `REBOOT_UPTIME_WINDOW_S` (120); the frame that confirms it is not held to the window again. A counter that starts anywhere below 2^31 is almost never inside that window, so a rebooted peer would be refused as a replay until its row expires, and a whole-hour `uptime_s` reads 0 for a boot's first hour or more, so the 120 s limit would not close in that time (§5.5 gives the host run). How PR 11 keeps the reboot window beside a counter and an uptime that do not date the boot is the maintainer's decision.
- **Questions this landing opened in other sections.** Reading the plan against `0a06c14b` and then `62bc0bc7` opened these, each stated where it arises and each the maintainer's: the vocabulary file's row shape and its classes beside this design's (§2.2, ledger HA62 and HA63); which generated output carries the board registry's names to the apps, and how the WAP's `hw` token, which is not a registry `id`, finds its row (§6.5); whether a signed-in page draws the strip's two reasons before the Protection panel reaches `/admin`, and whether PR 8 clears the stream's snapshot when the ceiling is lowered (§3.2); whether a self-test row's `code` and `detail` leave the other interfaces with its metrics (§12 PR 8); whether the app's two whole-fingerprint rows collapse (§6.5); how the desktop drawer reaches a flagship's owner tier, and whether the desktop card's values stay monospaced or follow §6.2's identifiers-only rule (§6.9, §12 PR 1); whether the WAP's two session writes of the BLE transmit power move to the bearer, and which side changes so the iPhone app's glass-knob writes pass the display's CSRF guard (§9; #1986 answered it: the app sends the token, and ledger A134 keeps the refusals the Nightlight section drops); whether a PR adds the flashers' per-row copy action or §6.2 drops it (§12 PR 1); how the display's one-key `q` console takes a `monitor` kind in the flash catalog (§12 PR 10); whether the SSCMA library's one read of the module's id at boot is accepted or its `begin()` replaced (§12 PR 7); whether a PR coarsens what Vision shares before Details describes it (§7.3; #1986 made the beacon's share an owner opt-in, off by default, and ledger F483 holds `SECURITY_MODEL.md`'s silence on it); whether a PR of this plan changes the WAP's Wi-Fi sensing probe and Chirp channel broadcasts (§7.2 list 1; ledger F481 and F482 hold them); whether the display's `/api/glass`, and its `/api/fleet` peer rows to a caller with no cross-site Origin, which give any host on the network each wellbeing Sense's breathing lock with its name and room, and `/api/glass` the seconds since each witness was last heard, stay the LAN glance the product promises (the comment above `handle_glass()` in `glass_web.cpp`) or become a finding (§7.2 list 2); whether a PR moves the WAP's household context, override and roster (`/api/presence`, `/api/household`) off the lan tier, where any session reads them and no PR in this plan moves them (§3.6, §7.2 list 2); and whether a WAP on mains power stops sending a battery of 100 (§1.4, ledger F398).

---

## Appendix A. Claims checked for this design, and the ones dropped

**Confirmed at `351d9f9a`, and checked again at `0a06c14b` and at `62bc0bc7` on 2026-11-16.** For this landing the budgets and code facts below were read from the tree again or run on it (at `62bc0bc7` where the 2.4.20 train touched their sources; the others' sources did not change between the two), and the SDK facts were read from the installed cores (Arduino-ESP32 3.3.8 with IDF 5.5.4, and 2.0.17 with IDF 4.4.7) and from espefuse 5.6.0's tables. Facts the installed cores do not hold were read at espressif/esp-idf's release tags on the same day: which release introduced each reset reason (`components/esp_system/include/esp_system.h`, §2.3), which address each radio interface takes (`components/esp_hw_support/mac_addr.c` at v4.4.7 and v5.5.4, §5.6), and the two SDK behaviors §5.1 and §5.2 cite.

- **Budgets.** The WAP route numbers in §1.2, from running the checkers. The flagship's worst case, from the flagship checker's own parser: revision 1's "73/61, 2 spare" was wrong, and it is 75/63, 0 spare. The display slots, from `flavors.json`: revision 1's "0x1F0000 for the 4 MB C3/C6 displays" was wrong for the nightlight-c3, which is guarded at 0x1E0000.
- **Code facts.**
  - `api_auth_check()` falls through to the bearer compare.
  - `api_auth_bearer_result()` makes one accounted grant.
  - Console keys: the flagship's free keys are q, y and z; the WAP's key set; Vision answers `j` and `h`; the display and Sentinel have no serial reader.
  - `glass_web.cpp`'s guarded and unguarded routes.
  - The release OTA manifests are version 1.
  - `figures.json` maps `board:seeed_xiao_esp32s3` to the WAP with `shared: false`.
  - The emulator compile set, including the per-flavor color and `ambient_led` TUs that revision 1 left out.
  - `USB_SERIAL` defaults to `"__MAC__"` on the S3 in both cores.
  - The eFuse **field** layouts in espefuse 5.6.0's `esp32s3.yaml`, `esp32c3.yaml` and `esp32c6.yaml`. Revision 1 listed a word table as confirmed, and it was wrong: on the S3 and the C3, BLOCK1 words 4-5 and BLOCK2 word 4 hold per-chip trims beside the SKU and block-version fields, and on the C6 BLOCK2 word 4 holds trims alone.
  - `ESP_RST_USB` and its siblings are enumerators, not macros, and IDF 4.4.7 has none of them. By the `esp-idf` release tags (§2.3), USB and JTAG first appear in IDF v5.2.0, and eFuse, power glitch and CPU lockup in v5.3.0; v5.1.4 and v5.2.2 carry all five.
  - The self-manifest's release worst case is 1,265 B (a host build of `self_manifest.h` at `0a06c14b`, §1.2), not "about 1.4 KB".
- **Findings.** Every finding in §11 was checked against the tree when it was first written. Four plan PRs have merged since, so §11 and §12 say what is left of each.

**Dropped or corrected from the proposals this design merges.**

| Claim | Correction |
|---|---|
| `esp_flash_get_physical_size()` is IDF 5 only | It is in the 2.0.17 SDK too. |
| `TO_REDACT` lacks `unique_id` | It already has it. |
| `check_wap_json_answers.py` lives under `scripts/` | It is under `firmware/scripts/`. |
| `sys_monitor.h` reads the revision with `efuse_hal_chip_revision()` | It calls `ESP.getChipRevision()`. |
| The flagship's MQTT buffer is 1 KB | It is 1,792 B. |
| The flagship has a function-static big-body buffer pattern | It has one, and this design dropped the claim in error: `handle_status()`, `handle_fleet()` and `handle_mqtt_ca()` in `securacv_network.cpp` each keep a body in a function static, off the HTTP task's stack. The details body is 768 B and this design puts it on the handler stack (§5.2). |
| `manifest::detail::Writer` can be reused as-is with a measure mode | It needs extending. |
| The capability ladder's "Absent" can read "On the board" | Absent means the silicon is not populated, so the row is omitted. |
| Editing display `src/fleet/*.cpp` or `src/io/rtc.cpp` needs no dist rebuild | Both are emulator-compiled. |
| `/api/device-info`'s `hw_token` has page readers | It has none. |
| Moving `hw_token` behind a WAP session protects it | Any LAN host can mint a session. |
| The pairing-PIN exposure is new | It was already filed, as F346. This design added only the health-log lines, and #1887 closed both: a Passkey Entry's digits reach only the bearer, and neither line carries the digits (P8). |
| A bonded BLE read is owner proof on the WAP | Not yet. F346 is decided (#1887), but a bond a session confirmed before it stays (§3.7, §14). |
| On-device model verification by SHA-256 | The pinned SSCMA library has no call that returns the model's bytes, so the firmware cannot hash it. From PR 15 the device reports a catalog-name match (§7.3), and the flasher verifies the hash at install time. |
| Echoing the Grove module's `INFO?` model name | It is never echoed. |
| Serving owner or household facts on the USB console | The console stays public-only. Posture is not a secret and already prints there (the flagship's `f` card and the manifest's `key_at_rest`), so revision 2 lets the serial tier carry it rather than claim to hold it. |
| A replayable signed-details form | HTTP binds the client's nonce. MQTT binds a per-boot random nonce and claims authorship only ("not proven current"); revision 1's `boot:<n>` counter form claimed a freshness no verifier could check. |


---

## Review history

This is a summary of the round-by-round record the design's branch carried. That record is not on main, so the tree cannot confirm its counts. By it, the design was written against `351d9f9a` in three revisions and reviewed there twice, each time through three lenses: code facts, privacy and buildability. Its branch then followed main through `377bbc6f`, `e4d07edb` and `3453dd26`, with three landing reviews (the second and third on `377bbc6f`), five reviews of the merge onto `e4d07edb` and three of the merge of `3453dd26`. Before it landed, every section was fact-checked again against `0a06c14b`. Every section was then read against `62bc0bc7`, after the 2.4.20 train, in three to five rounds of independent review and repair, each finding checked against the tree before anything changed; that record is not on main either.

- **Revision 1** merged the separate proposals into one design: one record cut into parts, one vocabulary, a generator for every copy, and a firmware writer that spells only generated keys. Appendix A lists the claims it made that later checks dropped or corrected.
- **Revision 2** made eFuse reads a per-chip field allowlist (§5.6), added the `activity` class (§3.1), made uptime whole hours counted from a per-boot random offset (§4.1), bound the MQTT signature to a per-boot nonce that claims authorship only (§8), and made `held` a generated constant (§4.1).
- **Revision 3** found that the WAP then asked no Host question (it does since #1848, §3.5) and that its posture already reached any session (§3.2, P20, P21), moved activity into its own `view=link` part bounded per sensor kind and reran §4.2's size estimates with every field, and added the privacy findings P17 and P22 to P24 (§11).
- **How a round worked.** Each round was an independent fact-check of the document against the checkout. Every finding was checked against the tree before anything changed, and SDK facts against the installed cores and espefuse's tables (Appendix A). A proposed fix was checked the same way, and several were narrowed or corrected when the code or a host build disagreed.
- **What kept being wrong.** A PR said to hide a fact that another answer at the same tier still gave: the uptime, the Wi-Fi signal, the crash and safe mode each survived a move that was said to hide them, which is why §3.2 and §7.2 list what a tier still reads and why a held line counts only what its tier cannot read anywhere (§3.4). "Off the LAN" read as "off the air": the opera and pairing frames carry the id, the fingerprint and the keys whatever the HTTP routes do (§7.2 list 1, §14). A number copied where it should be measured: the flash headroom was first PlatformIO's count, not the `.bin` the guards read (§1.2). Line-number citations, which every merge of main moved, so the document now cites a function or a quoted phrase. And work described as merged, numbered or gated before it was.
- **Prototypes.** PR 8's mesh-frame changes and its lowering of a stored privacy ceiling were prototyped and host-tested in private clones of the branch, at `e4d07edb` and at the merge of `3453dd26`, in the last days of October 2026 (those two commits are dated 2026-10-26 and 2026-10-27), and not on `0a06c14b`. §12 PR 8 gives the cases and what each failed on.
